It sits outside the grand total the way route (a)'s closing refinement does rather than being an item the sums reach, and stating that is what stops the `frozen` and `qualified` flags reading as though they close themselves.

The other four off-clock rows are the radio reference state machines (inventory rows 19–22), whose authoring rides the release program: they arrive with the roster the first release carries whole (R-18-004), and under R-12-043e each begins as a version-pin of the machine-checked symbolic analysis of its protocol, a transcription into the one prover, and a formal-to-formal review rather than an original reading of the prose standard. Deciding which edition of each Tamarin or ProVerif artifact serves as the row's upstream is the first task of each row's authoring and the cheapest part of it; the RRC row additionally names which procedures fall outside the published analyses and take the R-05-050 hand-transcription posture, so the thin lane is scoped before it is walked.

### Checklist conventions

* Checked items include concise completion evidence.
* **Exit evidence records what an item's gate measured, and two standing figures are stated only where they move**: from M0.6f the profile sweep refuses 199 of 199, and the legacy rig's agreeing prefix is 67 records on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members.
* **A proofs-lane item reports its own file's constant count and not the proof gate's global total.** The global figure is derived over the whole of [proofs/](../proofs/), so any lane that adds a `.v` moves it, which makes it the one exit-evidence figure a sibling landing invalidates rather than a property of the item that quoted it. The landed Gallina items already split on this without saying so, the earlier ones quoting the global and the later ones their own file's share; the later reading is the one this convention adopts.
* **Every item carries one estimate cell, and only what is inside it is authored.** An open item reads `· 6 h, range 4–8 · 0.7%`, a completed one `· 1.9 h actual · 0.2%`; the range and the actual are the estimates, in attended agent-session hours, and are re-priced when split. The rest is arithmetic: the midpoint is the mean of the range ends, and the percentage is that midpoint's share of the grand total. An item whose children carry the estimates carries none of its own.
* **Every subtotal, the grand total, its range, and the progress figures are sums over those cells**, recomputed by `tools/check.py` (`--fix` rewrites them), so a re-priced item moves everything resting on it in the same edit.
* **Every open item carries an authority class beside its estimate**, `I` where the authority it realizes is inside this repository and `X` where it is outside. The classes are priced differently because they have measured differently: over the thirty completed items carrying both an estimate and an actual, the eighteen whose authority is inside this repository ran at 0.66, and the twelve whose authority is outside it split, the ten that read, pin, install or measure an external thing running at 0.60 and the two that authored against an external authority at 1.68. **The 1.68 every open class-X cell is priced against is that second pool and not the class**, which is the whole of what makes n = 2 the weakness the risks table states, and the fit's pools are named here because they are not recoverable from the document: a completed item carries its actual instead of its class, the class having done its work at the estimate, so no rule computes any of the three ratios or any of the three counts.
* **A completed item marked `agent-parallel` ran under fan-out rather than at one attention, and its actual is measured on a different clock.** Its figure is the summed agent-session wall-clock of the authoring, verification and repair passes the item took, which is not the attended figure the completed items before it were priced in. **The two series are not pooled**, and the ratio above is over the attended one alone. What the label marks is the execution mode and never the quality bar: an agent-parallel item clears the same gates, declares the same tier, and is read the same way. **The series is six items now** (M3.2, M4.2b, M4.3, M5.1, M5.2, M6.2a) and **no item owns the re-fit across both modes**: it was read onto S7, S7 landed at four reconnaissance passes none of which was a calibration pass, and nothing has taken it since, so the ratio the whole convention rests on is owned by nobody.
* **The width test is set and it is a span floor: a class-X range's upper end is at least twice its lower end.** That is the *roughly a factor of two* this document already claims of every range, made a gate for the one class whose outturn says it is not decoration, and `tools/check.py` holds it. Class I carries no floor, having run under estimate.
  **The obvious threshold was tried first and is arithmetically unavailable, which is why this one is a span rather than a multiple of the midpoint.** *The calibrated value lies inside the range*, upper ≥ 1.68 × midpoint, is the test the ratio actually motivates; but a midpoint is the mean of the range ends, so that test is equivalent to lower ≤ 0.32 × midpoint and forces every class-X range to a span above five. Applied to the cells as they stand it would either drive the lower ends to a third of their midpoints or, holding the lower ends, re-price the midpoints upward by more than a factor of two, which is exactly the re-price the ratio is stated **not** to be. The two rules collide, and the span floor is what survives the collision: it is tied to the convention this document already states, it discriminates the class the measurement discriminates, and it leaves the calibrated total where it belongs, stated separately rather than folded in.
  **What the floor does not do is make the calibration a gate.** A cell can clear the span and still have a midpoint the ratio says is low, and nothing here reports that; the calibrated total is where it shows, and the 1.68 the whole convention rests on is still fitted over two completed items and is owed a re-fit no item carries.
* **`Wasm-parallel`** means the item's deliverable is Gallina logic needing no toolchain, no image and no machine model (§0), so its gate is the register rather than M1 and it may run in any lane from today. **The label names a validator the entry point does not carry, and the landed items say which one they used instead.** M1.5 closed the CertiCoq→Wasm loop on `demo.v` alone in a switch of its own, [tools/wasm-oracle/](../tools/wasm-oracle/) carries that file and [ipc_oracle.v](../tools/wasm-oracle/ipc_oracle.v) beside it plus a Dockerfile, a runner and a hand-run recipe, and no `run.py` command reaches any of it. Nine landings validated at the prover level alone, through `run.py proofs` and `run.py seed coq`: M3.2, M3.3, M3.6a, M4.2a, M4.2b, M5.1, M5.2, M6.1a and M6.2a. **Staging a repository Gallina source into the Wasm oracle is priced once and it is M4.3's**, which staged `ipc_oracle.v` at 4 h actual and is the only cell that has met the label's verb at the execution level; every other cell carrying the label leaves it uncosted.
* **Landing is two-tier.** Tier A is a full attended read: any register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit; any moved co-read pair; any new normative claim; every decision item. Tier B is a spot read of the item's findings, admitted on four mechanical conditions: checker green with every mutant killed, no Tier-A edit, the co-read ledger unmoved, and a rule holding whatever fact the item created.
* **A landing declares its tier, and a Tier-B landing names what holds it.** A landed item carries one `Landed: Tier A` or `Landed: Tier B under` line among its notes, the second naming in bold every rule that holds a fact the landing created. **A bolded rule id is a holder citation and a plain one is not**: a rule relied on is written in bold beside what it holds, which is the form the landed items that created one already use, and a rule merely discussed stays plain, so the document tells the two apart instead of leaving it to the reader. A holder need not be new, an existing rule whose subject a landing extends holding the new member and being named the same way. K-84 holds every holder citation to naming a rule [the registry](../tools/check-rules.md) carries and every Tier-B declaration to naming one, and it reads a declaration where it is made rather than demanding one where it is absent, a tier being a property of the reading a landing was given and not something a later hand assigns to it. What no rule decides is whether the named rule holds *that* fact, which is the residue every conferral here declares.
* **An item's acceptance predicate is authored and landed before its implementation lane opens.** M0.11 and M1.8a are the precedent and M1.8a states the reason: an instrument arriving with its own acceptance criteria has graded its own homework. In a multi-lane operation this is what lets a lane self-check, and it is the precondition for a Tier-B landing. **Five open items owe one and the convention names them here rather than leaving a lane to discover it.** M1.7, M5.3, M6.3b and M6.5b carry an estimate cell and no bullet at all, so they carry no predicate of any kind; M4.4 carries two bullets and neither is one, nothing saying what measurement closes *bring up one isolated C instance per emulated core*. Three of the five, M1.7, M4.4 and M5.3, are on the critical chain, which is where the convention's precondition is worth the most and where it is unmet. Where a predicate is not yet writable the cell says so, as M3.6b does, rather than staying silent.
* **Validation is generated, not authored, wherever an oracle exists.** The model-as-oracle technique is proven in this tree at 21,546 vectors (M2.1) and 658,659 vectors with twelve seeded defects killed (R1a). A hand-written corpus member where a generator would serve is a finding.
* **A fact has one owner and every other statement of it is generated.** Where an artifact restates a fact the model, the register or a contract owns, it is emitted from that owner and regenerated in the checking act. A hand-written restatement held equal by a rule is the older discipline and is a finding under this one: the rule can only tell you the two disagree, after they already do.
* `Parallel` means the item can proceed in a separate worktree and build directory.
* **`Parallel` is about the build lane and not about attention.** A linked worktree derives its own lane under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, iterate tree and SMT cache ([I7](#build-loop-instruments)), so two items that both write `model/` share no build tree, no log, and no emulator, and a build holds its own lane rather than the toolchain: a second `run.py model build` over a live one in the same lane is refused by name, while a sibling lane's runs beside it. **Lanes isolate files and build trees, not CPU.** The machine stays one thing, twelve cores under one WSL VM, so concurrent model work is priced in wall time rather than in correctness, and what overlaps a running build for nothing is the work that opens no lane at all: a provenance decision, a document act, or a checker rule.
* Later milestones remain deliverable-level until entry.

### Current summary

**In one paragraph.** Take the owed register acts and the two licence reads first, because they are the only work that cannot be delegated and they gate the most. Re-base the compiler item on stock CompCert, where the delta is a back end rather than a memory model. Let the Gallina front run on the Wasm oracle it was designed for, and let the assembler this repository already built be the assembler. Join the RVFI-DII ecosystem, build the three generators, and stop hand-writing what a model can emit. Generate the derived artifacts rather than policing their transcriptions, which retires much of the tool surface and the rules bolted on to hold those transcriptions equal. Split the gate that welded two independent tracks, strike the emulator fork whose two jobs other items now do, and make the lane portable so the host is a per-run choice rather than a migration. **The stated bill barely moves. What halves is the open work before a booting system, and what falls by nearly two thirds is the serial chain through it**, because the plan's problem was never the cost: it was that the schedule was serial where it need not be, hand-validated where it could generate, and pointed at start-froms that do not carry what they were priced to carry.

* Completed: S1, S2, S3, S4, S7, S8, S9, S10a, S11, S12, S13a, S13b, S14, S15, S16, S17, M0.1–M0.5, M0.6a, M0.6b, M0.6c (c1–c4), M0.6d, M0.6e (e1–e5), M0.6f, M0.6g, M0.6h, M0.7, M0.8a, M0.8b, M0.8d, M0.9, M0.10, M0.11, M0.12, M0.13, M0.14, M0.15, M0.16, M0.17, M0.18, M0.19, M1.1, M1.1a, M1.1b, M1.5, M1.6, M1.8a, M3.1, M3.2, M3.3, M3.4a, M3.6a, M4.1, M4.1a, M4.1b, M4.2a, M4.2b, M4.3, M5.1, M5.2, M6.0a, M6.0b, M6.1a, M6.2a, M6.4, R1a, and the build-loop instruments I0, I2, I3, I4, I5, I6, I7, I8, I9, I10, I11, I12 and the initial check/emit/FAST tooling. Five instrument clauses are **declines carrying a measurement**, the act of measuring having removed the premise: I2, I6 and I8 whole, I4's second clause, and I5's prefix-map clause. **S14 is a sixth of the same kind and the first outside the instruments**, S9 having retired the first of the two grounds I8 declined push CI on and the measurement then finding the second standing.
* **The serial constraint was lane 1 and it is discharged.** S1's owed register acts were the author's own, they gated the whole Gallina front and both RTL lanes, and nothing else could take them; thirteen of the fourteen are taken and the fourteenth is conditioned on a measurement rather than owed to an author. What is left in lane 1 is the profile amendments a later act creates, which arrive one at a time rather than as a block. **So the opening is no longer spent almost entirely there**, and the Gallina lanes open on the register as it now stands rather than waiting on it.
* **The Gallina front is not blocked and was sequenced as though it were.** §0's own discipline is that each component runs host-side on a stock Wasm engine *before it is lowered at all*, needing no toolchain, no image and no machine model, and M1.5 is landed and green. **Nine of the ten items that carried the label have landed under it** and M3.3 landed against the same discipline: M3.2, M3.4a, M4.2b, M4.3, M5.1, M5.2, M6.1a, M6.2a and M6.4 are complete, eight of them agent-parallel, and M6.4's own gate on M6.0b was met by that item landing first. What still carries the label is M6.3a of that ten, class X at M4.1b's read, beside M3.4c, which is not of it: M3.4a's landing created that cell out of its own residue, and it is class X at the crypto upstreams M3.4a was class X at. Only the lowering-and-integration items genuinely want the backend: M1.7, M3.5, M4.4, M5.3, M6.5a and M7.1.
* M0's serial path: **closed**. The C-class freeze is taken: M0.6 closed the decode surface, M0.14 and M0.16 put both latency classes and their maintenance in the model without adding an instruction, M0.8a fixed the per-class parameterization at the C class, M0.9 put the timing annotations over it, and M0.10 generated the emulator and gave it the revision every downstream artifact records. What is left in M0 is M0.8c. M0.8c is not a datapath awaiting its turn: its scratchpad landed, and its instruction surface is a second-act item conditioned on R-15-116's margin (R-15-014a (ix)), so what closes it is a profile act rather than a model one and it gates no milestone here. The next serial work is M1's toolchain spine, and it is ungated. **M1.9 heads it rather than M1.2**, its own cell running it ahead of the backend and its memory-plan artifact landed with one gate owed. Behind it the pin builds whole on this toolchain through to a linked `ccomp`, which is what M1.1b weighed; the pretty-printer census that build took no longer locates M1.2's cost, that item having struck the `-dcapasm` work outright and moved its start-from to stock CompCert's RISC-V backend, against which the delta is unmeasured.
* Available parallel work, in nine lanes rather than three, and the list is the open residue rather than the roster: **lane 1** the author's own, whose named content is discharged and which now carries the profile amendments a later act creates, one at a time; **lane 2** the spine, M1.9 then M1.2 then M1.7; **lanes 3–5** the Gallina front's residue, which M6.0b and M6.4 landing has reduced to M3.4c and M6.3a, M3.4c being what M3.4a's own landing left; **lanes 6–7** the RTL, R1b and R1c's map and devices; **lane 8** the instruments, whose open content is S10b alone; and **lane 9** the rules, because if each lane writes its own rule every lane edits `counts.py` and a conflict per landing is guaranteed, where one lane fed by items and by S8's register removes the collision and concentrates the one skill that most benefits from repetition. Four gating facts sit behind that list, and two shared surfaces sit under it.
  **Four documents every lane writes, and only two of them have an item.** `counts.py` is S10a's and is sharded; the per-item notes are S10b's and are not. The other two are held by no item at all: [the findings register](findings-register.md) takes an entry per counted finding from every landing, with K-82 holding it total in both directions and two figures `--fix` recomputes, so two lanes landing at once conflict on the text and on the arithmetic alike; and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) takes a row and a gitlink from M3.4a, M6.0b, M1.4 and R1c-ii against one table K-81 holds total against the index. The discipline lane 9 uses for K-numbers is what both want, a block of ids pre-assigned per lane. A fifth surface is narrower and worth naming: R1c-i is filed in an RTL lane and writes only [the composition](../model/config/verifiedos.json), the attested devicetree and [the validator](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail), which are the three files M0.8c also writes.
  **M3 is entered ahead of M1 and M2 by an ordering call rather than by accident**: M3.1 needed nothing from either, reading the frozen profile and writing devices, and the build order put it behind them only because later items of its milestone need a lowered image. M3.2 and M3.3 gate on M3.1, and M3.2's seal, unseal and quote halves on M3.4's crypto and M3.5's chain.
  **R1 gates on the M0 freeze rather than on M1**, R-18-003b putting the absence contract among the day-one deliverables, so the RTL track runs beside the toolchain spine and closes at its own gate rather than rendezvousing with the software one. R1a is taken; S3 initialized five of the imported cores R1b and R1c reach through, and `opentitan` stays uninitialized, which is what the elaboration refuses by name on.
  **M1.8's second gate is M1.4′ rather than M1.2**: [the measurement contract](freeze-measurement-contract.md) joins three inputs into the analyzer's one table, and two of them, the link map and the encoded image, are the composer's rather than the backend's. The analyzer needed neither milestone, the contract fixing every enumeration and every declared value, so what M1.8b waits on is a transport against a declared schema, and it waits behind the M8a gate with the freeze it serves.
  **Ungated is not unblocked, and M4.2 is where the two part**: no fork holds it, and its second half is held by the register acts M4.2b enumerates, each an obligation with no mechanism under it, which is a gap a milestone cannot close and which S1 is the act that closes. I1 was open the same way, its two standing clauses being settings on the human's box rather than in this tree, which is why it is struck rather than scheduled; S9 no longer closes them, the lane staying on WSL by choice, so they stand as an accepted cost rather than as an item.
* Total estimate: 1,087.5 h midpoint, class I 213.5 h and class X 497.5 h over the open items.
* Calibrated against completed-item outturn (class I 0.66, class X 1.68): approximately 1,353.2 h.
* Progress by estimate: 376.5 of 1,087.5 h complete (34.6%); 711 h remaining (65.4%).
* M8a gate: 161 h of open work falls at or before it, of which 93 h is class X.
* M8b gate: a 90.5 h chain of open work running beside the software one, R1b through M8b.
* Critical chain through M8a: **its head is discharged**, S1 and every provenance decision being landed, and M1.9 with them, its memory plan closed on a population that decided every site, so what remains of it runs M1.2, M1.7, M3.5, M4.4, M5.3, M6.5a, M7.1 and M8a; every Gallina item beside it is `Wasm-parallel` and adds none. Over those items the chain sums to 77–168 h at a 122.5 h midpoint, M1.2 entering as its six children because it carries no estimate cell of its own. **That figure is a hand-taken sum and no rule recomputes it**, unlike the two gate figures above, so it goes stale at the next re-price of anything on the chain and is read as a serial-path ceiling rather than as arithmetic the tool holds.

### Sequencing

The order the lanes open in, which is not the order the sections are written in. What governs it is that an hour spent on a decision or an instrument is worth more the earlier it is spent. **The opening premise is spent**: lane 1 is no longer the only lane open, its named content having been discharged and the fan-out having already run six agent-parallel landings, so the phases below are read as an order rather than as a calendar with the first week still ahead of it.

| Phase | Work | Why then |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Opening | S10b | The measurement that decided whether the struck milestone stays struck is taken (S4), the provisioner that retires the first of S14's two grounds is landed (S9), and S14 is declined on the second, so what is left in the opening is S10b, which the landings since have made more expensive rather than less, each of them writing another note into the file it moves |
| Behind them | S10a's sibling acts | Every instrument the lanes depend on, S9, S13a, S13b and S15 being landed and S14 declined on a measurement. **The instruments are front-loaded**: they pay back on every item after them and on nothing before them |
| Run | The Gallina lanes' residue: M3.4c and M6.3a | Gated on nothing but the register as it now stands. Nine items landed in these lanes ahead of the rest, so what the row schedules is the residue and not the set, and what S13a and S13b validate them at is the prover level: M3.2 measured that neither generated instrument reaches a statement artifact of this kind at all |
| With them | The spine, M1.9 → M1.2; the RTL lanes, R1b, whose start-from S7 settled, and R1c | Counterexample-driven under S11 from the first elaboration |
| Then | M1.7 → M3.5 → M4.4 → M5.3 → M6.5a → M7.1 → **M8a** | The critical chain the summary states, and lane 1's to release |
| Beside it | R2 → R3 → **M8b** | The RTL chain the summary computes, decoupled from the software one by the gate split |

### Risks to the sequencing

What would falsify the order above, and what each risk is answered by. Nothing here is a hedge: every row names either the item that measures it or the property that bounds what it costs. **Where the item has run, the row states its answer rather than the question**, including where the answer came back adverse or found the row's own premise false, which is the only way a risk table stops being a list of things once worried about.

| Risk | Severity | What answers it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The authority-class calibration rests on two completed items in the class that matters.** | High for the conclusion, low for the actions | Every act it motivates is independently justified. The convention already treats the ratio as a prior on range width rather than a re-price of midpoints, and the calibrated total is stated separately rather than folded in. **What is not answered is who turns the prior into a measurement**: the re-fit was read onto S7, S7 landed at four reconnaissance passes and took none of it, and no item has it now. |
| **M1.1b forfeits SECOMP's integration path for robust preservation.** | High: that theorem is the §5 end-state | The arms and their forfeits are recorded at the decision. SECOMP's recomposition, blame and back-translation are stated over the compartment layer rather than over `cheririscV/`, so the pin stays for the front and middle end. Refusing the arm costs M1.2's re-price and no structure. |
| **M4.1b's read foreclosed the CHERI-seL4 start-from.** | Taken: M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a are re-priced upward | Answered rather than pending. S7 recorded the read, 733 of 1,045 tracked files carrying a GPL-2.0-only tag with the CHERI work contributed under it, in the 6 h its four passes took together; M4.1b took the *author instead* arm at 0.4 h; and all three re-prices the severity column forecasts are taken, at M4.4, M6.3a and M6.5a. What is left exposed is the authoring itself, not an unread term. |
| **The M2 strike is wrong**, the multikernel wanting real SMP or the reference emulator being too slow. | High, for the whole of what was struck | Both rulings are taken: the multi-hart call at M4.4, and the throughput measurement at S4, which reads the emulator sufficient through M6 outright and past it against a boot budget M7.1 measures. Both upstreams stay gitlinks, so reinstating costs the pin. |
| **The Gallina front is authored against register acts that later move.** | Medium | Why S1 runs first. M4.2a is the precedent and the discipline: it authored what the register fixed and refused what it did not. |
| **Tier B admits a defect a full read would have caught.** | Medium | Tier B admits nothing without a rule holding whatever fact the item created, so the admissible defect class shrinks with every landing, and three of its four conditions are already gates that run: `run.py` decides them on the machine the landing is taken on, and K-84 holds the fourth as a declaration rather than as a promise. What an unattended runner would add is a second machine and a trigger, which S14 declined on a measurement. |
| **The model's own documentation bundle is not rich enough to supply what the four parsers read.** | Answered, and the answer is partial | S15 read it and both of the row's premises were false: the build had never produced the bundle on this host, and there were four live Sail parsers rather than five, `banks.py` having gone to the quarantine at S16. The bundle carries the definitions and the four parsers take them from it. **The retirement half does not follow and none of the seven rules retires**: each holds a figure sitting in hand-authored prose or in authored SystemVerilog, which a view of the Sail model is not a generator for, and that is a separate item at a separate estimate no cell carries. |
| **Generation makes a wrong fact consistent everywhere**, where a transcription would have shown a visible disagreement. | Medium | The tree's own evidence runs the other way. K-79's sites were held by nothing at all until the rule was written, and K-54's first run found a live disagreement in a document nobody had noticed. Consistency was never a property the transcriptions had; it was the property the rules were bolted on to create. |
| **The generator dividend does not materialize** across the Gallina front. | Measured, and adverse | The row named its own falsification condition and the condition was met: at M3.2, the first Gallina item to land with S13a and S13b in place, the QuickChick harness killed nothing and the enumerative vector grid killed nothing, all 244 kills being the prover's and the whole mutation score being the assumption gate refusing to compile. So the dividend is unrealized for a statement artifact, and what would change it is the widening M3.2 names and M4.3 has since performed once for the kernel surface: a `Require`, generator instances over the file's own types, and computable shadows of its deciders. It stays a small share of the reduction and is named per cell rather than asserted over the total. |
| **M6.2's offline-admission reading is wrong.** | Medium to high, and now only over M6.2b | S1 took the ruling: measured boot covers a statically composed image, which is what splits M6.2. The split is executed, M6.2a having landed the composition-time admission path at 12.4 h, so the residual risk stands over M6.2b alone, the on-device CIC kernel deferred behind the M8a gate, and a wrong reading costs that deferral rather than the work already done. |
| **The lanes exceed what one author can adjudicate**, Tier A being irreducible. | High for the calendar | Lane 1's queue is discharged, so the ceiling has moved off its serial content and onto the Tier-A read every landing still wants, which no instrument removes. The fan-out has run against that ceiling six times and the landings held. If the lanes are too many, the binding figure is the critical chain rather than the gate total, and the chain wants three lanes rather than nine. |
| **Rupicola's target does not exist**, which is the harder half of what this row covers. | Answered, and the answer forks the route | Both halves are now measured at the artifact rather than read from the paper, and both readings moved. The **subset** blockers are not inductive datatypes and general recursion, which are at zero sites and three nullary enumerations respectively, but higher-order code, at 73 of M6.1a's 100 definitional commands. The **target** is a fork rather than an absence: the route stands up whole and has two exits, and neither carries both properties this plan claims for it. The verified exit emits 39 words of RV32I with no capability instruction, which is the plain-RISC-V target R-18-002 forbids by name; the reaching exit runs through a pretty-printer carrying no theorem, so its admissibility rides M1.2 completing the backend. The CompCert-C/VST route stays the default. |
| **The Sail-to-µSail translator carries no licence at all**, which is a harder exposure than the hand translation the answer falls back on. | Medium | S12 read it at its own files: no `LICENSE`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING` or `COPYRIGHT`, no forge-detected licence, and no `license:` field in either opam package, so it is pinned and read and may not be copied, vendored or extracted from. The call is taken: ask the licensor, and author the translation by hand if no terms arrive before R-13-016 is scheduled. Nothing before the co-simulation gate depends on it, and a register gap rides it: R-13-016 books the seam's faithfulness obligation and no terms obligation beside it. |
| **Quarantine rots the instruments S16 sets aside.** | Low to medium | They move whole, with their selftest cases, and K-83 holds that nothing outside the quarantine imports or launches one of the sources it carries. **The two contract documents did not move**: they stay in `docs/`, what was quarantined being the machinery and never the obligation, with K-70 and K-55 still reading both from the landing loop. |

### Considered and rejected

Recorded so they are not re-proposed, which is the same discipline [the alternatives register](architectural-alternatives.md) keeps for the design.

- **Lowering as a post-pass over stock `ccomp -S` output.** Wrong on this machine's own semantics: an integer write yields an **untagged** capability (M0.6e e3, R-05-136), so ordinary RV64 pointer arithmetic clears the tag on every address it touches. Rewriting each such site into a capability increment needs type information CompCert has erased by `Asm`, and CompCert deliberately conflates pointers and integers at 64 bits. The idea's residue is what M1.1b rests on: the delta is concentrated in `Asmgen` and the calling convention.
- **Cutting the rules, the mutation selftest or the co-read ledger to reduce review cost.** Backwards. That machinery is exactly what makes a Tier-B landing admissible, so shrinking it moves work back into the one lane that cannot be widened.
- **Adopting CHERIoT-Ibex for the main core.** Already correctly excluded, its distinct compressed encoding being what R-15-005's single-encoding requirement refuses.
- **Parallelizing the build further.** I5, I6 and I10 measured it: clang took the generated unit's compile down by half and the residual is Sail's own single-threaded emission, which I6 showed a compiler mode cannot reach. CPU is not the constraint. Hardware still helps, through S9's finding class rather than through throughput.
- **Deleting `model/dependencies/`.** Checked at S3's scoping; every member has a live consumer, softfloat serving the surviving vector floating point and `asio` being S11's socket layer.
- **Raising the attended-hours budget.** Not a proposal at all. It is the one input this document cannot supply.

### S · Serial-path decisions and program instruments

The work that is undelegatable, the work that unblocks a lane, and the instruments the lanes run on. It sits ahead of the milestone track rather than inside one, because a milestone's items are the deliverable and these are the conditions under which more than one of them can be worked at once. Two rules sort it. **A decision is not an edit**: an item here that names an arm and its forfeits is closed by taking the arm, and nothing downstream is priced against it until it is. And **an instrument is authored before the lane that depends on it opens**, which is the acceptance-predicate convention above read forward rather than per item.

* [x] **S1 · Discharge the owed register acts** · 9 h actual · 0.8%
  * **Thirteen of the fourteen are taken and the fourteenth is not owed yet.** Each was a register or profile act the plan could not take for itself, and each blocked work that was otherwise ungated. Six kernel-semantics acts land as entries: R-07-031b closes the invocation enumeration at five and R-07-027a the object inventory at three classes plus two tables, with no reply object surviving (f1, f7); R-07-029a fixes the blocking discipline as rendezvous-or-refusal, collected by the new seam R-17-030x (f2); R-07-037c and R-07-037d answer the rotation in opposite directions, swapping the pending bits and admitting the in-domain residue (f3, f4); and R-15-220a puts the restore and the swap in a context term beside R-15-220's three platform terms rather than amending it to four (f6). Four more land where they belong: R-15-007a states `setCapBounds`'s containment domain (M2.1, R1a), R-09-034 carves the Debug Module out of the permanent test-exit closure (M3.1), M6.0b gives the IDL profile an item of its own (M6.4), and the K-64 hole is answered by a struck registry row (f8). The SoC definition is split at its own seam, the address map to R-15-002b's attested devicetree and the peripherals to route (c) (R1c). And the two rulings with priced consequences are taken: M4.4 is single-instance bring-up, and measured boot covers a statically composed image, which splits M6.2.
  * **What stays open is the matrix instruction surface, and it is not this item's to close.** R-15-014a (ix) admits it against R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1, and the re-pin target it would otherwise be a version pin of has not ratified: `Zvvm` stands at v0.9.0 ARC review candidate, its own plan's v1.0 date of 2026-08-27 already missed. So the act is conditioned on a measurement rather than owed to an author, which is a different disposition from the thirteen above and is M0.8c's.
  * **What the discharge cost that the estimate did not carry**: six new entries each needed the prose side they are extracted from, which is the co-read discipline read forward rather than a surprise, and sixteen pairs were re-read and blessed. Three prose sides carried the reading the act narrows and were repaired with it rather than left to disagree: R-11-023's *widths and offsets*, R-15-247h's die-wide reset hold, and R-09-034's unqualified permanent closure.
  * Landed: Tier A.
* [x] **S2 · Adopt the two-tier landing rule and hold it by rule** · 2.6 h actual · 0.2%
  * **A landing declares its tier, and a Tier-B landing names what holds it.** Three of the four conditions Tier B is admitted on are gates that already run; the fourth is a claim about the item and had nothing under it, so a Tier-B landing was admissible on a promise. It becomes a declaration, and **K-84** in the [meta](../tools/vos/checks/meta.py) group holds it: every bolded rule id in the corpus names a rule [the registry](../tools/check-rules.md) carries, and every Tier-B declaration names one.
  * **The form is the one the landed items already write**, a rule id in bold beside what it holds, where a rule merely discussed stays plain. That is what makes the reading live at thirteen citations rather than beginning empty, and it is why the gap at K-64 stays with the register act that owns the numbering instead of being reported against the document that reports it.
  * **What the arms not taken forfeit.** Holding every figure a completed item states was the stronger arm and is declined on the tree's own evidence: the completed items state gate measurements, `664/664 tests pass` and a 1,442,433-byte cache among them, which no rule should hold, so the arm needs the measurement-against-created-fact distinction it was meant to supply. It also collides with the claim rows, which already own every derived figure, giving one fact two owners. Inferring the tier from the item's own diff was the third arm: the checker's corpus is the git index at one revision, it has no diff, and nothing binds an item to a commit, which is S8's work. What the taken arm forfeits is a tier nobody can mis-declare.
  * **What it cannot decide, stated rather than closed.** Whether the named rule holds *that* fact is a reading, which is the residue every conferral here declares. And a declaration is read where it is made and never demanded where it is absent, forty-four landings predating the tiers and none assignable retroactively, so that half is forward-looking and the first declaration is this item's own. A holder written plain rather than bold is invisible to it, and the loss shows as a falling citation count floored at zero.
  * Net change: 143 lines added and 2 removed across four files.
  * Exit evidence: the checker green on all **82 rules** with all **92 mutants** killed and the registry covered both ways; `gate.py --fix` rewriting nothing, so no derived figure moved; the co-read ledger unmoved at 1,377 pairs; the tools' own suite green and both type checkers clean at their pins; and the six figures this item rests on each re-verified rather than restated, the proof gate at 104 axiom-free constants, the differential corpus at 26 of 26 with 245 checks, and the two type checkers at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered.
  * Landed: Tier A.
* [x] **S3 · Delete the dormant backends and inert vendored surface; initialize the gitlinks** · 2.1 h actual · 0.2%
  * The Lean target and its handwritten support are the row that mattered: a dormant target's support goes stale where no typechecker can see it, which c2 found and c4 found again, and deleting the target removes the class permanently. Rocq and SMT stay. With it went `handwritten_support/hgen/` M0.7 booked rather than deleted, `model/.github/` I8 measured as never having run, `model/os-boot/` and `model/doc/`, and `model/test/.gitignore`.
  * **A deletion is not an edit**, which is why it does not meet I4's and I8's refusal of permanent vendored divergence: it leaves nothing to reconcile at the next pin, M0.18's own ground for deleting `encoding.h` rather than curating it.
  * **The Lem, Isabelle and HOL4 targets go with Lean rather than beside it**, which is the one place the item's own list was wrong: `riscv_extras.lem`, `Holmakefile.in` and `ROOT.in` were live inputs to those three blocks of [model/model/CMakeLists.txt](../model/model/CMakeLists.txt), so *unreferenced* was true of `riscv_extras_fdext.lem` alone. Rocq keeps `riscv_extras.v` and the `build_rocq_common` target [rocq-sail-riscv.opam.in](../model/rocq-sail-riscv.opam.in) builds, and that file drops `sail_lean_backend` and `sail_lem_backend` with the two it no longer needs.
  * **`model/test/` beyond `unit_tests/` is refused, and the refusal is measured.** That tree's `ENABLE_RISCV_TESTS` block is the only thing that downloads the suite [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) configures with `-DENABLE_RISCV_TESTS=TRUE` and that `sweep`, `trace-diff` and a new lane's seeding then glob out of `test/*/riscv-tests`, so deleting it takes the 199-program profile sweep with it. What went is `test/.gitignore`, whose one entry named a JUnit file only `ci.yml` ever wrote. `model/dependencies/` is refused on the item's own ground, verified per member: CLI11 in `cli_options.cpp`, elfio in `elf_loader.cpp`, jsoncons in `config_utils.cpp`, softfloat in `riscv_softfloat.cpp` and the surviving vector floating point, and asio in the gdbserver and in S11's socket layer.
  * **One reference out of the deleted tree runs at configure rather than at build**, which is the reason a deletion still needs the reference sweep: [model/cmake/packaging.cmake](../model/cmake/packaging.cmake) gzipped `doc/ChangeLog.md` through `file(ARCHIVE_CREATE)`, which cmake evaluates while configuring, so `model/doc/` could not go without that rule going in the same act. The failure would have been every lane's `configure` rather than one dormant target.

* [x] **S4 · Measure Sail emulator throughput against the M2 gate** · 3.5 h actual · 0.3%
  * **The gate demanded a measurement and stated nothing to measure against, so the criterion is authored here and said to be authored.** M2's entry condition asks whether Sail is *sufficient through M6* and its trigger sentence asks whether S4 reads it *insufficient through M6*, and no register entry, no profile line and no contract states a kIPS floor or a boot-time ceiling for the development vehicle. R-18-004c is the nearest thing and it points the other way, its acceptance putting the emulator outside timed obligations by deciding completeness and footprint and nothing timed. No throughput figure for any emulator existed in this tree before this item; the ones below are the first.
  * **The criterion, in two conjuncts, stated before the figures are read against it.** *Through M6* the emulator is sufficient if no loop this plan runs is emulator-bound, which is decidable today because every such loop is already in the tree. *Past M6* the question is the composed boot, and the ceiling taken is upstream's own rather than a preference: `sail-riscv`'s README prices its Linux boot at about four minutes at 300 KIPS with tracing disabled, and its `os-boot/linux` target prices reaching userspace at order 2 × 10<sup>8</sup> instructions and about ten minutes. Those are the boot times this interpreter's own community already lives with, so 240 s untraced and 600 s traced are the ceilings, and at a measured rate they are an instruction budget rather than a rate floor, which is the form the question actually has: what is unknown is the image, not the machine.
  * **Nothing in the tree can carry the figure, so two programs were authored and neither is tracked.** The 199 `riscv-tests` are refused 199 of 199 on the frozen profile, every one addressing memory through an integer base register; the 26 differential-corpus members run 120 to 1,996 records and are startup-dominated; and the composed image does not exist until M7.1. So a twelve-instruction integer loop and a twelve-instruction loop with four capability memory operations in its body were written in the frozen dialect and assembled by [run.py model asm](../tools/vos/asm.py), retiring 24,000,017 and 24,000,020 instructions respectively to a clean HTIF exit. Neither joins [the differential corpus](differential-corpus.md), which is a membership K-50 and K-51 hold: a benchmark decides nothing about the model's answers and would be a corpus member that no check reads.
  * **The measurement, three runs an arm, on the emulator this repository's own tools build.** `sail_riscv_sim --show-times` reports initialization, execution, retired instructions and kIPS ([riscv_sim.cpp](../model/c_emulator/riscv_sim.cpp)); initialization reported **0 ms in all twelve runs** against executions of 60 to 335 seconds, so every figure below is execution and the startup that made a small program's rate meaningless is out of the measurement entirely. Untraced the integer loop runs **97, 107 and 95 kIPS** and the capability loop **71, 79 and 78**; under `--trace-commit` at 6,000,000 instructions the integer loop runs **76, 73 and 100** and the capability loop **68, 67 and 66**. Model revision `d359483-dirty` ([run.py model reference](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), Sail 0.20.2, upstream release 0.13.1, clang 21.1.8 at `RelWithDebInfo`, on a twelve-core Snapdragon X Elite X1E78100 under WSL. The spread is the host's: the tightest arm's three runs span 3% and the loosest 37%, and the ruling below is taken on the slowest run of each arm rather than on a median.
  * **The rate is a third of upstream's and neither reason is a defect.** Upstream's 300 KIPS is the plain `sail-riscv` C emulator; this one is capability-widened, so every fetch goes through PCC's bounds and permissions and every load and store through a capability check and the tag plane, and the repository builds it `RelWithDebInfo` at `-O2` rather than `Release` at `-O3`, the `--fast` profile differing from the canonical one only by dropping `-g`. So no build here is an `-O3` build, and the figure is the emulator the tools actually produce rather than the fastest one they could.
  * **The ruling: the strike stands, and the first conjunct decides it outright.** No loop before M7.1 is emulator-bound. The RVFI-DII rig is socket-bound at 1,022 instructions in 0.66 s and 500,000 generated words in 73.8 s (S11), which is 1.5 and 6.8 kIPS against the emulator's own floor of 66, so the rig waits on the socket by between ten and forty times; the whole differential corpus is 8,830 records, a seventh of a second of emulation against a process launch; and the profile sweep refuses 199 of 199 and emulates almost nothing. Reinstating M2.1 through M2.4 would spend 64.5 h of class-X work to accelerate a loop the emulator is not the bottleneck of.
  * **The second conjunct is a projection and this states it as one.** At the slowest untraced arm, 71 kIPS, the 240 s ceiling is a budget of **1.7 × 10<sup>7</sup> retired instructions** for the composed image's boot, about a twelfth of Linux's 2 × 10<sup>8</sup> to userspace; the traced ceiling at 66 kIPS is 3.96 × 10<sup>7</sup> and does not bind. Nothing in this tree sizes that boot, and the four things that dominate a Linux boot are the four the composed image does not do: no device probe over a general driver model, no dynamic relocation or linking, no initial ramdisk to unpack, and no page cache to warm. **M7.1 is what replaces this projection with a measurement**, and it is the one act that can reopen M2.
  * **What binds the traced arm is bytes rather than time.** Tracing costs about a fifth of the rate and not an order of magnitude, but the commit trace runs **57.6 bytes a retired instruction** on the integer loop and **67.0** on the capability one, 345,888,911 and 401,888,795 bytes per 6,000,000 instructions. A boot at the budget above therefore writes on the order of **1.1 GB** of trace per run, which is the cost a CI arm pays and the daily driver does not, and it is a storage figure rather than a throughput one.
  * Seven findings.
    * **The M2 gate carries no threshold at all.** Its entry condition and its trigger sentence both turn on *sufficient through M6* and neither states a number, and no artifact in the repository states one for it to mean. Closed at the gate by this item stating the criterion it rules against; the register half is the next finding.
    * **No register entry owns a throughput floor or a boot-time ceiling for the development vehicle.** Every `throughput` obligation in the register is on shipped hardware, and R-18-004c puts the emulator outside timed obligations outright, so the gap is a gap rather than an oversight. Reported and not closed: a register act reruns the review gate.
    * **No workload short of the composed boot can carry the figure, and none can before M7.1.** The refusal is total on `riscv-tests`, the corpus members are startup-dominated at 120 to 1,996 records, and the rig is socket-bound. Any later item wanting a throughput figure authors its own program or waits for M7.1.
    * **The commit trace's cost is storage rather than speed.** Twenty percent of the rate against 57.6 to 67.0 bytes a retired instruction, which is what makes the CI arm's constraint disk and the daily driver's constraint neither.
    * **A lane's emulator is stamped `-dirty` whatever the lane's state is.** [The configure line](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) exports `GIT_DIR` to the child so that `git describe` finds the lane's commit, which is I7's finding answered, but it exports no `GIT_WORK_TREE`, so git takes the child's working directory as the tree and reads every tracked file as deleted. The commit is right and the flag is noise that would mask a genuinely edited lane. Reported and not closed.
    * **Three commands the table declares host-capable cannot run on the host.** `config-keys`, `validate-config` and `asm` are in the `model` command's `host_ok` set, and `model`'s own `main` calls `env.load()` before dispatch, which refuses on `win32`, so the declaration is unreachable and an assembly on the host is a hop into the guest. Reported and not closed.
    * **The merged register file makes an ABI integer name an authority destroyer, and the failure surfaces millions of instructions late.** `s0` is `x8` and `c8` is where every program here parks the root data capability, so a loop counter named `s0` clears that authority and the program runs correctly to its exit store and then traps there. The corpus header warns about `c1` for the same reason and nothing warns about `c8`.
  * Exit evidence: the twelve runs above, taken against model revision `d359483-dirty`; build green; **ctest at 2 of 2**; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **96 properties**; the profile sweep unmoved at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with 245 checks over 8,830 records at manifest version 9; `model reference` green and `model devicetree` at 5,717 bytes; the proof gate closing **180 constants**; the checker green on all **84 rules** with every one of **94 mutants** killed, the repair path holding and the registry covered; both type checkers clean; and the co-read ledger unmoved at **1,384 pairs**, this item touching no register entry and no prose span.

  * **The posture call is portability rather than a move: WSL stays the daily driver, and a dedicated box or a hosted Action instantiates the same lane whenever one is run there.** What decides it is not the environment findings, which are an irritation rather than an argument, but that S14 is unbuildable without *something*: I8 declined push CI on two grounds and the first is that no stock image carries the opam switches this lane declares, the prepended z3, or the interpreter floor. A host that *is* this lane carries all three by construction, and so does any host a provisioner stands up, which is the weaker premise that buys the same thing without a migration.
  * **The deliverable is [tools/run.py provision](../tools/vos/cli/provision.py), the lane stated as an executable fact list.** Nineteen rows, one per switch, pin, checker, distribution package and layout invariant, each naming the loop that wants the fact, the artifact that owns it, a probe that reports what is actually there, and, where this tree states one, the command that would put it there. The default reports and changes nothing, `--apply` runs exactly the plan and re-probes, and `--only gate|toolchain` narrows and names the rows it did not decide about, which is S13b's rule for a narrowed run read one instrument over. It is declared `lane="guest"`, so a host invocation hops exactly as `model` and `rtl` do. Native rather than containerized is what makes it architecture-agnostic, and the measurement below is what makes that concrete rather than asserted.
  * **Every version, switch name and pin in the table is imported from the module that fixes it**, so the file is rows and not a second copy of the tree's pins, and the constants moved to their owners in the same act: `SAIL_SWITCH`, `SAIL_VERSION`, `SAIL_INSTALL`, `Z3_VERSION`, `Z3_PREFIX`, `Z3_DISTRIBUTION`, `ROCQ_VERSION`, `ROCQ_INSTALL`, `BUILD_ROOT`, `TYPECHECK_CACHE` and a public `build_root()` in [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py), with `ROCQ_SWITCH` now composed from its own pin, and `quickchick.py`'s install recipe made public. **A recipe is argv and the sentence a person pastes is composed from it** by `env.install_line`, so the refusal that prints a recipe and the tool that runs it cannot come to disagree; written the other way round they are two copies and only one is ever run.

That is one appended row in `_FLOOR_SITES` and one registry row, with no new mutant: the existing `py314` seed still applies and the new site is fail-closed by K-75's own reading, both a moved pattern and a wrong figure being findings there. What stands in for a mutant at the new site is a behavioural case rather than prose, [test_checks.py](../tools/tests/test_checks.py) building a sandbox whose provisioned floor disagrees with `ty.toml`'s and asserting K-75 names that site, with its agreeing twin beside it. **K-67's window does not widen**: the provisioner composes `ty==` and `ruff==` from `typecheck.py`'s own constants, and a pin composed from the constant that fixes it is not a restatement of it. Inventing a command for one of them would be the unowned derived fact the working rules refuse, and it is the same discipline that keeps the distribution-package rows to packages with a named consumer already in this tree. **The three routes are not three rows**, which the findings block below states as the measurement it is: two of them are rows, the third is a route nothing here probes, and two rows carry no command for reasons that are neither.

A flake, an image and a workflow each need the same fact list, so the provisioner is what each of them would have to contain. The difference between an empty plan because there is nothing to do and one because there is nothing this tool may do is carried by the verdict rather than by the plan.

* [x] **S11 · Join the TestRIG ecosystem over RVFI-DII** · 6.5 h actual · 0.6%
  * The rig is [tools/run.py testrig](../tools/vos/cli/testrig.py) over two new modules and one old one: [vos/rvfi.py](../tools/vos/rvfi.py) is the wire format and the projection from one of its packets onto the commit trace's records, [vos/vengine.py](../tools/vos/vengine.py) is the stream generator over the frozen dialect, the socket, four seeded defects and delta debugging, and adjudication stays [vos/trace.py](../tools/vos/trace.py)'s, so the rig and the corpus decide what a divergence is through one function. Four commands: `protocol` reads the wire off the codec on the host, `handshake`, `run` and `bridge` drive the emulator in the guest. [differential-corpus.md](differential-corpus.md) §9 is the contract, and §8 carries the four predicates CI holds.
  * **The protocol is read at its own source rather than guessed.** `CTSRD-CHERI/TestRIG` is pinned and read at its own licence file, which is BSD-2-Clause and permissive like every other row of the submodule table; the version-2 packet is **not** in that repository's own protocol document, which describes version 1 alone, and is read from the model's own [core/rvfi_dii_v2.sail](../model/model/core/rvfi_dii_v2.sail). **The socket needed nothing added**, which is what M0.12 preserved the plumbing for: the emulator negotiates version 2 on a port and retires an injected instruction, and no line of `model/` changed.
  * **This repository carries a dialect of the standard packet rather than an extension of it.** The M0.12 widening is *inside* the packet, the destination's tag being one bit of the Integer extension's padding and the access's tag one bit above the CHERI-widened masks. What does not fit is structural: a variable-length effect list for a block write, a fifth register file for the four capability registers, and a cause field where the packet's trap is a boolean. That is a different grammar and not a longer packet, and extending would fork the wire away from every other TestRIG implementation to buy fields the record stream already has. **Version 1 cannot carry the widening at all**, truncating the masks to their byte halves and so deleting the tag bit rather than clearing it.
  * Net change: 2,092 lines added and none removed across nine files, four of them new, and none of them under `model/`.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite at **30 modules**, the new one at **21 cases**; the model typechecking and building green with its bundled suite; the differential corpus at **26 of 26** with every digest matching; the legacy rig at **prefix 67 on all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members**; and the rig itself over **1,307,963 generated instructions**, of which 1,215,700 in the no-defect arm across five templates agreed over 1,995,965 records, 500,000 uniformly random words agreed in 73.8 s, and **all four seeded defects were reported on 20 of 20 seeds each**, the counterexamples shrinking from 1,027 instructions to **1, 1, 5 and 7**. The packet-against-record bridge agrees on every template; on the random one it measures the format gap at **10,560 of 12,769 records the packet has no field for**.
  * **The second executor still does not exist and the rig says which one it drove.** M2's fork is struck and the RTL is R2's, so today it adjudicates the emulator against itself under a seeded defect, each written to need a witness most instructions do not carry, which is what makes the shrink a measurement rather than a demonstration. Its other arm needs no second executor at all: one run emits both dialects, the packets over the socket and the records into the trace log, and that is the only place §9's field-by-field meeting is measured rather than read. When R2 supplies the RTL's `rvfi` port it is this socket it connects to, and nothing on this side changes.
  * Seven findings, and the first two are why the rig works at all.
    * **A torn record reads as a divergence about a run that agreed.** The commit trace's default destination is `stdout`, block-buffered into a pipe, while the RVFI diagnostics go to an unbuffered `stderr`, and the end-of-trace acknowledgement prints verbosely whatever the trace flag says, its call site passing a comparison where every other passes the flag. On one descriptor a diagnostic lands inside a half-written record. The rig gives the trace a file of its own.
    * **Forty-seven milliseconds an instruction, and it is Nagle's algorithm on the implementation's socket.** A version-2 reply is two or three separate writes, the packet and each announced extension, and the second waits on an acknowledgement this side has nothing to piggyback on. Disabling it here does not reach the held write, which is the emulator's; quick acknowledgement, re-armed after every read, took 1,022 instructions from **49.1 s to 0.66 s**.
    * **The block operations stop the emulator rather than reporting something narrower.** The packet's write and read raise an internal error above sixteen bytes, so `cbo.zero`, `cbo.scrub`, `cloadtags` and `creclaim` abort a run under injection. A generated stream excludes them, which is §5's *the packet is not the transport* seen from the side that has to generate around it.
    * **The two emitters disagree about a word whose low two bits are not `11`.** The profile excludes `C` and fixes ILEN at 32, but the fetch path still takes upstream's compressed branch: the commit trace reports the sixteen-bit halfword the model decoded and the packet reports the whole injected word. Which is right is the model's question; the generator spends no stream on encodings the profile does not have.
    * **The source half of the Integer extension is declared and never written**, both source addresses and their data always zero, which is vacuously legal under the packet's own rule and is what a comparison over the full field set would find first.
    * **The physical-address width is 64 and not 36**, so a commit memory record's address is sixteen hexadecimal digits: the 36-bit space is a property of the composition and not of the model's address type. The bridge measures the width off the records it is comparing rather than restating it.
    * **The drift this item would otherwise leave unheld is held by a rule**, K-85 in the differential group: every record kind §4's schema table declares appears in exactly one of §9's meeting table and its elision table, and that membership is the record set the projection and the view actually produce. Without it a schema-version bump drifts §9's tables away from the code in silence, which is the stale-claim shape every other rule here exists to catch.
  * Landed: Tier A.

It is pinned so the edition is fixed and read, and it may not be copied, vendored or extracted from; what it emits is a question no file in it answers.   * This repository has proved the model-as-oracle technique twice and used it nowhere else: M2.1 emitted 21,546 vectors from the model itself and R1a emitted 658,659 over thirteen kinds, two sweeps exhaustive over their own domain, killing twelve seeded defects on between 4 and 61,579 lines.     * **The technique is an instrument rather than a method.** [tools/run.py oracle](../tools/vos/cli/oracle.py) is M2.1's and R1a's rig with the question taken out of it: a spec under [tools/oracle-specs/](../tools/oracle-specs/) names the model sources and, per line kind, the parameters, the domain that walks them, the Sail that calls the model and what to print, and the harness is emitted from it. A parameter's domain is `all`, `draw` or `table`, which is the exhaustive sweep, the sampled one, and the corners a sweep off entropy alone does not reach, and a probe may mix all three, R1a's decode sweep being `all, all, all, draw`. The vectors cross as **text** and the format is R1a's unchanged, so `rtl.py`'s census and comparison read it already. Two specs land: the capability format at **19,472 vectors over eight probes** and the frozen Keccak permutation at **1,000 over two**.
    * **[tools/run.py seed](../tools/vos/cli/seed.py) generates the defects rather than carrying a list of them.** [vos/mutate.py](../tools/vos/mutate.py) walks a Sail or Gallina source with 25 named operators, cutting out comments (a scanner, Rocq's nesting) and string literals, and defaulting the Coq lane's region to the definitional commands, a mutation inside a proof script killing itself and deciding nothing. **Three verdicts and never two**: a stillborn mutant did not compile and decided nothing about the oracle, a killed one moved its answer, and a survivor is the finding. Four oracles are wired: the generated vectors, the prover, the model's own `$[test]` harness and QuickChick.
    * **[tools/run.py quickchick](../tools/vos/cli/quickchick.py) is the Gallina front's input side, which the Wasm oracle has never had.** The enumerative half prints **1,152 vectors** over the admission algebra in the CertiRocq oracle's own switch; the randomized half runs **four property sets at 10,000 draws apiece** under QuickChick, with the counterexample shrinking no enumeration has.
    * **Each instrument is held to seeded defects it kills, and 61 distinct ones are.** The capability format's algebra: nine live mutants of `cap_common.sail` killed on between **1 and 5,499** of 19,472 vectors, and five of `cap_format.sail`'s packing on between **5,638 and 9,170**. The Keccak permutation: seven killed on **1,000 of 1,000**, every one in the rotation and modulus tables that item's own transcription defects lived in. The admission algebra: **27 mutants, the whole population over its definitions, all killed**, ten of them by the generated vectors alone after the shipped theorems had accepted them, on between 28 and 1,152 lines. A finding on the way: asking for the package by name in a clean switch resolves **Coq 8.16.1** rather than the Rocq 9.1.1 this tree pins, so the prover is asked for by name.

    * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green at **33 modules**, four of them new; both type checkers clean; `model.py typecheck` clean and the build green through configure, build and ctest; the proof gate closing **104 constants**; the `$[test]` harness at **96 properties**; and `rtl.py crosscheck` unmoved at **658,659 vectors with no disagreement, line for line over 658,662 lines**, which is what makes the rig refactor behaviour-preserving rather than asserted so.
    * Three findings.
      * **The `$[test]` harness reaches the packing after all, and the difference is resolution rather than reach.** All five live packing mutants are killed on both sides; the harness names one property for every one of them, being the first to fire and the point at which it stops, and the vectors name which field moved and on how many lines.

And R-15-067k names ratification and abandonment as a standing adoption's two exit events, where `Zvzip`'s reshaping is neither: an unratified extension can today be re-shaped out of its own admission argument with no gate firing, and whether the disposition should name that is a ruling this item reports rather than takes.
  * Net change: 5 lines added and 1 removed in one file, with no register act.
  * Exit evidence: the three host gates green in one run; the checker green on all 81 rules with 1,377 co-read pairs read as they now stand, 22 co-stated facts at 63 sites and 5 owned figures at 12 sites unmoved; the selftest killing every one of 90 mutants with the repair path holding and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean. The co-read ledger is unmoved by construction, its pairs running between the register and [spec.md](spec.md) while this item's edit lands in [the profile](isa-profile.md), which is not a side of one.

**S subtotal:** 89.6 h · 8% · 69.1 h complete · open range 14–27 h.

### M0 · Hardware reference

Curated Sail model (§1) → single-core RV64IMV+CHERI emulator; ISA tests green. Define the profile-freeze measurement contract (§0): versioned corpus manifest, emitter-provenance schema, report schema, admitted region classes, and per-choice acceptance thresholds. Publish the shared [differential-testing corpus and the capability-widened commit-trace schema](differential-corpus.md) (§10) that every later executor emits.
