Under the max configuration the same cut excludes 17 tests, the RV32 and RV64 halves of the supervisor family and `pmpaddr` plus the two RV64-only rows; the RV32 machine-CSR test still passes and is not excluded.
    * Four findings.
      * `minstret` had a consumer that is not architectural, RVFI's `order` field, so deleting the counter would have deleted the differential rig's instruction ordering with it; a model-internal `retire_count` register with no CSR address replaces it.
      * The reservation flag on the memory API was never only that: `res`/`con` selected the reserved-read and conditional-write kinds of the concurrency interface, and after `Zalrsc` its one caller is the AMO, whose two halves are exactly those. So the parameter is *derived* from `is_amo_access(access)` rather than deleted, and stops being passable independently of the access it describes.
      * Two tests hang rather than fail and so cannot be left to a ctest timeout: `rv32si-p-dirty` and `rv64si-p-dirty`, whose handlers wait on a page-table A/D update that never comes with S off, the behaviour c2 booked as a profile refusal now appearing where it blocks.
      * `pagesize_bits` survives the walker: PMA regions must still be 4 KiB-aligned so that no PMA property changes inside one access, so the constant is renamed `pma_granule_bits` rather than deleted.

  * [x] **c4 · Vector fork** · 3.5 h actual · 0.3%
    * Removed `vstart` and, with scalar floating point, the whole coupling it was named beside: the `f0`–`f31` register file, `fcsr`/`frm`/`fflags`, `mstatus.FS`, the `F`/`D`/`Zfh`/`Zfhmin`/`Zfa`/`Zfinx`/`Zdinx`/`Zhinx`/`Zhinxmin` extensions with their instruction files, and the scalar half of the bf16 converts. The two forks the profile books (R-15-040, R-15-040a) are now the model's shape rather than a residue in its configuration: `Zve32f` no longer requires `F` and `Zve64d` no longer requires `D`, and the validator rule that demanded them is gone.
    * The vector unit keeps every floating-point form it had here, and M0.19 has since reclaimed two of the three encodings the next bullet books as the freeze's. The scalar operand of a `.vf` instruction is the low SEW bits of an **integer** register, which is where a soft-float-register calling convention already puts a floating-point scalar (R-15-039, R-15-040, [spec.md](spec.md) §15); `vfmv.f.s` writes its element zero-extended into one rather than NaN-boxed, there being no wider register to box into; and rounding is the static RNE of R-15-083, since RVV encodes no rounding-mode field and the conversions that name round-toward-zero pass it themselves.
    * Net change: 5,448 lines removed and 430 added across 56 files, nine deleted outright. The `c_emulator` harness is in that count: the GDB stub's floating-point annex, the `--trace-fpr` option, and the `freg`/`fcsr` accessors and write callback all named registers that no longer exist.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 261/261 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 129 keys ([tools/run.py model config-keys](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), 29 fewer than M0.6d's 158.
    * Expected profile refusals: 53 of 199 physical-variant tests, 52 refusals and the standing `rv64si-p-dirty` hang. This batch adds 24 to M0.6d's 29: eleven `rv64uf`, twelve `rv64ud`, and one more `rv64uzfh`, the test that had passed on F/D register moves while the residue stood. The ctest suite loses the same three families in both XLENs, 66 tests, which is the whole of 327 → 261.
    * Four findings.
      * **The `.vf` forms had to be re-homed rather than deleted**: ten instruction families name an `f` register upstream, and deleting the register file leaves the encodings needing a source, so the operand moves to the integer file and no encoding changes. The cost is that `vfmv.v.f` and `vfmv.s.f` become bit-for-bit duplicates of `vmv.v.x` and `vmv.s.x` and `vfmv.f.s` differs from `vmv.x.s` only in zero- versus sign-extension, which is three encodings the freeze can reclaim rather than a curation batch's call.
      * **`fflags` has no destination and two comparisons still read it**: 44 accrual sites go, and the softfloat interface's flag half is discarded except at `fp_gt`/`fp_ge`, where the invalid-operation flag *is* the result, a quiet comparison answering false on a NaN. The software-visible consequence is that no sticky IEEE flag is readable, so a program needing to detect an invalid or inexact result tests the result.
      * **The scalar files were carrying the vector unit's classification predicates**, 33 `f_is_*_{H,S,D}` plus `negate_{H,S,D}`, and the float library's width-generic `float_is_*` are the same tests, so each three-way dispatch collapses to one line and the negate to a sign-bit flip; only `canonical_NaN` and three flag constructors moved, to [float_classify.sail](../model/model/core/float_classify.sail).
      * **The `vstart` cut deleted an implementation-compatibility hack**: the four scalar-move instructions computed an LMUL-based element count only to match the bound other implementations chose for the `vstart` check, so the count, the LMUL read and the question go together; the whole-register load and store lose their partial-register prologue and the vector-crypto element-group start collapses to zero.
    * One residue, booked rather than closed. R-15-039a states that a vector operation is all-or-nothing while the model writes elements as it goes, so a fault at element *i* leaves the earlier elements written with nothing recording where. On this machine that is a fail-stop program error rather than a resumable trap; whether the register-group write should be buffered to make the claim literal belongs to the capability-fault path M0.6e authors and is settled there.

* [x] **M0.6d · Close the CSR bank** · 2.5 h actual · 0.2%
  * Narrowed `mie`/`mip` to the machine-timer bits, hardwired the implementation identifiers to zero, made `misa` read-only, and deleted `Sdtrig`'s `tselect`. `MEIE`/`MEIP` and `MSIE`/`MSIP` are gone as *fields*: the `Minterrupts` bitfield carries `MTI` alone, so the unnamed bits of the underlying `xlenbits` are the hardwiring and no legalization can set them. `mip` becomes read-only outright, its one writer the timer comparator.
  * Net change: 455 lines removed and 135 added across 19 files, three deleted outright. Two stragglers of c3's delegation cut go with them, both dead rather than wrong: a CMake variable holding the delegatable-bits mask nothing has read since `mideleg` left, and the `WFI` comment stating an exemption over a register that no longer exists.
  * Exit evidence: build green; 327/327 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 158 keys ([tools/run.py model config-keys](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)).
  * Expected profile refusals: 29 of 199 physical-variant tests, every one explained: ten `Zfh` tests the profile excludes, the three c1 cuts, the three c2 cuts, the standing M0.6b pair, c3's ten, and this batch's one, `mi-p-breakpoint`, which opens by writing `tselect` to count the triggers it may use. The sweep is now a repository instrument rather than a per-batch shell loop ([tools/run.py model sweep](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), which is also what classifies a hang as a refusal that blocks rather than leaving it to a ctest timeout.
  * Four findings.
    * The trap on an unallocated CSR address was already total and needed no work: `is_CSR_accessible` defaults to false in `csr_end.sail`, so `check_CSR` rejects every address no clause claims, and c3's exclusion of `mi-p-pmpaddr` for trapping rather than reading zero is the standing evidence. This batch adds four more addresses, `tselect` and `tdata1-3`, whose name-map rows went with the register.
    * Deleting the two interrupt *fields* deletes their two sources with them: the Simple Interrupt Generator device existed only to drive `MEIP`/`MSIP` and goes entirely, and the CLINT's `msip` door with it, leaving the CLINT as the machine timer and nothing else.
    * The external-interrupt bit was the sole reason `mip` read one value into `rd` and another into its own read-modify-write, so its deletion collapses `doCSR`'s two-value split into one and takes the platform-interrupt OR, the `XipReadType` enum, and `read_mip` with it.
    * Two enumerations follow the one-reachable-value rule c3 applied to `Privilege`: `InterruptType` keeps `I_M_Timer` alone, and `breakpoint_cause` goes entirely, `ebreak` being the only breakpoint source once the trigger module is deleted, which also removes a non-injective mapping whose two arms both encoded cause 3.

* [x] **M0.6e · Transplant CHERI capability semantics**
  * Port capability types and compression, merged registers, tag memory, PCC/sentries, machine trap capabilities, load/store checks, and ISAv9 trap causes.
  * Differentially compare RVFI-style traces with the M0.4 oracle.
  * The transplant carries ISAv9's **128-bit** parameterization rather than the frozen one, and that is the batch's shape rather than a delay: the M0.4 oracle is the differential reference, it is a reference only while the two models agree on the format, and what this batch owes M0.6f is an algebra whose field widths are the only thing that has to move (R-15-007a).
  * The extension hooks the transplant hangs off (`ext_data_get_addr`, `ext_control_check_pc`, `ext_fetch_check_pc`, `ext_check_CSR`) have been in their final, privilege-free shape since c3.

  * [x] **e1 · Fix XLEN at 64** · 1.2 h actual · 0.1%
    * `xlen` stops being a configuration key and becomes the constant 64, and the RV32 configuration goes with it: the generated `rv32*` configurations, the `--rv32` default and its embedded JSON, the RV32 test lanes in all three corpora, and the RV32 prover-backend target. The base is RV64 (R-15-001), no plain-RV32 target exists anywhere on the platform (R-18-002), and there is one model of one machine (R-15-005).
    * Net change: 385 lines removed and 254 added across 40 files. Deleted with the base: `mstatush`, which is the RV32 high half of `mstatus` and whose address is now unallocated and therefore trapping (R-15-014); the `Architecture` enumeration with its two-bit encoding and the `architecture()` and `in32BitMode()` queries over it, `misa.MXL` becoming the constant `0b10`; `Zbkb`'s RV32-only `zip` and `unzip`; the RV32 encodings of `zext.h` and `rev8`; the RV32 arms of the `Zbs` mask computation; and `asidlen`, left over from address translation. Thirty-seven vacuous `xlen == 64` guards collapse with them.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 128 keys ([tools/run.py model config-keys](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)), one fewer than c4's 129, the key being `base.xlen`. The RV64 profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199, 52 refusals and the standing `rv64si-p-dirty` hang, which is the point of running it: the freeze removes no RV64 surface, and 261 ctest cases become 150 because the corpus carried an `rv32*` half for a machine this model can no longer be asked for.
    * Four findings.
      * **The transplant forces this cut rather than merely profiting from it.** A register is a capability whose address field is the register width (R-15-007i), and the 128-bit encoding e2 ports has no 32-bit instantiation in this tree, so an RV32 configuration would be a machine with no capability format to run under.
      * **Fixing XLEN changes the generated C++ interface and not only the model.** A configuration-dependent `bits(xlen)` is emitted as the length-carrying `sbits` and a static one as a native `uint64_t`, so sixteen callback signatures in the emulator harness follow it, and the physical-address ones follow too because `physaddrbits_len` was conditional on XLEN as well.
      * **`in32BitMode` was already unreachable and the enumeration under it nearly so**, the one-reachable-value rule c3 applied to `Privilege` arriving here from the configuration rather than from a deleted mode.
      * **The RV32 half of the corpus was never evidence for this profile**, exercising a base the profile does not have, so its removal costs no coverage and every excluded RV64 row is still excluded for the reason it was.

  * [x] **e2 · Port the capability format and algebra** · 0.5 h actual · 0.0%
    * [cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) carries the field widths and the packed encoding; [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) carries CHERI Concentrate over them: decode and encode, the null-capability memory transform, bounds, permissions, sealing, and the monotone derivations with their representability checks. 638 lines of Sail across three new files, the third being the unit test below, with the upstream licence vendored as `model/LICENCE.cheri` because the port derives from a BSD-2-Clause artifact this repository otherwise holds only as a submodule.
    * The capability layer lands in `core/` rather than under `extensions/`: the machine is purecap-only (R-15-001, R-18-002), so the capability *is* the register type, there is no hybrid mode to gate, and an extension gate would be a configuration key with one admitted value. The extension directory takes the instruction surface in e5.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass, and the eight properties in [test_capability.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_capability.sail) pass in the model's own `$[test]` harness: the all-zeroes granule decodes as untagged NULL and NULL stores back as all zeroes (R-15-182, R-15-060), encode and decode round-trip, the reset capability spans the address space, an aligned narrowing is exact and reads back, a five-byte narrowing is byte-exact (R-15-007c), an unrepresentable address move yields an untagged result rather than a trap (R-15-007h), sealing changes only the object type, and permissions round-trip.
    * Two findings.
      * **The relocation guards are dead at the source and are not carried across.** `have_cheri_relocation` is a constant `false` upstream and both the PCC and DDC relocation paths hang off it, so the port folds them out rather than transplanting unreachable branches into a model with no DDC to relocate.
      * **The tag plane needs no new machinery.** Sail 0.20's concurrency interface already carries a capability tag on its read and write requests and the linked runtime implements `emulator_read_tag`/`emulator_write_tag` behind it, so e3's tag memory is a parameter of the memory interface the curated tree already instantiates rather than the upstream model's hand-wired side channel.

  * [x] **e3 · Merge the register file and stand up the tag plane** · 0.5 h actual · 0.0%
    * `regtype` becomes `Capability`, so the file is merged by construction (R-15-007i): `X(r)` reads the address field, `C(r)` reads the whole register, and an integer write yields an untagged capability at that address, which is the merged file's discipline and the reason no capability is reconstructed from a bit pattern (R-05-136). Reset writes the null capability into all 31, because a machine whose registers hold authority cannot leave them undefined the way the base ISA leaves the integer file.
    * The validity tag becomes the metadata every physical access carries, keyed per granule, and the memory path needed no new plumbing for it: the modern tree already splits `mem_read_meta` from `mem_read` and `mem_write_value_meta` from `mem_write_value`, with the plain forms passing `default_meta`, so a data write clears the tag of every granule it covers for free, which is tag clearing as a property of the write path rather than an instruction (R-15-007r, R-15-115a).
    * Net change: 118 lines added over five files. Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; four more properties in the model's `$[test]` harness, that an integer write clears the authority, that the two readings are one register, that `x0` reads null whatever is written to it, and that the tag plane is granule-keyed and cleared across a wide write.
    * Finding: **the granule keying is the model's to do.** The Sail runtime stores one tag per address it is handed, and the concurrency interface's own tag field is keyed by the access address, so nothing below the model rounds an address to a granule. `__WriteRAM_Meta` shifts the address down by the capability size and writes every granule the access spans, which is what makes a tag belong to a granule rather than to a byte, and what makes a wide vector store clear all of them rather than one.

    * PCC authorises every fetch and control transfer and the base register authorises every data access, so no unchecked path remains. `MTCC`, `MEPCC` and `MTDC` are the architectural trap state (R-15-073): a trap installs MTCC as the executing PCC and saves the interrupted PCC in MEPCC, `mret` unseals a sentry out of MEPCC into the executing PCC (R-15-008, R-15-071), and the integer `mtvec` and `mepc` CSRs become views of those two addresses. A capability violation raises ISAv9's cause 28 and reports the violation type and the register that raised it in `mtval` (R-15-073a). Privilege becomes the capability: `ext_check_CSR` and the `mret` check both read the access-system-registers permission off PCC (R-15-003).
    * Net change: 402 lines added and 131 removed over 13 files, two of them new. The page-table-walk accumulator and its error types go with them: they described a walker deleted in c3.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; the profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199; four more `$[test]` properties, that a trap saves PCC into MEPCC and installs MTCC, that `mret` unseals a sentry, that a fetch outside PCC's bounds and a fetch under an untagged PCC each raise their own violation, and that a store without store permission and a load through an untagged base register each fault where the access is rather than where the value was made.
    * Two findings.
      * **The transplant cannot delete DDC on its own schedule, and the corpus is why.** Every test in `riscv-tests` addresses memory with an integer base register, so with no default data capability every load and store faults at its base: the first build with DDC deleted hung five machine-mode tests on exactly that. Hybrid mode and DDC therefore stay until M0.6f, which also sets that item's exit criterion, since after the cut `riscv-tests` is not a corpus this machine can run and the batch is gated on purecap test programs (M0.12) instead.
      * **The trap raise has to sit in the postlude.** A check is core surface because it reads PCC, but raising a trap calls the exception machinery in `sys`, compiled after `core`, so the three `ext_handle_*_error` hooks and the two privilege-failure hooks move to `postlude/cap_traps.sail`, the first point at which both halves are in scope.
  * [x] **e5 · Port the instruction surface and stand up the differential harness** · 3 h actual · 0.3%

    * Three ISAv9 groups are **not** carried across, each because the machine no longer has what they act on rather than because the profile excludes them: `FPClear` clears a scalar floating-point file c4 deleted; `LoadRes*`, `StoreCond*`, and the sub-word `lr`/`sc` decodings ISAv9 adds to the base need a reservation c3 deleted with `Zalrsc`; and `AMOSwapCap` is a capability-width atomic swap where `A` is narrowed to `Zaamo` + `Zabha` at byte, halfword, word, and doubleword (R-15-024), so the width it names is not one the adopted atomic surface carries.
    * The differential rig is [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) `trace-diff`, over the trace dialects and adjudication [tools/vos/trace.py](../tools/vos/trace.py) holds: either executor's dialect becomes one record stream, the two are aligned on the first program counter the curated model retires, and the report names where they stop agreeing. The record set is the intersection of what the two print today, a retire, a register-write address, and a data read.
    * Net change: 1,940 lines added and 35 removed across 13 files, six of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; 150/150 tests pass; the profile sweep is unchanged at 53 of 199; the ten new properties in [test_cheri_insts.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_cheri_insts.sail) pass, bringing the model's own harness to 28; and the rig runs the 54-member `rv64ui-p-*` corpus with an agreeing prefix of 97 records on every member.
    * Four findings.
      * **The read path was discarding the tag.** e3 wired the granule-keyed plane into the write path, but `checked_mem_read` returned `default_meta` unconditionally, an upstream `TODO` about folding metadata across splits, so no capability could ever be loaded with its tag set; e5 is the first batch with an instruction that reads one, which is what surfaced it. The fold is now the conjunction over the splits, exactly that granule's tag for an unsplit access and the conservative answer for a split one, at the same polarity as the write path's clearing.
      * **`lc` collides with `cbo.zero`.** ISAv9 puts the capability load at MISC-MEM `funct3` 010 where the cache-block operations also sit, separated only by a zero destination and a small immediate; upstream never meets them, not implementing the `Zicboz` this profile adopts (R-15-060). The destination separates them here, and the frozen dialect re-encodes this anyway (R-15-007), so what is owed at the freeze is a decision rather than a repair.
      * **The two executors neither start at the same instruction nor report a register write at the same width.** The oracle enters through a reset-vector ROM at 0x1000 this platform has no counterpart for, so the streams align on the first PC the curated model retires rather than on the step number; and the oracle prints the whole capability on a register write where this side prints the address alone, which M0.12 widens. The store side is not comparable at all, the oracle emitting no store line.
      * **`riscv-tests` cannot reach agreement past its own prologue, which is a fact about the corpus rather than about the transplant.** Each test points `mtvec` at the end of a group and then writes `medeleg`, an illegal instruction once c3 stripped delegation, so the trap skips the group the oracle retires and both machines reach the same result by different routes. The prefix is 97 on all 54 members, so the regression the rig carries is that it must not *shorten*, which `--floor` enforces.
    * One refactor the surface forced, and it removes a duplicate rather than adding one: the explicit-authority loads and stores name their own authority where an ordinary access lets the mode pick it, so [core/addr_checks.sail](../model/model/core/addr_checks.sail) splits into `cap_mode_auth`, which selects, and `cap_data_checks`, which decides, with `ext_data_get_addr` composing the two.

* [x] **M0.6f · Re-parameterize to 64+1 bits** · 5 h actual · 0.5%
  * The format is [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §4.1's and spends the 64 bits exactly: a 36-bit address in the low bits, a 4-bit object type, a 5-bit permission field, a 5-bit exponent, and 8- and 6-bit mantissas with the top's high two derived, the validity tag outside them and the tag plane's granule narrowing with the capability to 64 bits (R-15-007, R-15-002a, R-15-203). No reserved field, no software-defined permission field, no capability-mode flag, and no revocation colour appears, each needing a bit the table has already spent (R-15-007n, R-15-001c, R-08-004b).
  * **Permissions become an enumerated lattice rather than one bit per permission**, which is the one place the narrowing changes the algebra (R-15-007b). Bit 4 of the field is local versus global, orthogonal to authority; the other four name sixteen shapes, from the bottom element through the read and read-write data shapes, the three transitivity levels a capability load passes on, the stack's `store-local`, the execute shapes, the privileged code root, and the two sealing authorities. The decode is **total**, so the field has no illegal encoding to reject; the two lattice exclusions are finite checks over the table rather than properties of one distribution, no admitted set holding both store and execute (R-15-007l) and none holding both seal and unseal (R-15-007o); and what software sees is an *expanded* 12-bit bitmap, which is what lets `candperm`'s mask stay an ordinary bitwise operand over a non-orthogonal field, its result the largest admitted set inside the intersection. R-15-074's `load-global` and `load-mutable` transitivity arrives with the permissions that express it, applied on the load path and exact rather than rounded: the read half of every admitted set is itself an admitted set.
  * Removed with hybrid mode: `DDC` and its `cspecialrw` number, its `mtval` register index, the capability-mode flag with the second decode mapping and its `NOT_CAPMODE` wildcard, `cgetflags`/`csetflags`, the four explicit-authority data forms and the four explicit-authority capability forms, ISAv9's `cclear`, and the two mode-independent jumps. Removed on their own grounds: the reconstruction group (`cbuildcap`, `ccopytype`, `ccseal`, and the `cgethigh`/`csethigh` pair), the runtime bounds-rounding group (`cram`, `crrl`, `csetboundsexact`), `ctestsubset`, `ccleartag`, `cinvoke`, and the software-defined permission field. Six cause codes go with the mechanisms they named.
  * Net change: 1,125 lines added and 1,255 removed across 23 files, one deleted outright, `extensions/I/jalr_seq.sail`, whose one clause was the integer reading of `jalr`.
  * Exit evidence: build green; `verifiedos.json` validates against the regenerated schema and its key set agrees with the generated max configuration at 128 keys, unchanged, the re-parameterization touching no configuration key; and the model's own `$[test]` harness passes at **36 properties against 28**, thirteen of them new, covering the field budget, decode's totality over the encoding, the permission enumeration's totality and its two exclusions, narrowing's monotonicity over every codepoint and every single-bit mask, the split root set, the frozen object-type space, the out-of-range address, the sentry split at `cjalr`, and the load's transitivity.
  * Five findings.
    * **The exponent field carries the case flag, because the table leaves no bit beside it.** CHERI Concentrate spends a bit saying whether the exponent sits inside the mantissas; here the exponent is an explicit five-bit field, so the flag rides the field IEEE-754 fashion, zero the denormal case and *k* + 1 the normalized case at effective exponent *k*. The effective exponent tops out at 30, so the five bits are spent exactly. The packing is forced by the field table rather than chosen, and it costs no mantissa bits where upstream's costs three.
    * **The byte-exact threshold the frozen widths deliver is 128 bytes and the padding above it is one part in 64 per side, and R-15-007c is amended to them.** The exponent is chosen so the decoder knows the length mantissa's top two bits, which is what lets the top mantissa be stored in six and derived into eight, so an exponent of zero reaches half the mantissa's span. The register's 256 bytes and one-part-in-256 read off the mantissa width alone, the same two bits separating each figure from the width. Both are now model properties over an unaligned base, so a later change to the widths or the packing cannot move them silently, and every consumer moves with them: R-15-007e and R-15-007f's admissibility argument for the indexed load and store, which the narrower threshold **strengthens**, R-15-007k's relocated rounding, R-08-004b's closed bit budget, and the alternatives register's declined bounds-check elision.
    * **An integer register is 64 bits and an address is 36, so a register's integer reading is its whole 64 data bits, and R-15-007i is amended to say so.** The address field is their low 36, which keeps "an integer operand is that register's address field" true of every integer that *is* an address (R-15-002a) and leaves the rest to RV64I's arithmetic; reading at the address width would truncate every integer write to 36 bits and leave the machine without XLEN arithmetic. Nothing is forged by the wider reading, an integer write being untagged, and the round trip rests on decode being total over the 64 bits. The null transform applies to the register reading too, so `x0` reads integer zero and a register stored with `sd` and reloaded with `lc` is the capability it was. The one root is likewise a **pair**: W+X being unrepresentable, reset hands PCC the execute side and `c1` the store side, which carries `store-local` because `candperm` can only remove (R-15-007p).
    * **The corpus goes with DDC, and it goes entirely rather than family by family.** Every `riscv-tests` program addresses memory with an integer base register, which a purecap machine reads as an untagged capability and faults on, so eleven extension-by-extension filters collapse into one ground and the suite is excluded from registration; the profile sweep still runs it and returns 199 refusals of 199 against 53. The differential rig loses its second executor at the same edge, an oracle being a reference only where it implements the same machine, and the prefix falls from 97 to 67 on all 54 members. The rig outlives the oracle: its standing second executor is the RTL under co-simulation, M2's fork having been carried for that job and struck with the rest of M2, and its corpus is M0.12's.
    * **Membership is by enumeration, which reaches four instructions and one otype decision this item's own list does not name.** Nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j), so `cinvoke` goes with the call gate `cjalr` replaces (R-15-068), ISAv9's `cclear` with the split file its two quadrants address (R-15-069b), and the two mode-independent jumps with the second mode that gave them a reason. The same rule freezes the object-type space: three of sixteen codepoints are reserved, the unsealed one and **two** sentry edges, R-15-071's forward/backward split being a reservation in that space rather than a later amendment to it. `cjalr` enforces the split by role and `csealentry` mints the forward edge alone, so a return capability is never software's to forge.

  * The six rows land as one batch: `cmovz`/`cmovn` in the dialect's own encoding, the revocation plane with its filter and its bitmap, `fence.t`, the capability indexed load and store, `cclear`, and the machine-level AIA pending array. `cloadtags` needed nothing, being ISAv9's and already carrying the two parameters the profile fixes (M0.6e, e5). No measurement-conditioned provisional row is modeled, and the single-check multi-register save is struck rather than deferred (R-15-036n).
  * **The three custom-opcode rows spend one opcode between them.** ISAv9 already spent custom-2 on the whole capability surface, and custom opcode space is exactly what the freeze's single measured act makes the remaining candidates compete for (R-15-014a, R-15-067e), so `cld`, `csd` and `cclear` take custom-0 separated by `funct3` and leave custom-1 and custom-3 whole; the indexed pair carries `ld`'s and `sd`'s own width code, so the width reads off the three bits the base ISA puts it in. `cmovz`/`cmovn` take the dialect's encoding rather than that space and so contend for nothing, at the first free `funct7` pair above ISAv9's highest three-operand allocation.
  * Net change: 1,845 lines added and 46 removed across 23 files, eight of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **43 properties against 36**, seven of them new; the differential corpus green at **15 of 15** with every trace digest matching, four members and 37 checks new and the eleven existing digests unmoved; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Five findings.
    * **The revocable interval's width is the model's to bound and the covered union is a predicate rather than a list.** R-08-005a sizes the payload at Σ|I|/64 bytes against the SRAM capacity budget, a composition property, where a Sail register is a fixed width. So the plane fixes 4096 granules, `validate_config` refuses a composition that does not fit rather than letting its top granules fall silently outside the filter, and a second interval is a clause in `revocation_covered` rather than a mechanism, every consumer quantifying over the predicate.
    * **Deleting the AIA indirect interface is what puts the pending array in the address map.** Upstream an interrupt file exposes `seteipnum` to senders and leaves the pending bits to `miselect`/`mireg`/`mtopei`; with those excluded the array has nowhere else to be, and *software reads pending bits with ordinary loads* is R-15-065's own sentence, so the file has two doors and the receiver's is also how a bit is cleared.
    * **`fence.t`'s Sail content is the enumeration rather than the barrier**: the flush set is one structure and this model has no store buffer, so what the file carries is why every other structure is absent from the set. Its reserved fields trap where `fence`'s are ignored, this instruction having no future extension to reserve them for.
    * **The generated test-matrix configurations are a second declaration of the platform**: two new keys broke the build at schema conformance on `rv64d_*.json` rather than on the profile's own configuration, a key added to one and not to [config.json.in](../model/config/config.json.in) being a build failure rather than a divergence found later.
    * **The model's own harness runs under the generated *max* configuration rather than the profile's**, so a property reading a configured policy is a property about the wrong machine: that a misaligned indexed load faults is the profile's answer and not that configuration's, which defers misalignment to the PMA. Profile-conditional behaviour belongs in the corpus.
  * One repair outside the model, because the gate could not otherwise run: [run.py typecheck](../tools/vos/cli/typecheck.py) looked for its pinned `ty` and `ruff` beside the running interpreter, which is where a POSIX interpreter and a Windows virtual environment put console scripts and not where a Windows *system* install puts them, so a stock host install read as *not installed* however many times it was installed. A pinned-version gate whose failure mode is indistinguishable from an absent tool is a gate that gets skipped, so it takes both directories.

* [x] **M0.6h · Add the three bespoke block instructions** · 5 h actual · 0.5%
  * `vmclear` (R-15-069d), `creclaim` (R-15-007s), and `cbo.scrub` (R-15-177a) land as one batch, none of them inherited from either upstream model. `vmclear` and `cbo.scrub` join `fence.t` under `extensions/platform/`; `creclaim` lands beside `cloadtags`, whose authority checks it shares and whose group it walks.
  * **The four custom-opcode rows still spend one opcode between them.** `vmclear` takes custom-0 `funct3` 001 beside `cclear`'s 000 and below the indexed access's width-coded 011 and 111, so custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). The other two contend for none of that space: `creclaim` takes the first sub-opcode of ISAv9's two-operand group that ISAv9 never allocated, so a reader checking it against ISAv9 finds free space rather than a re-used opcode; `cbo.scrub` takes MISC-MEM `funct3` 010 at immediate 5, at a zero destination as every cache-block operation has, rather than one of the three points `Zicbom` vacated, because reusing a named standard encoding makes a stock disassembler print the wrong instruction where an unallocated one makes it print none.
  * Net change: 1,172 lines added and 53 removed across 17 files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **46 properties against 43**, three of them new; the differential corpus green at **17 of 17** with two members and 19 checks new and the fifteen existing digests unmoved; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    * **The vector gate is the class and not the context, and running it is what found that.** R-15-069d's word is *vectorless class*, a static property of the hart, and the model has two predicates that both read as "V is available": `hartSupports(Ext_Zve32x)`, which is that property, and `currentlyEnabled(Ext_Zve32x)`, which folds in `mstatus.VS != Off`. The first build gated on the second and failed its own decode assertion at a reset whose `VS` is `Off`. The repair is not cosmetic: `VS` is partition-writable, so a partition could dirty the vector unit, write the gate off, and leave the switch's `vmclear` trapping on exactly the residue it exists to erase (R-07-014a, R-15-214), and it would make the clear conditional in the sense R-15-069c rejects.
    * **The scrub had to become an arm of the access type rather than a convention of its caller.** `CacheAccess` carried one arm and the four matches over it were wildcards or single-arm, right while `cbo.zero` was the only cache-block operation. The scrub differs in both directions: it reads *and* writes, so it needs both permissions or a holder of a read-only view could cause a write; and it verifies an error-detecting code, so it belongs over main memory rather than over whatever `supports_cbo_zero` admits, a device register having no codeword to scrub. Both are properties of the operation, so `CB_scrub` is a second arm and the four matches gain a case each.
    * **The welded block size stopped belonging to `cloadtags`.** Defined beside the first instruction that needed it, it is now read by four instructions in three modules, so it moves to `core/cap_format.sail` beside the granule it is a multiple of. That is also where it is legible as the parameter M0.13 scores against two geometries at once (R-15-007q, R-15-014a (vii)), a value satisfying one consumer and not another being a defect rather than a choice.
    * **`cbo.scrub`'s telemetry and fail-stop have no architectural state to hang on, and are not invented here.** This model has no error model, so nothing is ever correctable or uncorrectable and a fault-injection register would model the harness rather than the machine. What the file carries is where each half lands: correction telemetry goes to the sentinel, a device and a core class rather than a structure the scrubbing hart can read, which is what makes correction invisible in the timing (R-15-179); and the fail-stop needs no new channel, the block read already returning `MemoryOpResult`, so a detection joins the path a PMA refusal takes.
    * **What the scrub *is* in an architectural model is the tag-preserving pass, and that is only visible against the write path.** An ordinary store of a granule's own bits back to it clears that granule's validity tag (R-15-007r), so maintaining a capability-bearing array through the store path would destroy the authority in it. That is why the scrub is a block operation of its own rather than a load-and-store loop, and it is what [platform-scrub.s](../corpus/platform-scrub.s) checks by doing both to the same block.
    * **`creclaim`'s clear lands in memory where the load filter's lands in a delivered value**, the two being the reclamation half and the containment half of one protocol rather than two spellings of one act: clearing a revocation bit puts the object back in service for the filter and does not for `creclaim`, the tag being gone from the granule rather than from a value (R-08-007a). The clause reads each granule as **bits and a tag** rather than as a decoded capability and writes back the bits it read, so surviving granules are untouched bit for bit and a cleared one differs in its tag alone, which keeps R-15-007r's exclusion of a *bulk* tag clear undisturbed.

#### Remaining M0 deliverables

* [x] **M0.7 · Model Ztso and static-only prediction** · 3.5 h actual · 0.3%
  * The model stops being silent about its memory model and states it. `Ext_Ztso` enters the extension registry ([core/extensions.sail](../model/model/core/extensions.sail)) hardwired `true` and read from no configuration key, which is the requirement rather than an oversight: R-15-004 adopts Ztso normatively in place of RVWMO, so a key would make the memory model a composition parameter and admit a composition of this machine that is not this machine. It is the one row in that registry that is not a `config` read, and `hartSupports` is what the generated `riscv,isa` string and `riscv,isa-extensions` array are built from, so an attested devicetree now names the model where it named nothing.
  * **`fence` collapses to a drain or a no-op and `barrier_kind` collapses with it.** The eleven-member enumeration ([core/phys_mem_interface.sail](../model/model/core/phys_mem_interface.sail)) becomes one, `Barrier_RISCV_drain`: nine were the RVWMO predecessor and successor lattice R-15-017 deletes, `Barrier_RISCV_tso` was `fence.tso`'s and that instruction is now a bare retire, and `Barrier_RISCV_i` had been unreachable since `Zifencei` left at c1. `fence.t` emits the same member, so the flush set and the drain are one structure rather than two spellings (R-15-213), and the difference between the two instructions is the condition rather than the effect.
  * **`fm` stops being an operand.** It is the literal `0b0000` in the `FENCE` encdec, so every reserved value reaches no clause and traps (R-15-014, R-15-017), and the drain predicate reads the I and O bits directly as one mask per side rather than as an arm of a 256-case lattice.
  * Net change: 499 lines added and 173 removed across twelve files, two of them new and one deleted.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **56 properties against 52**, four of them new; `verifiedos.json`'s key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **166 keys, unchanged**, no key added because the memory model is not configurable; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,741 bytes against 2,733, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to, and naming `ztso` in both renderings; the differential corpus green at **20 of 20** with one member and 8 checks new, the manifest at version 4, and the nineteen existing digests unmoved; the checker green on all 57 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Five findings.
    * **The item's honest reach is small, and stating it is part of the deliverable.** `sail_barrier` is a no-op in the C backend, no emulator source names a barrier kind, and the model runs one hart, so nothing about ordering is observable end to end; the nineteen existing trace digests being unmoved by a batch that rewrote the fence is the measurement of that rather than a coincidence. What the model now does is *state* the memory model, R-15-016 putting the guarantee itself on the RTL side. The one architecturally observable change in the item is the reserved-`fm` trap.
    * **That trap is the profile diverging from the base ISA's semantics rather than from its encoding, and it is a deleted rule rather than an added check.** The base ISA instructs an implementation to treat every reserved `fm` as `fm`=0000 and execute it, for forward compatibility with fences a later extension might define; R-15-014 refuses that for the whole encoding space. So `fm` leaves the `FENCE` union for a literal the encdec matches, and the reserved values trap by reaching no clause, the decoder doing with them what it does with any unallocated word.
    * **The drain condition reads the I and O bits, which is what lets `is_fiom_active` go.** Upstream ignores I and O in `fence`'s execute and made the implication conditional on the `FIOM` bit of `menvcfg`/`senvcfg`, both of which left with the mode they gated at c3, leaving a function hardwired to `false` with one caller. The unconditional reading that replaces it is strictly stronger, so nothing is lost, but it is a semantic divergence from upstream `fence` rather than only an encoding one and belongs in front of a reviewer.
    * **One enumeration member had been dead since c1 and nothing said so**, which is the defect class the narrowing removes rather than the one it found: `Barrier_RISCV_i` lost its only constructor site when `Zifencei` was excluded, and an enum member with no site is not an error in Sail. Narrowing to one member also orphaned [handwritten_support/riscv_extras_sequential.lem](../model/handwritten_support/), scaffolding of the RMEM lane c1 retired that no `CMakeLists.txt` references, so it goes with the members it names; the `hgen/` herdtools directory beside it is unreferenced on the same footing and is booked rather than deleted on this batch's authority.
    * **Static-only prediction adds no Sail at all, and what the model gains is a citation.** A search of the model tree for predictor state returns nothing, which is both the state R-15-019 requires and the state an unwritten requirement would leave, so from inside the tree the two are indistinguishable. R-15-021 says why that is correct: the deletion is discharged structurally by rows A-04 through A-06 of the [absence contract](absence-contract.md) rather than by RTL refinement, no model being able to state *there is no branch predictor* (R-15-098). So the row numbers land in [fence_t.sail](../model/model/extensions/platform/fence_t.sail), the one file already making the claim, and a reader who follows them arrives at the register.

  * Split at entry, and the split is the C-class freeze's: M0.8a is the class table and the roster, which is all the freeze needs from the parameterization and adds no decode surface, and M0.8b to M0.8d are the datapaths those classes name, deferred until before the fast emulator would have carried them, which the M2 strike has now removed. They are parallel with each other because they touch different extension directories, and each is one build configuration of the same model rather than a fork of it (R-15-005).

    * The classes land as composition-time declarations and nothing else: [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) carries R-15-113's five-class enumeration, the per-class datapath record, and the roster `mhartid` indexes; both enter the attested devicetree R-09-007 puts them in ([postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)); and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface**: the class is not architectural state and no CSR reports it, one kernel binary running unmodified on every class and selecting its per-hart state, the core's class, and the island binding out of `mhartid` against that devicetree at boot (R-15-052b, R-07-012, R-04-009).
    * **Counts are derived and declared nowhere** (R-15-113). The roster enumerates harts and a class's count is the number of its entries, so the reference instantiation's six C, four plus two V, two M, one S and one RoT is a claim about the composed roster with exactly one thing to be checked against. `frozen` beside the numbers does what `qualified` does beside the memory classes' (M0.14): R-15-108's exploration selects VLEN per class, core count, issue width, pipeline depth and scratchpad size, it has not run, and a table carrying R-15-113's reference figures with nothing separating them from selected ones is how a placeholder becomes an architectural input.
    * **K-60** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py), over the new [vos/coreclass.py](../tools/vos/coreclass.py), holds the table itself. Four artifacts write it, three of them prose in three different idioms, and the rule requires every class's vector geometry to be one figure across all four and every count the specification states to be the composed roster's. Report-only and never repaired, for K-57's two reasons: the composition is under a `-text` tree, and which geometry a class takes is R-15-108's exploration rather than arithmetic.
    * Net change: 1,522 lines added and 32 removed across 22 files, three of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **59 properties against 56**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **228 keys against 166**; the differential corpus green at **21 of 21** with one member and 7 checks new and the twenty existing digests unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green, the generated tree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 4,466 bytes and accepted by the emulator's own bound against the region it is written into, and refused by that bound against the region it had; each of the **fourteen** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on **all 62 rules against 61**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean.
    * Seven findings.
      * **`mhartid` was a selector into a table that did not exist.** R-15-052b's criterion is that the reachable value space is a composition-time constant fixed by the core roster, so a hart identity outside the roster is not a value the machine can present. `platform.hartid` was read, hardwired into a register, and held against nothing, so any composition could declare any identity and the criterion had no instrument at all; the roster closes it.
      * **The class table cannot derive the vector geometry, so the binding is a refusal.** `vlen_exp` in [core/vlen.sail](../model/model/core/vlen.sail) is a **type**-level configuration sizing the vector register file, so one build realizes one VLEN and no value-level record can compute it. What is left is a refusal, a composition whose roster puts this hart on a class declaring some other geometry being rejected before it runs; the generated configurations' template binds the C row to their own exponent so all eight stay consistent with their own class table.
      * **The `Zvl` ladder stopped at 1024 and would have described a V-class build as narrower than it is.** This profile's V class is VLEN=4096 and its M class 1024, and a truncated ladder is invisible today, every rung above 1024 reading false at every geometry this model is built at. It would have surfaced at M0.8b as an attested devicetree understating the machine's own guarantee, so both rungs land here and the generated tree is unmoved.
      * **The `cpu` node hardwired hart zero.** `reg = <0>` and `cpu@0` were emitter literals while `mhartid` was configurable, so a composition at any other identity would have described hart zero to a machine that is not it. Both now follow `plat_hartid`; the label stays `CPU0`, being a DTS label rather than an identity. Every configuration this tree ships is at zero, which is what made the repair free and also what hid it.
      * **The roster is one property rather than sixteen nodes, and both halves of that are forced.** A devicetree property carries cells or a string and not both, so the class column is an index the class nodes emit beside their own names; and a `cpu` node asserts a hart the emulator runs, which is one (R-15-005), so sixteen would describe cores that are not there and would not fit the ROM region besides.
      * **A devicetree source is a grammar rather than a rendering, and `dtc` is what says so.** Juxtaposed cell arrays are a syntax error rather than a concatenation, and a property written after a subnode is refused rather than reordered. Neither is visible from inside the model, whose emitter is string concatenation and typechecks either way, which is why the compile is in the exit evidence and the size is measured on the blob.
      * **The tree outgrew the region it is written into and nothing said so, because the bound was on a path no ordinary run takes.** The blob went from 2,741 bytes to 4,466 against a 4 KiB ROM. The emulator carries the bound, `dtb_within_configured_pma_memory`, but it sits on the `--device-tree-blob` path while `--validate-config` exits before reaching it, so a *generated* tree that no longer fits was silent on every build, corpus run and sweep. Three things follow: the ROM region is sized for the tree it holds rather than left at 4 KiB, which is upstream's spike-bare default rather than a budget anybody set, and the region's extent is the composition's to state; the per-class core count comes back *out* of the tree, a count beside a roster being one fact written twice inside one attested artifact; and `model.py devicetree` now generates, compiles, and hands the blob to the emulator's own bound rather than to a second copy of it, fired in both directions before landing.

    * Seven findings.
      * **The type-level configuration is compile-time for the provers and load-time for the emulator, and only the second half was written down.** M0.8a concluded from `vlen_exp` being type-level that one build realizes one VLEN. That holds of the prover lanes, SMT and Rocq since S3 retired the other two, which take `--config` on the Sail command line and bake the geometry in. It does not hold of the C++ lane, which takes no `--config` at emission: the emitted model reads the configuration when it loads one, and `sizeof(vlen_exp)` answers 8 or 12 according to which file it was handed.
      * **The model has one inactive-element mask and R-15-115b's obligation attaches to a third of it.** `init_masked_result` and `init_masked_source` return one bit vector clear for three unrelated reasons: past `vl`, past a fractional LMUL's element count, or turned off by `v0`. Only the third is a *mask* and only the third is what R-15-085 forbids observing, `vl` and `vtype` being architectural registers a partition reads back. Checking every inactive element would have made the discarded check a function of `vl` and put the tail into the memory traffic, a cost with no obligation behind it. The separation is derived from the existing function rather than restated, a second copy of the tail arithmetic being how the two would come to disagree.
      * **The model pads every masked-off access rather than only the failing one, which is stronger than the requirement and is forced.** R-15-115b pads the *failing* masked-off access so that an unauthorized address never reaches the fabric while the bank cycle is still spent. This model executes no cycles, so an access issued here would contribute no timing and would instead contribute what a masked-off element must not have: a store would write memory, and a load of a device endpoint would be a read with a side effect. A discarded check has no architecturally observable result, so the property asserts both halves against one address, the operation retiring and the same check at the same address reporting the violation it did not raise.
      * **Two of the item's four requirements were already satisfied and nothing said so.** Every clause of the vector memory surface already reached memory through `data_read`/`data_write`, which compose `ext_data_get_addr` over `cap_data_checks`, so R-08-003's per-element check has been in the model since the e5 transplant; R-15-115a's tag clear is likewise the write path's. What was missing was the statement, the citation, and anything that would notice either going.
      * **The extension-context dirty fired once per element and put the geometry into the commit trace.** `wV` calls `dirty_v_context` on every element it writes and that function wrote `mstatus` unconditionally, so one vector load emitted VLEN/SEW `mstatus` records: `vector-memory` traced 585 records at VLEN=256 and 1,425 at VLEN=4096 for the same program, which makes a member's digest a fact about which class ran it. Made conditional on the value moving, the idiom `write_vxsat` already uses, the trace is 515 records and byte-identical at both geometries.
      * **`vlseg<nf>e<eew>ff.v` was a vector operation that was neither all nor nothing, and the register has since answered it: the form is excluded.** Its defined result on a fault past the first element was to trim `vl` and retire rather than raise, which R-15-039a's all-or-nothing rule does not admit. This item left the clause as it found it, because either that rule was narrower than it read or the fault-only-first forms left the profile, and neither is a curation batch's call; R-15-039b takes the second arm, and the bullet below is the deletion it makes owed.
      * **The assembler's vector rows needed one operand that is spelled by being absent.** `v0.t` marks a masked operation and nothing marks an unmasked one, which is how every RISC-V assembler spells it and how the model's own `maybe_vmask` mapping reads it back, so the mask is the one operand whose *absence* is a value rather than a missing argument. It is handled where the arity is checked rather than by giving each masked form a second mnemonic, which would have doubled forty-three rows to say one thing.
    * **The fault-only-first forms are deleted, which is a profile amendment carried rather than this item reopened.** R-15-039b excludes `vle<eew>ff.v` and the `vlseg<nf>e<eew>ff.v` segment forms, so `VLSEGFFTYPE` leaves [extensions/V/vext_mem_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/V/vext_mem_insts.sail) entire, 84 lines of union clause, `encdec`, execute clause and assembly mapping; [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py) loses the four `vle<eew>ff.v` rows with them, so no corpus member can assemble a word the model no longer decodes.
      **`lumop` 0b10000 decoding to nothing is the end state rather than a hole in the encoding space** (R-15-014): every reserved, custom and unused encoding traps, so a point no clause claims reaches the illegal-instruction path by the route every unallocated point does. That is the whole of what the deletion costs, R-15-040a's disposition covering the residue unchanged and no calling convention or compiler naming the form.

    * **Re-priced and deferred past M8a on this item's own finding.** If IME ratifies, the surface R-15-014a (ix) admits is a version pin rather than an authored encoding, so the cell prices a pin and a review rather than an encoding act; and the item gates no milestone here, its scratchpad half being landed and its instruction half conditioned on a margin no backend and no composed image can supply.
    * **The scratchpad half is in the tree and the item stays open on the other half, which the profile has not yet enumerated.** The software-managed scratchpad is an addressable region class ([sys/pma.sail](../model/model/sys/pma.sail)): capability-governed plain memory at a fixed address range, on neither of main memory's two latency classes, coherence-exempt, and not executable, with a composition declaring a wrong one refused rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). The partition switch's single unconditional pass covers exactly the declared range and takes the validity tags with it (R-07-014c, R-15-007r, [extensions/platform/vmclear.sail](../model/model/extensions/platform/vmclear.sail)); the attested devicetree carries the link from a class to its range rather than the range twice (R-09-007, [postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)); and R-15-009's standing re-pin obligation is recorded beside the geometry the model declares for the unit ([core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail)). **No instruction is added, and what stops it is a gap in the enumeration rather than a shortfall in the work**: the frozen profile books the matrix extension as a *unit* and names no mnemonic, operand form, or encoding for it, and nothing enters the profile by inheritance (R-15-007j).
    * **The custom-opcode ledger does not move.** M0.6g's three rows spend one opcode between them, M0.6h's four still do, and this item adds no fifth: custom-0 (`0b0001011`) carries `cclear` at `funct3` 000, `vmclear` at 001 and the indexed pair's width-coded 011 and 111, leaving 010, 100, 101 and 110 unspent in that opcode and custom-1 and custom-3 whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). The matrix extension is not one of those rows *yet*, and which act admits it is now written down: R-15-014a's second-act delta carries it at **(ix)**, its instruction surface, operand sourcing and encoding together, conditioned on R-15-116's sustained dense-GEMM margin, which no backend and no composed image can supply before M1. So custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole against it exactly as M0.6g and M0.6h left them whole for the rows the freeze's single measured act admits, and spending either here would be modelling a measurement-conditioned provisional row, which is the one thing M0.6g's exit condition forbids.
    * **What remains, and why it is a gap rather than a remainder.** R-15-118's coprocessor line and R-08-003's per-element checks need an instruction to be stated over, so they arrive with the surface and not before it; R-15-116's admission is already taken, dense int8/bf16 inference clearing the 8–10× bar and the extension kept, so what is unwritten is the encoding and not the decision. Closing it needs the profile to enumerate the extension's instructions the way it enumerates every other bespoke row's, which is a register and profile act ahead of a model one (R-15-001a).
    * **The margin the surface is conditioned on now has a normative denominator, and that is the one thing about this item that could still close it the other way.** R-15-116a fixes the RVV expression the margin is taken against as the strongest one the profile admits on the M class, dot products included wherever the standing adoption R-15-067l records has ratified by then, and requires the report to record the denominator's own extension set beside its figure. A batched dot product collects the reduction tree an int8 GEMM otherwise pays per output element and produces eight outputs at once, so it is a term in that denominator rather than in the numerator, and *the admission above was taken against a denominator this profile has since made stronger*. Both branches are live and this item closes on either. If the margin clears, the disposition is as written and the surface is a pin or an authored encoding. If it falls below the threshold, R-15-116 **deletes the extension by its own criterion**, and this item closes by striking its instruction half rather than by authoring one: `vmclear`'s empty matrix inventory (below) becomes empty by construction, R-15-117's software duty and R-15-009's re-pin obligation go with the unit, and custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole for something else. Under §1's value function that is the cheaper outcome, which is why the measurement is worth taking before the second act rather than after it.

Its task group's ratification plan puts the specification frozen at v0.9 on 2026-06-27, TSC approval at v0.99 on 2026-08-17, and ratification at v1.0 on 2026-08-27. **AME**, the arm that does introduce architectural state of its own, took a one-year task-group extension in April 2026, and it is the arm this design's deletions argue against.

Its own plan's ratification date of 2026-08-27 is therefore already missed at the read, by the same margin and in the same way the dot products and the bf16 pair missed theirs, which is the third time this profile has read a fast-track plan against its own tags and found the plan ahead of the artifact.
