Neither arm is reached before R-15-116's margin is measured, and that measurement can still delete the extension outright, which is the cheaper outcome under §1's value function and is why it is worth taking before either arm is priced. What the read does change is the **standing question a re-pin must answer first**, now askable in the concrete: `Zvvm` holds matrix data in the vector registers, so its geometry is a function of the M class's declared VLEN, and whether [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail)'s separate matrix geometry survives that is the first thing the pin decides rather than the last.
      Three caveats ride that. The dates are targets rather than history, so the edition is read at the act that would pin it. An *integrated* extension makes the matrix geometry a function of the vector geometry, so IME over the M class's declared VLEN is a question against [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) and the first thing a re-pin must answer. And a ratified extension is not thereby an admitted one (R-15-007j), so the second act still admits the surface against R-15-116's margin and still enumerates what it admits.
      What this changes is the disposition rather than the schedule: if IME ratifies, the surface R-15-014a (ix) admits is a version pin rather than an authored encoding, and R-15-009's re-pin obligation acquires a target it can name instead of a lineage it can only watch.
      **The lineage is three tracks and this item's reading covered two.** Beside the attached AME and the integrated IME there is the vector-coupled **VME** (`Zvopmm`), whose matrix multiply takes two vector registers, computes their outer product and accumulates into a matrix accumulator register file, needing no two-dimensional matrix load and store surface and no major CSR bank of its own. R-15-009 now names the set rather than one track, and the third is the one this profile would pay least for, adding nothing against §5's closed CSR table and no load and store forms beside R-15-007e's, at the price of being the least free of the M class's declared VLEN. Which track a re-pin takes is decided at the re-pin, so the read this item owes at that act is over three plans and not two.
    * Net change: 576 lines added and 22 removed across ten files, one of them new.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **65 properties against 62**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys, unchanged**, the region class being a property of a list member rather than a key of the configuration tree; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, no configuration here declaring a scratchpad for it to carry; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 64 rules with every mutant killed; and both type checkers clean.
    * Ten findings.
      * **The profile books the matrix extension as a unit and names no instruction, so there is nothing here to admit.** Every other bespoke row in [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §3 names its instruction, operand form and encoding decision; the matrix row names a unit, a disposition and a re-pin target, and a construct the enumerations do not name is absent (R-15-007j). Three neighbouring decisions are taken and read as though they were this one: no architectural tile file, no block-scale register (R-15-117 keeping de-quantization in software), and outer-product sourcing recorded as an exploration question rather than an ISA commitment. All three are about what the extension does not **hold**, and a surface whose operands come from vector registers and memory needs none of it. What is left undecided is the mnemonic, the operand encoding and the opcode.
      * **The empty profile cell was the symptom and the missing sorting rule was the defect.** No Sail file carries a matrix instruction; what [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail) holds is geometry, a composition declaring how large a systolic array is rather than a machine knowing what it executes. What sorts the surface is R-15-116's admission against a **sustained dense-GEMM margin**, a measurement against generated output, so the surface belongs to the second act at (ix) rather than to an amendment. R-15-014b now states the missing rule: a unit booked without an instruction surface names the act that will book it, and one booked with neither is a review-gate finding. **So this half is out of scope rather than blocked**, and modelling the surface now would be the measurement-conditioned provisional row M0.6g's exit condition refuses.
      * **A scratchpad is a placement rather than a structure, which decides how much of one a Sail model can carry.** R-15-167 makes it plain memory at a fixed range with no fill, no eviction, no valid or tag array, and no lookup that is not the ordinary address decode, so it is declared beside every other property the composition fixes about a range. That leaves one irreducible new fact, *whose* it is, everything else being a refusal over a field the PMA already had: not coherent, not executable, not on a latency class. R-15-165's own test makes the thinness correct rather than lossy, the contents being a function of an explicit software placement and never of access history.
      * **It is on neither latency class, and stating that relaxes an M0.14 invariant rather than adding a third class.** R-15-247's two classes are two media of *main* memory and R-15-168 makes the scratchpad the local tier above them, so the validator's *every array is on exactly one class* becomes *every array that is not a scratchpad*. A third `MemoryClass` arm would misstate R-15-247's own count, move the figures K-60 holds, and give `memory_class_at` a value no latency constant stands behind.
      * **The matrix half of `vmclear`'s inventory is empty, an enumeration result rather than a milestone's residue.** M0.6h booked two absent members; one lands and the other turns out to be nothing to clear, since R-15-117 keeps de-quantization in software, the tile file is not imported, and R-15-118's coprocessor line leaves the unit no state of its own. What a partition can dirty is the scratchpad and nothing else.
      * **The scratchpad has no size, and no configuration in this tree declares one.** R-15-108 selects scratchpad sizes and the exploration has not run, which is what `frozen` is false for in every class row; a range written into a shipped configuration ahead of it is how a placeholder becomes an architectural input. So the model carries the shape, refuses a wrong one, and carries no range.
      * **The scratchpad is unreachable from the C-class golden emulator, so this item adds no corpus member and its harness installs its own address map.** The frozen emulator is the C class (M0.10) and R-15-165 puts the scratchpads on the V and M datapaths, so no corpus program can address one. The three new `$[test]` properties install a region above every region the configurations declare and restore what they found: a property about a structure no shipped configuration has is a property that must supply it.
      * **A new per-region property does not move the key-set gate, and schema conformance catches it instead.** `memory.regions` is a *list*, so a member's properties are not keys of the configuration tree and both declarations agree at 270 before and after; what bites is the schema, which grew the property and refuses a region without it in either file. An unmoved key count is therefore not evidence that nothing was added to a configuration.
      * **The build tree is one tree, and a second worktree's build fails at `configure` rather than at compile.** `cmake` records the source directory in its cache, so a lane building the canonical build directory from a different checkout is refused with a cache mismatch before a line is compiled. The lever needed no tool change, [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) already reading `VOS_BUILD_DIR` and `VOS_LOG_DIR`, and it is the mechanism by which two items that both write `model/` serialize.
      * **This item owes the checker no rule, because the fact it would hold is a model fact rather than a document one.** The one cross-artifact agreement it creates is between a class's declared `scratchpad_bytes` and the address map's declared range, both in the configuration the Sail validator reads, so the refusal fires on every run of every loop rather than on a document sweep. A checker rule over the same pair would be a second reader of one file.
  * [x] **M0.8d · FEC and Keccak units** · 11 h actual · 1.0%
    * The LDPC and polar decoders as fixed-geometry core-issued capability-operand instructions with deterministic iteration bounds and no firmware (R-15-119), and the frozen Keccak-f[1600] permutation as a single vector instruction on the vector-bearing classes (R-15-056, R-15-057, R-15-059), whose Sail semantics are fresh against FIPS 202 and the NIST ACVP vectors (R-15-058). Both are attachments to a class the roster already names, so what this item adds is surface and not parameterization.
    * **Both halves are landed and the profile names all three things it books for each of them.** [isa-profile.md](isa-profile.md) §3 carries the Keccak row's shape and re-pin target and now carries the FEC row's mnemonics, operand form and encoding beside them, so nothing on this item enters the profile by inheritance and no instruction here is surface a row does not name (R-15-007j, R-15-014b).
    * `vkeccak.vi` lands in [extensions/keccak/](../model/model/extensions/keccak/), its own directory because the fork is bespoke: everything in `extensions/vector_crypto/` beside it is a ratified extension with an upstream clause behind it and RVG-84 has not ratified, so the permutation and the instruction are authored here and diffed against the draft at each PQC Task Group milestone. [keccak_p1600.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_p1600.sail) is the permutation as a **pure function** and [keccak_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_insts.sail) is the element group, class placement, round-count immediate and encoding; the split is R-15-057a's own, the re-pin exchanging an encoding and leaving the semantics where they are.
    * **Both round counts are frozen and neither is added later** (R-15-056a). FIPS 202 makes Keccak-*p*[1600,12] the **last** twelve rounds of Keccak-*p*[1600,24], so it is a round range over one constant table rather than a second table. Both sit on the `Zvkt` fixed-latency list as two rows of the timing-annotated model (R-15-053), two rather than one because a single row scaled by the immediate would be a latency computed from a field of the instruction, the shape that table's signature exists to refuse. **The re-pin obligation is recorded at the foot of the instruction file** as two named deltas: the disposition of the 448 bits the 2048-bit element group carries above the 1600-bit state, written zero here, and the vector-length mandate.
    * **The seven custom-opcode rows still spend one opcode between them.** `vkeccak.vi` takes custom-0 `funct3` 010 between `vmclear`'s 001 and the indexed access's width-coded 011, and `ldpcdec` and `polardec` take 100 **together**, the code family riding `funct7`, so custom-1 and custom-3 stay whole and 101 and 110 stay unspent besides (R-15-014a, R-15-067e). A fork takes custom space rather than an unallocated point in the standard OP-V opcode deliberately: moving into space the standard allocates **is** the re-pin, so a reader checking this encoding against the opcode map finds an instruction where non-standard instructions belong and a stock disassembler prints nothing rather than the wrong thing (M0.6h's ground for `cbo.scrub`). The three RVV instructions the Keccak corpus member needs are **not** in the dialect table, being M0.8b's to add, so `keccak-perm` spells them out with their fields.
    * **The FEC surface is two mnemonics over one operand form**, `ldpcdec cd, cs1, rs2` and `polardec cd, cs1, rs2` in [extensions/fec/](../model/model/extensions/fec/) (R-15-119b): `cs1` authorises the soft-symbol block, one signed 8-bit log-likelihood ratio per received coded bit; `cd` authorises the hard-bit block and sits in the `rd` field read as a source the way `csd`'s does; and `rs2` packs the block descriptor, the soft and hard counts, the composition's index into its declared code set, and a reserved field. The split between [fec_codes.sail](../model/model/extensions/fec/fec_codes.sail), the shape a composition may declare, and [fec_insts.sail](../model/model/extensions/fec/fec_insts.sail), the surface, is R-15-119a's and the same division the Keccak fork makes.
    * **The invariance R-15-119a requires is a mechanism rather than a promise, and the mechanism is one word.** A descriptor names a code by **index** into what the composition declared, so a base graph, lifting size, reliability sequence and rate-matching schedule have no instruction field to reach and two builders holding two sets of TS 38.212's tables assemble the same words. It is asserted from both ends: a property encodes one instruction under three declared code sets and requires the three words to be equal, and [fec-surface.s](../corpus/fec-surface.s) reads each faulting word back out of the trap value and requires a changed descriptor register's *contents* to move no field of it.
    * **The gate is the composed roster entry's FEC attachment and nothing else.** What decides which predicate an instruction takes is whose instruction it is **and what state it touches**: `vmclear` is the switcher's and gates on the static property alone, `vkeccak.vi` is the partition's own and folds in `mstatus.VS` because it writes the vector register file. These are the partition's too and touch **no extension context at all**, their whole effect being memory reached through two explicit capability operands (R-15-118, R-08-003), so the static half is the whole gate, and it is the roster's rather than the class's because the decoders follow the statically pinned radio cores (R-12-038, R-15-114).
    * **The capability checks are the existing path and there is no second copy of one.** A code block runs past the memory interface's widest access, so the pass is a walk and each step goes through `data_read` or `data_write`, which compose `ext_data_get_addr` over `cap_data_checks`. What the two authorities buy is that a fault names the register whose authority was short: the soft block and the decoded block are different objects held by different code for different spans, and an in-place form would have let a decoder's write reach every byte its read covered.
    * Net change: 1,211 lines added and 8 removed across 14 files, four of them new, for the Keccak half; and 1,302 added and 51 removed across 23 files, four of them new, for the FEC surface.
    * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **84 properties against 77**, seven of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **290 keys against 267**; the differential corpus green at **25 of 25** with **233 checks**, one member and 9 checks new, the manifest at version 7, and every other member's digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green, the generated tree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at **4,890 bytes against 4,466** and accepted by the emulator's own bound against the region it is written into, the growth being the four FEC geometry properties on each of the five class nodes; each of the **eight** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration, with the unmutated composition and a well-formed FEC-bearing one both accepted; the checker green on all 72 rules with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green; the proof gate closing 19 constants; the co-read ledger whole; and both type checkers clean.
    * Eight findings.
      * **The gate is the class *and* the context, and M0.6h's answer is right for its instruction and wrong for this one.** `vmclear` gates on `hartSupports` and deliberately not on `currentlyEnabled`, being the **switcher's** instruction and not trappable by the partition it exists to erase. `vkeccak.vi` is the **partition's own** and writes the architectural vector register file exactly as `vsha2ms.vv` does, so it folds in `mstatus.VS`; gating it on the class alone would let a partition that has turned its own vector unit off write that file, deleting the extension-context gate rather than honouring it (R-07-012, R-15-049). What decides the predicate is whose instruction it is. The class half is the other conjunct, reading `keccak_unit` straight out of [core/core_class.sail](../model/model/core/core_class.sail).
      * **No row enters the extension registry, and that is the requirement rather than an omission.** `hartSupports` builds the generated `riscv,isa` string and `riscv,isa-extensions` array, so an `Ext_Zvknhk` row would have an **attested** artifact name a standards-track extension this machine does not implement, RVG-84 not having ratified. What the tree says instead is the vendor property M0.8a put on the class node. The re-pin adds the registry row, on the day the name means something.
      * **R-15-057a's `Zvl128b` mandate is unreachable, and the model is correct either way.** An element group of 2048 bits needs LMUL × VLEN ≥ 2048 and LMUL tops out at 8, so the effective floor is VLEN = 256 and no `vl` makes the instruction legal at 128. The mandate is therefore **not** written as a conjunct, a condition no configuration can fail being the dead arm M0.16 found; the geometry check is strictly stronger and the disagreement is booked as the second re-pin delta. That floor corroborates the placement, VLEN = 256 being the C class's own geometry and where R-15-059a states its figures.
      * **The two defects the known-answer vectors found were both transcriptions.** `keccak_mod5`'s wildcard swallowed twenty as well as nineteen, so *pi* sent lane (4, 4) to row 4 where the standard says row 0; and a Sail vector literal is `dec`-ordered, so the round-constant table was read backwards. Neither would have been found by reading the file. Worth recording: the property that **names** the first, that *pi* permutes the twenty-five lanes, was written before the vectors and never ran, the harness running alphabetically so the symptom aborted the executable ahead of the cause. A known-answer vector says *wrong* and a structural property says *why*, and the ordering between them is not a test file's to choose.
      * **Where this model stops on the FEC half is correction, for the reason `cbo.scrub` stops at the syndrome.** The pass computes the hard decision, the sign of each log-likelihood ratio, which every declared decoder geometry agrees on before a message passes; belief propagation above it corrects a *channel*, and this model has none. So the decoded word is the hard decision here and the corrected block on a machine with a channel, nothing changing but the value the pass computes. A clause computing one schedule's answer would state a **microarchitectural** choice as an architectural result, R-15-119a having put the decoder parallelism and polar list width in R-15-108's frozen set.
      * **The corpus found an encoding defect the harness structurally cannot, which is the argument for carrying both.** A hand-written `vse64.v` merged the `rs1` and `funct3` nibbles and named `c11` where it meant `c10`, an uninitialized register whose untagged value faulted the access. A `$[test]` calls `execute` on a decoded instruction and so never sees a mis-encoded word at all.
      * **A property that saves a list register and restores it by assigning the saved binding does not restore it, and only the harness's per-property reset hides that.** Reading the register it is restoring and dropping the head it prepended works. No run is wrong today, the leak never crossing a property boundary; it is recorded rather than quietly repaired because [test_matrix_scratchpad.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_matrix_scratchpad.sail) stands on the same idiom over `pma_regions`, correct as run and no longer correct the moment a property wants to observe its own restore.
      * **The one-directional refusal set is the point, and the symmetric one would have refused every composition in this tree.** A composition declaring codes with no hart carrying the decoders is refused, a channel code with no unit to decode it being a receiver with nowhere to run its physical layer. The converse is not refused: a hart carrying the units on a class that declares no geometry is R-15-108's exploration not having run, the state every configuration here is in. The same asymmetry makes the maximum-block refusal conditional on a geometry having been *selected*, a zero reading as *not selected* rather than as a zero-length datapath.
    * **The re-pin's clock is nearer than the fork's framing implies.** The PQC Task Group approved its development-complete milestone on 2026-07-13, and its ratification plan runs v0.6 on 2026-07-16, v0.8 stabilized on 2026-07-30, **v0.9 frozen on 2026-09-03**, public review from 2026-09-22, v0.99 on 2026-11-20 and v1.0 on 2026-12-31, scoped throughout at one opcode in standard encoding. R-15-057a's accept named the stabilization, the review and the ratification target and did not name the freeze, which is the milestone the two booked deltas change character at: before it a divergence may be the draft's next revision, after it a divergence is one of the two specifications being wrong. The accept now carries it.
      Two consequences strengthen this item's conservatism. The custom-0 encoding is a bridge with a stated end rather than an open-ended fork. And the task group carries **Sail model integration** among its deliverables, so the ratified semantics arrive as a second transcription of FIPS 202 against the one [keccak_p1600.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_p1600.sail) holds, making the pair a differential check at re-pin time rather than one transcription replacing another.
    * **What remains is neither half of this item.** The `Zvbb`/`Zvbc` software Keccak R-15-058 retains as the portable path is a software artifact rather than model surface and belongs with the code the toolchain milestone emits; and an FEC decode that *retires* is unreachable from this rig, the golden emulator being the C class and the reference composition attaching the decoders to the pinned radio V pair, so [`fec-surface`](../corpus/fec-surface.s) reaches the encoding and the refusal while the properties reach the pass. Both are booked where they belong ([differential-corpus.md](differential-corpus.md)).
    * **The FEC decoders are on the first release's critical path, and nothing holds them any longer.** R-18-004 carries the radio roster whole and puts FEC-unit bring-up (R-18-005) and carrier certification on the critical path to first parts; the single roster deferral is the browser. The roster question underneath is closed in the register: R-12-038 pins the PHY servers on the radio V-class cores with the FEC units, R-12-041 makes the cellular stack NR RRC and 5G-core NAS, and R-15-129 makes the FEC units the silicon half of the 5G/6G generation floor. A 5G NR physical layer is what that roster **is**, so the base graphs a certified receiver decodes are TS 38.212's own and a bespoke parameterization is not a substitute. This plan's order did not hold the surface back either: R-15-119 carries no measurement conditioning, so R-15-014b owed it at the provisional freeze, a specification act with **no build prerequisite**.
      **The licence call is taken and its arm is to publish table-free**: TS 38.212 is neither pinned nor vendored, and the read at its terms is that in-house copies for product design are carved out while reproduction in a conveyed artifact is not, so a base graph transcribed into a tracked source in a public repository is the one act the carve-out does not reach. The surface is authored **parameterized over the codes' parameters and fixing none of them**, the numbers arriving at composition from the copy the builder holds, so no normative table is tracked here and an auditor's check is a search of the tree rather than a reading of it. The forfeit is self-containment, stated rather than absorbed: the tree alone does not compose an FEC-bearing image, the reproducible-build story is narrower for that component, and every builder takes the 3GPP read themselves. **The permission arm stays open, and it now carries a trigger rather than a later**: it composes with this one rather than replacing it, and seeking 3GPP's own copyright form is taken at the first act that would ship an FEC-bearing image rather than model one, which is where the forfeit starts to bite and is behind the M8a gate and behind the deferred application program (R-18-004). Naming the trigger is the whole of the act: an arm that stays open with no condition attached is the deferral without a trigger this document refuses elsewhere, and the forfeit costs nothing until a builder other than this one has to compose the image.
      Two consequences reach past this item. **The register states the parameter and not its value**, the shape R-15-247p's bank count and R-15-014a (vii)'s block size already take, the difference being only that the value is owed to a builder's copy rather than to a measurement; the composition files take the same shape from the other end, declaring the *shape* of a code set and an empty list of them. And **TS 38.331 rides the same arm**, the RRC state machines being owed by R-05-048 and R-18-029 against the same instrument's terms, so the radio reference machines are authored against procedures rather than transcribing normative tables into the tree. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries no 3GPP entry in any of its inventory tables.
    * **The decoder mathematics have a Rocq start-from, and it supplies neither of the two things blocking this item.** `affeldt-aist/infotheo` is a Rocq formalization of information theory and linear error-correcting codes over MathComp, carrying **LDPC** with encoders and decoders beside Hamming, Reed-Solomon and BCH, distributed on opam. It fixes no base graph and no lifting size, which is what a start-from on this arm may not do; where it bears is one layer under both, on the decoder mathematics the geometry would eventually parameterize, a Coq-native reference this repository would otherwise author from the literature. Three caveats: its LDPC development is the *acyclic* case rather than the protograph-lifted codes a 5G-class decoder uses; a formalization of a code is not an implementation of a decoder, still less a fixed-geometry deterministic-iteration-bound one; and its terms are unread here, as every start-from's are until the milestone that would incorporate it reads them.
* [x] **M0.9 · Add documented timing annotations** · 5 h actual · 0.5%
  * The annotated layer lands as one table of operation classes in [core/timing.sail](../model/model/core/timing.sail): the profile's §9 contracts as latency rows, the two partition-switch constants that are per core class, the relation R-15-085b states between the on-list and off-list vector accesses, and `qualified` beside all of it. The shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)), and nothing the machine executes reads any of it: the model runs no cycles, so this is documentation the tools hold rather than behaviour they check.
  * **K-63** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) is what the item turned out to owe, and it is not the rule it set out to owe. It holds every requirement id the model files this tool can read cite against the register, 166 of them across five files, which is a class of citation that reached no rule at all before: `model/` is outside the checker's corpus wholesale and K-11 reads only tracked documents, so a mistyped id in a Sail comment rendered as an argument and pointed at nothing.
  * Net change: 679 lines added and 17 removed across 13 files, one of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **62 properties against 59**, three of them new; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at **270 keys against 228**; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green and the tree unmoved at 4,466 bytes, the timing table deliberately not being in it; each of the **six** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on **all 64 rules against 63**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    * **The contract is the signature rather than a comment beside it.** `timing_cycles` takes an operation class and no operand, register, address or prior state, so there is nowhere for a value to enter it. That is what data-independent latency looks like in a model that executes no cycles (R-15-053, R-15-095): a future consumer wanting an operand shows up as a changed signature rather than as changed arithmetic.
    * **The scalar table is one table because the front end is one front end.** Scalar front ends are one shared microarchitecture across every class, which is what makes kernel-path WCET a single analysis (R-15-112), so a per-class scalar latency would be a second front end stated by arithmetic. Two rows *are* per class, the eager zeroize and the padded fence, not because their cost varies but because one kernel binary budgets a partition switch on every class it may run on (R-07-012, R-15-220).
    * **The table is deliberately not in the attested devicetree, and R-09-007 is why.** That requirement enumerates core classes, islands, the NoC schedule, OPP tables, and radio calibration and limit values, and a latency is none of them: these constants are inputs to an off-device WCET derivation rather than values a booting kernel reads (R-11-015, R-18-024).
    * **The crown-jewel row does not move, and `qualified` is what keeps that honest.** R-17-041 makes the latency *magnitudes* the crown-jewel specification and not one is measured: `Zicntr` and `Zihpm` are implemented in no lifecycle state so nothing here can read a cycle (R-15-077), development measurement rides lifecycle-gated Debug Module trace on real RTL, and the freeze contract refuses a host-timed column. What landed is the table those magnitudes will be written into; what would earn the flip is a total map from every instruction to its class.
    * **The membership rule this item set out to owe cannot be written without becoming the defect it would catch.** 11 register entries say an operation is one entry in the timing-annotated model, and holding that set against the table needs a mapping from each entry's prose to a class member, a third copy of the correspondence; a body-pattern selection returns two of the 11, the register stating one obligation in five wordings. So membership stays a person's, resting on the profile's §9 table K-14 already holds.
    * **The gap nobody had named is that the model's own citations reached no rule at all.** The curated model argues from the register in every file and not one of those citations was checked. K-63 closes it. **What its window is has since moved and the instruction here moves with it**: the rule reaches by kind, through `corpus.is_model_citation_path`, rather than through the `MODEL_FACTS` value window this item wrote it against, because a window sized for the other purpose left it reporting `ok` about a quarter of its subject. So widening K-63 costs nothing on that list, and `MODEL_FACTS`, now ten paths rather than five, is the separate value window K-57 and K-79 read.
* [x] **M0.10 · Generate and freeze the C-class golden emulator** · 2 h actual · 0.2%
  * The emulator is generated and frozen as the executable ISA reference, and what freezing it took was giving it an identity: [cmake/project_version.cmake](../model/cmake/project_version.cmake) now runs `git describe` in the tree it describes rather than in the build directory, so the binary carries the model revision it was generated from, and `model.py reference` prints that revision beside the compiler that emitted it and the two corpora that say what it does. Everything downstream is stated against a particular model, and until now none of them could name which.
  * Exit: curated single-core emulator with ISA tests green, at revision `abcc685`, Sail 0.20.2, **62 properties** and **21 corpus members carrying 193 checks over 5,231 commit-trace records**.
  * Net change: 101 lines added and 13 removed across four files.
  * Exit evidence: build green; `model.py reference` printing a known revision and exiting 0; the model's own `$[test]` harness at 62 properties; the differential corpus green at 21 of 21 with every digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green at 4,466 bytes; the checker green on all 64 rules with every mutant killed; and both type checkers clean.
  * Four findings.
    * **The reference could not name itself, and nothing said so.** `execute_process` runs in the current binary directory unless told otherwise, and an out-of-tree build directory is not a repository, so `git describe` exited 128 and every emulator this tree ever built stamped itself `unknown commit`. That is cosmetic upstream, where builds are in-tree; it is freeze-blocking here, the freeze contract requiring a report to carry the Sail model revision its cycle columns came from.
    * **The exit criterion had to be re-read, because the suite it names is one this machine cannot run.** M0.6f excluded `riscv-tests` outright, so *ISA tests green* as written names a corpus retired two items before this one entered. Its replacement is this repository's own pair, the `$[test]` harness and the differential corpus, and `reference` prints both beside the revision: a reference's identity is what it is **and** what it has been shown to do.
    * **The stamp is set at configure time and the working tree decides it.** `model.py build` reconfigures on every run, so the revision is never stale, but an emulator built from a tree under edit carries `-dirty` and only one built from a clean checkout names something a third party could reproduce. `reference` prints the dirty marker rather than refusing it, a gate that failed on an edited tree being a gate that gets turned off; it refuses an **unknown** revision, the state this item closed.
    * **`--version` still reports upstream's release number and that is right rather than residue.** 0.13.1 is a true fact about the lineage this tree was curated from, and the model's own identity is the revision `--build-info` carries beside it. Changing it would diverge from upstream inside the one file every reconciliation reads, to say what two other commands already say.
* [x] **M0.11 · Define the profile-freeze measurement contract** · 2.5 h actual · 0.2%
  * [docs/freeze-measurement-contract.md](freeze-measurement-contract.md): six corpus members (`FM-1` to `FM-6`), a seven-step composition recipe, five operand classes and five admitted region classes with four enumerated refusal reasons, nine decisions (`FD-1` to `FD-9`) each carrying question, corpus, unit, procedure, threshold, default, and report columns, one report in two renderings generated from one record, eight declared parameters, and twelve CI predicates (`G-1` to `G-12`). Registered as a view in `tools/check.py`, so the requirements it must carry are checked in both directions.
  * Three methodological rules do the work, and each closes a way the measurement could be true and useless: bytes are **measured** on the encoder's output with R-15-036h/j reported beside them as a residual check rather than in place of them; the dictionary is **re-selected inside every variant**, a candidate instrument changing the histogram it would be scored against; and a **closing pass** at the admitted configuration produces the realized dictionary, so the frozen one is the machine's and not the sweep's.
  * Thresholds are derived where the register fixes the quantity and declared where it does not. Derived: 22.4 encoded bits per instruction, the optimistic `C` counterfactual at 70% of a 32-bit stream, which is R-15-036k's *p* = 0.804 expressed through the axis actually observed; **zero slot widenings** for outlining, since a partition's capacity is its slot width (R-07-032, R-07-037, R-15-036p); and for `rcstep`, that it move at least one tuple from refused to admitted, a decode ceiling being refused above rather than degraded through (R-15-238c). Declared, and collected in one table: two materiality floors keyed on what the choice costs, 0.5% of encoded text where it consumes custom opcode space and 0.1% where it only selects among forms of admitted instructions.
  * The ordered act carries one nil row and one pending row. The single-check multi-register save/restore is struck ahead of the freeze (R-15-036n), so a measured row against it is an amendment rather than a freeze (`G-5`). Of the two rows R-15-014a's closed delta adds, R-15-067e's further code-size candidate and R-15-031a's fusion set, the second is the one whose admission is the deferred §15 exploration's, so the instrument owes it the emitted adjacency histogram and the report carries it as pending.

The transport is the preserved RVFI plumbing, a second callbacks class in the slot `rvfi_callbacks` occupies, emitted under `--trace-commit`; and the packet itself is widened where it can be, `rvfi_wX` carrying the destination's tag and the memory masks carrying one bit above the byte mask, which closes the two `TODO`s the tree carried and is what the RTL's `rvfi` port needs (§11).

    * **A fetch of an instruction wholly inside PCC's bounds raised a length violation**, which `cap-control`'s bounded sentry entry found on its first run. The profile excludes `C` and fixes ILEN at 32, so `min_instruction_bytes` is four; it does not adopt `Ziccif`, so a fetch still splits into two 16-bit granules; and the per-granule bounds check asked for a whole instruction from the *second* granule, two bytes past the instruction. A capability bounded exactly to the end of its last instruction was therefore unusable as an entry point, which is the shape R-15-071's bounded domain entry takes. The check now takes the width its caller reads, and two properties in [test_capability.sail](../model/model/unit_tests/test_capability.sail) hold both halves.
    * **The store-side root arrives in the link register**: reset hands `c1` the root data capability and `c1` is `ra`, so a program that calls overwrites its own authority.   * The two classes land as composition-time declarations and nothing else: [core/memory_class.sail](../model/model/core/memory_class.sail) carries the enumeration and the per-class record, the placement is a PMA beside every other property the composition fixes about an address range ([sys/pma.sail](../model/model/sys/pma.sail)), the constants enter the attested devicetree R-15-247m puts them in ([postlude/device_tree.sail](../model/model/postlude/device_tree.sail)), and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried ([postlude/validate_config.sail](../model/model/postlude/validate_config.sail)). **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface** (R-15-247h, [absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17): no access path calls the class query at all, a machine consulting the class at run time being the hierarchy R-15-247 refuses.
  * The record is eight fields per class, `qualified` beside seven numbers: usable density, bank count, the retention corner as a floor and a ceiling, and the four latency constants. Both configurations declare a second-class region beside the first-class one, at 8 GiB above the existing RAM, so both classes are live at distinct constants in the profile's own configuration and in the generated max configuration alike.

  * Five findings.
    * **The record is symmetric and the machine is not, and the validator is where the difference is held.** R-15-247m names the same four groups per class, so the record is one shape read twice; R-15-247n makes the first class latched and the second charge-storing with no restoring feedback, so three fields have no meaning on the first class. They are declared zero there under the model's zero-is-absent idiom and the validator refuses anything else, rather than leaving a composition free to declare a refresh deadline over a latched array.
    * **The retention corner is two fields because only one of them is ever read.** R-15-247m states it as a measured interval rather than a point, and R-15-247c consumes retention by the refresh deadline alone, so a mean with a tolerance would invite the ceiling into a guarantee. A floor and a ceiling with one consumer named in one function is that requirement realized: a second consumer shows up as a new call site rather than as new arithmetic.
    * **There is no separate fetch constant to declare.** R-15-247j prices second-class code against "that class's fetch constant", and with R-15-164 deleting every cache there is no instruction cache and nothing to amortize, so a fetch is a read of whichever class the code resides on. The delta the placement rule makes admission-visible is the difference of two numbers the record already carries, computed by the static memory plan (M1.9).
    * **A placeholder nothing distinguishes from a measurement is how it becomes an input, so the qualification state is attested.** R-15-247m admits no density figure as an architectural input ahead of the measurement, and bring-up needs numbers before R5 has any. `qualified` is carried in the record and emitted into the devicetree, false in every configuration here, so a tree says on its face which it holds. No property asserts its value: a test fixing it would be a test about the calendar.
    * **The corpus member is a set of claims that nothing is observable, which is what makes it worth running.** R-15-247s puts no trust gradient on the boundary, so [platform-memclass.s](../corpus/platform-memclass.s) checks that a capability round trip, the tag a data store clears, `cloadtags`, `cbo.zero`, `cbo.scrub`, an atomic and a bounds violation all answer the same on the second class as on the first, and that the revocation filter is keyed by the loaded capability's base rather than by the class of the slot holding it. The real difference is a latency constant, invisible from inside the machine and exactly the quantity the memory plan charges placement against.

A prohibition over the corpus was tried and is the wrong instrument, firing on a wired link rate and on scanout's framebuffer draw. So K-55 holds the three bookings and the comparison arm opens when M0.8 and M1 land the operands.
    * **The tag plane is one field read five ways, which is why it is one rule rather than five claims.** A granule that moved would leave every spelling individually plausible and jointly wrong, the state no reader detects. K-56 therefore takes the charge as its enumeration, and a byte the composition pays for that neither list nor that clause accounts for is a finding rather than a silence.

  * The sequencer lands as §12 matter and nothing else: [sys/memory_sequencer.sail](../model/model/sys/memory_sequencer.sail) carries the composition-fixed cadence table, the bank-staggered phases, the mode-exit discharge path (R-15-247q), and the fail-stop read taken once after a fixed dwell (R-15-247f); the register slave's window enters the address map in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail) and **answers no requester, at any width, in either direction**; the cadence enters `platform_config` and the attested devicetree beside the class constants M0.14 put there; and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried. **No instruction, no access type, and nothing on the decode surface** (R-15-247h, [absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17), and nothing in the sequencer reads `mtime`, a counter, an occupancy figure, an access record, or a temperature, which is R-15-247r held by what these functions take rather than by a rule about them.
  * Net change: 963 lines added and 14 removed across 12 files, two of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; `verifiedos.json` validating against the regenerated schema with its key set agreeing with the generated max configuration at 166 keys, seven of them new; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **52 properties against 48**, four of them new; the differential corpus green at **19 of 19** with one member and 8 checks new and the eighteen existing digests unmoved; the generated devicetree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at 2,733 bytes, inside the 4 KiB ROM region it is written to; each of the twelve new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration and the unmutated profile accepted; the checker green on all 54 rules with every mutant killed; the proof gate closing 19 constants; and both type checkers clean.
  * Six findings.
    * **The discharge needed no mechanism of its own, which is R-15-247e read as a model.** The requirement realizes the drain through the cells' *existing* write devices, and this model's ordinary write path is those devices, a write through it already clearing the validity tag of every granule it covers (R-15-007r). So the exit-path discharge is a **schedule over the write path**, adding no instruction, no access type and no second way to reach the array, which keeps A-17 an auditor's search of the decode surface. It lands where R-15-182 and R-15-060 already put an all-zeroes granule, which is why R-15-189j's OFF→ON regeneration pass is obviated rather than scaled to capacity.
    * **A window that refuses and a window that is absent are different facts, and only the first can be stated by claiming the address.** An IO address no device claims falls through to the RAM path and reads as zero, so a slave left unmapped is silent rather than refusing and *no requester-visible behaviour outside the mode transition* would have nothing behind it. Claiming the window and answering every access with a fail-stop is what a program can run into, and [platform-memseq.s](../corpus/platform-memseq.s) runs into it one page from the contrast.
    * **Refresh and discharge are modeled differently on purpose, only one of them having architectural content to execute.** A refresh restores a charge this model does not represent and disturbs nothing observable, so what it can get wrong is the cadence, and the model carries the deadline arithmetic and a refusal. The crossing direction is not taste: the retention floor is multiplied up into cycles rather than the sweep divided down into microseconds, which needs no runtime divisor and rounds nothing, where the other direction could round a cadence into a compliance it does not have.
    * **The unit of work is the bank because the walk over the banks is R-15-198's**, power and reset sequencing being one composition-time sequence table executed by the RoT firmware as the only sequencer. That also keeps the exit path a property rather than a measurement: a composed bulk domain is gigabytes, and a whole-domain pass is a run of the emulator rather than something a `$[test]` asserts.
    * **The fail-stop's negative reading has no source in a valid composition, and that is stated rather than papered over.** The completion indication is the pass's own answer, negative when a bank's write is refused or a phase reaches no bank, and this model has no charge to leave behind, so a valid configuration always confirms. What the model holds is the **shape**, read once after a fixed worst-corner dwell with no poll and no retry, over a real architectural latch rather than a fault-injection register. A machine that can leave charge behind joins that read as a case rather than as a mechanism.
    * **One validator arm was dead the moment it was written, and running the seeded refusals is what found that.** `config_is_valid` short-circuits and the class record's own check refuses a zero bank count first, so a second message saying the same thing is a branch no configuration reaches; that arm has to be the arithmetic's precondition, an early return rather than a refusal. Generally: a validator group placed after another inherits what that one already refuses, so a message it repeats is unreachable rather than redundant.

Drop `c` and it reaches `fmv.w.x` in `crt0.S`, illegal because the `f0`-`f31` file left at c4 (R-15-039). Drop that and it reaches `csrw pmpaddr0`, `mcause` 2 against an unallocated CSR address (R-15-075, R-15-014). Underneath all three the recovery path is already gone: `la t0, 1f` assembles to `auipcc` plus `addi`, so the first instruction yields a PCC-derived capability and the second **untags it**, `csrw mtvec` installs an untagged `MTCC`, and the first trap through it faults `mcause` 28 `mtval` 0x402. Every run ends `possible trap loop detected`, the set's own escape hatch for an unsupported PMP being itself unreachable here.
  * **So no `-march` string and no curated subset revives it, and that decides the arm.** Keeping the three vector tests would keep programs that cannot reach their own trap handler, stack, or HTIF exit door: `reset_handler` opens by writing integer zero over all thirty-one registers, `ra` first, which is `c1` and the store-side root reset hands it (R-15-007p), so the prologue disarms the program before `main`. What the set needs is M1.4's purecap toolchain, whose `la` derives a capability off PCC rather than materializing an integer, as the corpus assembler already does. **This item closes the staleness and does not claim the dormancy**: reviving the lane is gated on M1.2 and M1.4.
  * **`encoding.h` is deleted rather than curated or generated.** A surgical edit inside a 5,380-line vendored header carrying 3,481 defines closes nothing and costs the permanent vendored divergence [I4](#build-loop-instruments)'s second clause and [I8](#build-loop-instruments) both refuse. Generating it from the model's decode surface is worse, being a second copy of what [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py) already transcribes from the `encdec` clauses, generated for a consumer that reads **three** of the 3,481 defines. Deleting the file leaves nothing to reconcile at the next pin and nothing for the absence contract's auditor to hit.
  * **Two third-party components leave what this repository redistributes**, the RISC-V encoding header and nanoprintf 0.8.0, and the paragraph resolving their position outside [model/LICENCE](../model/LICENCE)'s `dependencies` carve-out goes with them: no component now states its terms in a source-file header instead of a licence file beside it.
  * **K-80** in the [links](../tools/vos/checks/links.py) group is what the deletion owed. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) discharges its notice obligations by naming the file each component's terms arrive in, stated as a backticked path wherever the file is not one a sentence sends a reader to; K-12 reads links and a path in backticks is text, so the vendored table was unheld and deleting a licence file would leave a page claiming to redistribute terms the tree no longer carries with every rule green. Membership is the **index** rather than the filesystem, which separates the claim from K-12's, and the reading is fail-closed four ways.
  * Net change: 9,227 lines removed and 170 added across 33 files. Twenty-three are deleted outright and carry 9,196 of the removals; the other ten are the files that named the harness and the three that hold the new rule.
  * Exit evidence: build green; **ctest unmoved at 2 of 2**, which is the measurement that says what the option cost, `FIRST_PARTY_TESTS` being off so the tree contributed no registered test to remove; the model's own `$[test]` harness unmoved at **84 properties**; the profile sweep unchanged at **199 refusals of 199**; the differential corpus green at **25 of 25** with 234 checks over 8,489 records, manifest version 8, every trace digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` unmoved at 4,890 bytes; `model.py reference` green; the checker green on **all 77 rules against 76**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the floors group's uncounted enumerations at **33 against 32**; and both type checkers clean.
  * Three findings.
    * **The RV32 half was broken at build rather than dormant, and no gate could see it because the option is off.** The retired `first_party/CMakeLists.txt` registered every test twice and asked cmake for `config/rv32d_v256_e32.json`, while [model/config/CMakeLists.txt](../model/config/CMakeLists.txt) generates the `rv64d_v${vlen}_e${elen}` stem alone. Half the harness therefore depended on a file no rule produces, so `-DFIRST_PARTY_TESTS=ON` failed at `configure` rather than at a test, and the XLEN loop was removed here rather than upstream.
    * **What the deletion costs the checker is a sixth of what it reads, and its subject does not move at all.** `corpus.is_model_citation_path` admits `.h`, `.c`, `.json` and `CMakeLists.txt`, so K-63 read those twelve files and every sandbox copied them: the window falls from **214 files and 1,873,537 bytes to 202 and 1,562,634**, `encoding.h` alone being 201,590 of the 310,903 removed, while K-63's own figure is **unchanged at 1,271 citations across 88 files**, none of the twelve carrying a requirement id. A rule whose cost falls by 17% while its subject is untouched is the shape of a window admitting by kind rather than by name.
    * **`model/.github/` stayed byte-identical to its pin here and I8's enumeration grew by one, and S3 has since answered it: the tree is deleted.** Six of `ci.yml`'s eight matrix rows passed `-DFIRST_PARTY_TESTS=true` to a build that declares no such option, joining `generated_smt_rv32d` as a second reason those workflows could not run if they ever ran. Editing the workflow would have been the permanent vendored divergence I4 and I8 both refuse, which is why this batch moved that item's prose rather than the file; deleting the tree whole is the act neither refuses, and it is S3's.

**M0 subtotal:** 118.6 h · 11% · 113.6 h complete · open range 3–7 h.

### M1 · Toolchain spine (incl. the CHERI-CompCert prerequisite)

Build the *functional* CHERI-RISC-V CompCert backend **first** (no purecap CertiCoq GC is needed, the on-device path is GC-free, §0), then bring up both roles, the host-side **CertiCoq → Wasm** oracle and the **GC-free on-device lowering** (CompCert-C/VST through CHERI-CompCert; arena extraction via MetaCoq→Rust onto the Rust→CHERI compiler for the allocation-light components), producing runnable purecap artifacts from a trivial Gallina program on the M0 emulator.
Ship the frozen-dialect **assembler, linker, and image composer** (§0) in the same milestone; nothing downstream links without them. The bring-up realization of that sentence is M1.4′, the in-tree trio M0.12 already proved on the corpus, with M1.4's LLVM re-homing its hardening replacement behind the gate.
Build the profile-freeze analyzer (M1.8a, landed) and, behind the M8a gate, the backend provenance outputs and the ordered dictionary, outlining, operand-form, bitfield, stack-save, and indexed-address reports against the generated corpus: the freeze decides a profile nothing before the gate is compiled against, R-15-014a having made the provisional one a total compilation target.
Every later milestone is purecap and managed-runtime-free from here.

  * Condition: alive, not abandoned (pushed June 2026; blame merged March 2026). Compartment-aware CompCert holds through every pass with the correctness proof adapted (admits confined to `Stackingproof.v` pending recent changes); recomposition and blame proofs complete; back-translation complete on `ccs-backtranslation`; the top-level RSCC theorem is formalized in `security/RSC.v` but not yet integrated with those three. States Rocq 9.1 / OCaml 5.2.1 / Menhir as its requirement, which is a requirement rather than a measurement: what the pin builds under on this toolchain is M1.2's.

Branch `fix-cap-backend` (`1881ed0f`, June 2024) is 15 commits of unmerged backend work ahead; the 2022 `cheri-backend` branch is superseded.
  * Implication for M1.2, where the usability claim is measured rather than inferred and the contingency is answered: the re-homing's real gap is the unverified, unintegrated capability backend and its ISAv8-lineage capability model versus the §4.1 64+1-bit purecap dialect, exactly the residual [inspirations.md](inspirations.md) states.

M1.2 is the act that spends it, so the call is taken here.

That attaches to VST's *concurrency* path, which §7's L2 linearizability and §5's per-core kernel could reach for. It is a clause of this call rather than a call of its own, adding nothing under **obtain terms** or **contain it** and becoming a second route back to the same licence only under **route around it**. Acquire VST through the opam distribution, which does not convey it.
