OCaml is 5.4.0 against a 4.05-to-4.14 test, which is what `-ignore-ocaml-version` is for. And `make proof` alone fails on a clean tree, the `%.vo: %.v` rule resolving its stem through `VPATH` only once `.depend` exists.
  * **`-dcapasm` emits, and what it emits is what bounds this item.** The flag is wired through `Clflags`, `Driver` and extraction's `print_CapAsm`, and on FP-free C it writes a Gallina program term rather than assembler text. Straight-line register-only code emits clean and nothing past it does: one `int` loop emits a `TODO: __Pbgew__`, a pointer-indexed loop seven such tokens, a struct field access eight, the missing mnemonics being the ordinary word loads and stores through a capability and the comparisons beside them. The pretty-printer is where this sits, and the denominator is the declaration rather than the file: `CapAsm.v` declares **258** instruction constructors (lines 233 to 578), where the 479 `| P…` lines the file carries are those plus every `exec_instr` arm, which is the reading trap. Against 258, **228** have a live arm in `backend/PrintCapAsm.ml`'s 748-line match, **202** of them printing a `TODO: __<mnemonic>__` literal and **26** a term, while the other **30** are double-precision forms with no arm at all, the half of the surface this profile deletes in its own right (R-15-039). That is M1.1's "admittedly incomplete pretty-printer" measured, and the incompleteness is the common case rather than the corner.
  * **The correctness proofs are outside the build and every admit is inside it.** `SECARCH` names 14 of the 33 files; the other 19 are not compiled, six of them the `*proof.v` files. Every `Admitted.` and `admit.` in the directory falls in the 14 that are: **35 `Admitted.` and 10 `admit.`**, in `CapMemory.v` (21 and 7), `OCapValues.v` (10), `CapAsmgen.v` (3) and `CapGlobalenvs.v` (1 and 3). What builds is the capability backend's definitions with its correctness argument stubbed.
  * **The step relation is empty at the pin, which is sharper than the admits and decides what the re-homing starts from.** `CapAsm.v:1845` reads `Definition executeAllowedCap (c : occap) : bool := false.` with a `FIXME` beside it, and that constant gates all five step rules (`:1851`, `:1865`, `:1905`, `:1933`, `:1949`), so **no `step` is derivable and the target language's transition relation is vacuous**. There is no `semantics` record either, the definition that would plug `CapAsm` into CompCert's simulation framework being commented out at `:2052` under a `FIXME priority 1`, which is why the six `*proof.v` files sit outside `SECARCH`. Five capability instructions are declared with their semantics commented out: `PCgt` (`:1369`), `PCgs` (`:1381`), `PCseal_e` (`:1428`), `PCCseal` (`:1424`) and `PCinvoke` (`:1176`), and `CapAsmgen.v:1434` emits `PCgs` as `make_return`'s cross-compartment discriminator, so the generated return path names an instruction the machine does not define. The re-homing therefore starts from a design sketch whose definitions compile rather than from a working translation whose parameters move.
  * **The re-homing delta, and the first thing it settles is the shape of the problem.** `cheririscV/` fixes no 128-bit capability *layout*: the capability is abstract in the Cerise lineage, `OCVmem : permission -> locality -> ptrofs -> ptrofs -> ptrofs -> ocsealable` (`OCapValues.v:262`), carrying base, top and address as three full-width `ptrofs` with no exponent, mantissa or compression anywhere in the directory, so the abstract state is three words wide where the footprint is two. There is accordingly no `cheri_compressed_cap`-equivalent algebra to re-parameterize, and the 128 bits enter only as a *footprint*: a chunk's size and alignment (`CapAST.v:70-71`, `CapMemdata.v:47`, `:109`), a typed slot's count (`CapLocations.v:48-50`), and the sixteen-byte `Q128` (`CapMemdata.v:146`, `:666-669`) whose all-or-nothing fragment check is this model's surrogate for the validity tag. What has to move is the eight axes below rather than field widths.
  * **Axis 1, the capability's storage width, is the one this lane moved, and what it measures is where a re-parameterization's cost actually sits.** Upstream welds CLEN to twice XLEN by reading the capability's width off `Archi.ptr64` (`CapAST.v:57`, `:58`, `:171`), with no `Archi.capwidth` beside it, so a 64-bit machine admits only a 128-bit capability. The lane adds `CapAST.cap128` as a width switch of its own, sets it false, and repoints the **35** sites that select a capability's width at it over six files, `CapAST.v`, `CapMemdata.v`, `CapGlobalenvs.v`, `OCapValues.v`, `CapMachregs.v` and `CapAsmgen.v`, leaving `Archi.ptr64` to mean the pointer width alone. `CTcap` and `CMcap` then resolve to the already-present 64-bit forms, so a capability is 8 bytes at 8-byte alignment, one typed slot and `Q64`'s eight fragments, which is [cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail)'s `cap_size` exactly, and `CapAsmgen`'s `opldc`, `opsc` and `opUsc` select `PClwc`, `PCscw` and `PCUscw` where they selected the 128-bit forms. Three of the 35 are unreachable by search and were found by a failing proof: the `OCVcap`-against-`CTany64` arms of `has_type`, `normalize` and `load_result` (`OCapValues.v:550`, `:1724`, `:1790`) name no capability constructor, so no grep for one finds them.
  * **Axis 1 is landed, against a clean tree rather than an incremental one:** `make clean` and the full sequence put all fourteen `SECARCH` files and the 203 `.vo` back at exit 0, `make proof` at 21 min 37 s and 1.9 GB peak, extraction and the link green on top. What it cost is the finding: the definitions move mechanically and the proofs do not. Upstream discharges every capability case by splitting on `Archi.ptr64`, so each split now needs the second boolean beside it, **fourteen lines of tactic repair over two files**, in `normalize_type`, `normalize_idem` and `load_result_rettype` (`OCapValues.v`) and four lemmas in `CapMemdata.v`. Two of those selected residual goals **by index** and the wider split moves them, so both are rewritten to walk the disjunction, `repeat first [ left; reflexivity | right ]`, which survives a further split rather than needing the indices recounted. One `Section Examples` goal drops a `rewrite ARCHI` that has no subterm left and was never load-bearing. **The change reaches the output**: on a C program that spills a capability the emitted term carries `PClwc` twelve times where the pin carried `PClcd`, and the stubbed-arm census moves with it, 52 tokens over 2 constructors becoming 64 over 3, the printer's 26 real arms having been written against the 128-bit selection. On a program that loads no capability the term is byte-identical, which is the honest scope of the demonstration. Two things the axis deliberately does not move: the argument-passing layout (`CapConventions1.v:234`, `:258` advancing an outgoing offset by a hard-coded two slots per argument), which is the ABI rather than the width; and `CapValues.v`, which carries the same three tables outside `SECARCH`, so repointing it would repoint something nothing builds.
  * **Axis 7 is not the cheap deletion it reads as, and the reason is recorded so it is not rediscovered.** `X32` is a **second reserved scratch** rather than a spare: `CapAsm.v:1670` states that no LTL register maps to `X31` or `X32`, and `CapAsmgen.v:919`, `:921` and `:928` use the pair together in the stack-offset sequences (`PCgl_s X32 SPC :: … :: Psubl X31 X32 X31`). Deleting the thirty-third register, which no five-bit field can encode, therefore needs a second scratch found inside 32, which is the ABI question axis 1 already declines to answer rather than a deletion. `DDC` is load-bearing for the same kind of reason: `CapAsmgen.v:1352` and `:1392` load each compartment's heap capability into it across the compartment switch, so removing it is entangled with a heap model this dialect does not have at all (R-08-011). The scalar floating-point file is the only genuinely mechanical part of the axis. What that moves is the order rather than the total: axis 7 is not the cheap one to take first.
  * **Axis 2, the permission field, is a replacement and not a narrowing.** SECOMP's is a twelve-element abstract enumeration `O | RO | RW | RWL | RX | E | RWX | RWLX | URW | URWL | URWX | URWLX` (`OCapValues.v:39-51`) ordered by a hand-written `permFlowsTo` (`:93-140`), crossed with a three-valued locality `Global | Local | Directed` (`:57-60`) under its own `locFlowsTo` (`:65-76`). The profile's is five bits naming one of 32 codepoints, sixteen authority shapes crossed with one global bit, decoded by a total `perms_expand` into a twelve-bit architectural bitmap and narrowed by `perms_narrow`'s largest-admitted-subset search ([cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail), lines 88-184). Neither order embeds in the other: the profile carries access-system-registers, the `load-global`/`load-mutable` transitivity pair, `store-capability`/`store-local-capability`, and a `Permit_Seal`/`Permit_Unseal` split no admitted set holds together (R-15-007o), none of which SECOMP has, and SECOMP carries four uninitialized permissions and a `Directed` locality the profile has none of. Locality does not survive as a field at all, `store-local` being bit 5 of the profile's expanded bitmap and riding the permission code (R-15-074), so the `locality` type and its uses across six compiled files go with it. The two together are read on 167 lines of the fourteen compiled files.
  * **Axis 3, uninitialized capabilities, is the axis the calling convention rests on, so deleting it is a design act rather than a deletion.** `URW`/`URWL`/`URWX`/`URWLX` (`OCapValues.v:48-51`) are neither read- nor write-allowed (`:190-200`); a `U` capability's authority is its *cursor* rather than its bound (`CapMemory.v:280`), it reads strictly below the cursor (`:331`), stores at or below it (`:322`), and a store at offset zero advances it (`:356-373`), which is the frontier discipline the frame layout is built on. `CapMemtype.v:123-133`'s `UNINIT` access kind threads it through every load and store, 26 `PCU*` constructors carry it into the assembly (`CapAsm.v:394-421`), and `CapAsmgen.v` compiles **every** stack access through one: `Mgetstack` and `Msetstack` (`:1063-1083`), `transl_load` and `transl_store`'s stack arms (`:1115-1218`), the frame carve `PCUtoPtr_s X31 SPC SPC :: PCsbase SPC SPC X31` (`:1333-1334`), and `store_zeros_routine`'s byte-at-a-time frame zeroing (`:1275-1285`). The canonical stack capability is `URWL` plus `Directed` (`CapGlobalenvs.v:186-190`). The profile has neither: its stack capability is `perms_stack`, the one shape carrying `store-local` ([cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) line 119), over a frame the static memory plan lays out at composition (R-08-011), so the carve, the zero-initialization and the spill discipline are all re-derived rather than edited.
  * **Axis 4, sealing, diverges in the field and in the authority, and axis 5's sentries diverge with it.** SECOMP's object type is `Definition seal := ptrofs` (`OCapValues.v:24`), so the otype space is the whole address space, and sealing authority is a separate seal-capability *object* holding a range and a cursor, `OCVseal : seal -> seal -> seal -> ocsealable` (`:263`), consumed by `seal_capability`/`unseal_capability` against a bounds check on that cursor (`:1480-1496`); there is no `Permit_Seal` and no `Permit_Unseal` anywhere in the directory, and `CapAsmgen.v:1374-1376` gives each *call site* its own otype by offsetting a per-compartment seal key. The profile's otype is four bits naming sixteen classes, three architecturally reserved and thirteen composition-allocated with no runtime type allocation ([cap_format.sail](../model/model/core/cap_format.sail) line 58, [cap_common.sail](../model/model/core/cap_common.sail) lines 49-65), and its sealing authority is two permissions the lattice never admits together. SECOMP's sentry is the `E` permission, upgraded to `RX` on installation into PCC while *preserving the cursor* (`OCapValues.v:1472-1476`), so it does not force entry at the base, and global function entries use a second mechanism again, a **sealed** `RX Global` capability (`CapGlobalenvs.v:180-184`); the profile's are two reserved otypes, a forward edge and a backward edge, with `cjalr` admitting each only in its own role (R-15-008, R-15-071).
  * **Axis 6, bounds, is the one place the delta adds an obligation rather than exchanging one.** SECOMP's bounds are exact by construction, three `ptrofs`, so nothing in `CapAsmgen` ever computes a representable alignment and no narrowing can round. The profile's are CHERI Concentrate at 8 and 6 mantissa bits, byte-exact to 128 bytes and rounding outward above that (R-15-007c), and R-15-007k requires **every** narrowing the profile's code emits to be exactly representable, discharged against the slot plan rather than by a runtime instrument, with `CRAM`, `CRRL` and `CSetBoundsExact` unadmitted. That is a side condition on the backend's emission with no upstream analogue. Its input is R-08-011's slot plan, which **M1.9** produces **ahead of this item rather than after it**: [MemoryPlan.v](../proofs/MemoryPlan.v) carries the plan, the placement rule and the delta, and what M1.9 still owes is its seeded-weakening gate rather than the plan. R-15-007k already owns the obligation and already locates it on the plan, so nothing here is a gap in the register.
  * **Axis 7, the register file, carries one part that has no encoding.** `CapAsm.v:46-53` declares `X1` through `X32`, which with the separately modelled `X0` is **33** integer registers where stock `riscV/Asm.v` declares 32 and where a RISC-V register field is five bits wide, so the thirty-third is unencodable in this dialect or any other. Beside it sit a full 32-entry double-precision file (`:62-70`) the profile deletes with scalar FP along with the 30 constructors naming it (R-15-039), and `DDC` (`:103`) with its six `exec_load_ddc`/`exec_store_ddc` forms (`:1228-1251`), which is the hybrid mode R-15-001c deletes. Two register conventions are broken beside them and both are the pin's rather than the profile's: `SPC`, the stack capability, is `X3` (`:133`) while `CapAsmgen.v:1290` reserves `X3` for the caller's seal key, so `:1318`'s `PCseal X4 SPC X3` seals `X3` with itself, and the declared `SPPC := X2` (`:135`) that RISC-V's ABI would put the stack in is used nowhere. The merged reading is the one thing that already agrees, `CapAsm.v:43-44` modelling exactly the merged integer-and-capability file [reg_type.sail](../model/model/core/reg_type.sail) carries.
  * **Axis 8, the address model, is named and deliberately left out of the estimate.** SECOMP addresses are `ptrofs` offsets inside CompCert's block model, split three ways by `heap_ptr | stack_ptr | seal_ptr` (`OCapValues.v:401-404`) with `derive_offset` reading the region, over a runtime `Mem.alloc`. The profile is one flat 36-bit physical space (R-15-002a), dense by construction (R-15-002b), with no heap and composition-time allocation. Whether that is a re-homing of the memory model or a replacement of it is what this axis turns on, and nothing measured here answers it, so the figure above excludes it and says so rather than carrying a number nothing stands behind.
  * **One further finding is recorded because it is load-bearing and is not a difference from the profile but a defect in the pin.** `is_directed_store` (`CapMemory.v:296-304`, mirrored at `CapMemtype.v:320-330`) is the clause that stops a stack capability being stashed below itself, and its own comment says it checks the stored value's maximum read authority against *the address it is stored at*. The pattern variable in `| OCVcap c =>` shadows the function's `c` parameter, so `derive_offset k c ofs` reads the address off the value being stored rather than off the authority it is stored through, and the condition becomes a statement about the value alone. Under the `UNINIT` kind, whose offsets are negative by construction (`CapMemtype.v:126`), it then reads as `a <= a + ofs` and fails for the offsets the discipline uses. Nothing in this repository rests on it; it is recorded so that a lane re-deriving the directed-store rule reaches for the comment rather than the code.
  * **The prerequisite this box did not hold is held, and what each output turns out to be is the finding.** The generated `compcert.ini` puts `-mabi=lp64d` on the preprocessor, assembler and linker and no `riscv64` cross toolchain is installed, so `ccomp` reaches a host `gcc` that rejects the flag; relaxing the **preprocessor** line alone is what `-S` needs, the other two waiting on the cross toolchain M1.4 stands up. On a C program shaped to [`base-integer`](../corpus/base-integer.s)'s and [`base-memory`](../corpus/base-memory.s)'s subjects, `ccomp -S -dcapasm` exits 0 and writes both. The `-S` half is 87 lines of stock lp64d RISC-V carrying **no capability mnemonic at all**, `-S` reaching the `riscV/` backend rather than `cheririscV/`, so it is a baseline to diff against rather than purecap output. The `-dcapasm` half is a 280-line Gallina term carrying **56 `TODO: __<mnemonic>__` tokens over 38 distinct constructors**: the long-word arithmetic and branches (`Paddl` ten times, all six `Pb*l` forms), the capability loads and stores (`PClb` through `PCsw`), and the uninitialized stack forms the frame discipline runs on. No corpus member is an input to either, all twenty-five being hand-written dialect assembly where `ccomp` takes C. One limit of the baseline: on a function with twelve pointer arguments the **stock** `backend/PrintAsm.ml` raises an uncaught `Failure "not implemented"` where `-dcapasm` writes its term, so the baseline is narrower than the capability path it is meant to be diffed against.
  * **The differential rig's shapes do not meet, and what is missing is an executor rather than a corpus.** [differential-corpus.md](differential-corpus.md) §6 adjudicates two executors record by record, and neither `ccomp` output is a record stream. Three things stood between them when the delta was taken against `cheririscV/` and **the start-from decision removed two of them.** The vacuous step relation and the 33-register file with a `DDC` are the pin's, and an authored backend over stock `riscV/` inherits neither: its semantics are written rather than taken, and its register file is the profile's merged 32 by construction. What stands is the encoder from the emitted stream to the frozen dialect's binary, which is **M1.4′**'s. What this item has beside it is the build, the delta, the axis above, and the `-S` baseline the authored output will diff against.
  * **Two duties R-18-014a places on this backend are scoped in here rather than discovered later.** Backend *completeness* includes selecting the frozen capability indexed load and store (R-15-007e) and bitfield extract and insert (R-15-067a) wherever the pattern matches, and three of those rows are decided at the **final freeze**: the indexed form's scale immediate against the emitted mix (R-15-007g), and the bitfield pair's field-specifier form, whether the insert form is carried, and **whether the pair is carried at all** (R-15-067d). Each is a selection pattern written against a row that can move and one may be deleted outright; R-15-014a settles the circularity by making the provisional profile a total compilation target, so what is left is rework at the margin, priced inside the range above. The second duty, **outlining and tail merging** (R-15-036o, R-18-003c), presupposes a backend already emitting correct purecap code and is **M1.3a** rather than a placement inside this item.
  * **Gate satisfied at M1.1a: contained.** The backend is a build-time producer this repository never redistributes, so the re-homing may proceed. Two things follow into this cell: the work must not create a path by which the backend or a derivative of it is conveyed, and the cell prices the re-homing alone. The forfeit, an image a third party cannot rebuild, is M1.1a's to state and M1.4's and R-05-023a's to live with.
  * **The containment decides the shape of this item's evidence, and leaves one question open that is not technical.** A re-homed `cheririscV/` is a derivative of the backend, so it cannot live in this repository under [COPYRIGHT.md](../COPYRIGHT.md)'s map: every measurement above is taken in a build lane outside every checkout and reported here rather than landed as a diff. **The re-homed tree lives at `c:\Users\symbi\source\repos\verifiedos-cheri-compcert`, a local git repository with no remote, published nowhere**, on the same footing as the CHERI-QEMU fork M2.1 stands up, so the non-commercial term is untouched and containment holds. Its first commit is the pin extracted verbatim and the axis above is its second, `1e79ed1`. **What it supplies downstream is a configured and built stock CompCert 3.17 at the pin, which is M1.1b's start-from, plus axis 1 as a landed measurement over `cheririscV/`**; the patch series is a patch series on the directory this item no longer re-homes, so M1.3, M1.6 and M1.7 resume from a built toolchain rather than from a resumable re-homing. One question stays open and it is not technical: whether anybody maintains a fork of a non-commercially-licensed compiler over the life of this plan. **The route-around-it arm is not a second one**: M1.1a re-weighed it against what this item measured, on exactly the ground that arm was priced against a *verified* backend, and it holds with the trigger for re-weighing it again named at that cell.
  * **M1.1a's own re-weigh trigger is not reached, and the cell says so rather than leaving it to a reading.** That trigger is M1.2's midpoint and the bootstrap claim, and neither is met: no hour is booked against this item, axis 1 having landed under the previous start-from and being a measurement rather than progress through it, and nothing since has moved a third-party rebuild onto the critical path. M1.4 records the direction outright, containment leaving the toolchain undistributable so the reproducible-bootstrap ground is not available to be spent, and M1.6 moves it the other way only as far as *a component lowered this way is third-party-rebuildable, and that does not recover the kernel*. What would fire it is a release criterion or R-05-023a's instrument citing a rebuild by an independent party as evidence rather than as hygiene, which is a licensing and product call and is not taken here.

It sits here rather than at its number because its two ends fix it: it presupposes a backend already emitting correct purecap code, which is M1.3's, and the freeze report measures against an outlined corpus, which is M1.8b's.
  * R-15-036o's two scope rules are the deliverable's shape rather than a constraint on it: the transforms are **intra-compartment**, and only regions needing no frame of their own are candidates. A region that would take a frame is not a cheaper encoding of the same code, and a transform crossing a compartment would move code across the boundary the whole authority model is stated at.
  * The pass emits the **S4 provenance stratum** the analyzer consumes, region class by region class, because the measurement is a bytes-and-worst-case-cycles pair per admitted class rather than one figure: a transform that buys bytes and costs the worst case is a finding rather than a saving, and only the sidecar makes the pair computable.

* [ ] **M1.4 · Re-home LLVM MC/`lld` and compose static images** · 8 h, range 5–11 · 0.7% · X · after the M8a gate
  * Exclude general dynamic linking.
  * **Re-priced and deferred behind M1.4′**, because once `dialect.py` is generated from the model bundle (S15) this item's residue is disassembly and object tooling rather than an encoder, and under M1.1a's containment the toolchain is undistributable anyway, so the reproducible-bootstrap ground for taking it early is not available to be spent.
  * **The upstream is pinned and its terms are read, so this item starts against a named edition rather than against whatever is current.** `upstream/llvm-project` is a gitlink at `ca7933e4`, the commit `llvmorg-22.1.8` names, and [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the row. `LICENSE.TXT` at that pin is **Apache-2.0 with LLVM-exception**: permissive, no copyleft term, no non-commercial restriction, and so the only licence on the three that gate this plan's toolchain which constrains neither the arm taken nor the arm left open. The exception waives the notice conditions of Apache §4(a), (b) and (d) for portions embedded in object form by compilation, which is the clause a compiler's own runtime pieces raise rather than one this item spends. The gitlink is unpopulated on purpose, this item not having started; a clone that needs it runs `git submodule update --init upstream/llvm-project`.
  * One reading trap the pin records against: an annotated tag and the commit it names are different objects, and `git ls-remote --tags --refs` prints the **tag object**. `llvmorg-22.1.8` is `e0130735` as a tag and `ca7933e4` as a commit, and a gitlink stores the commit, so the pin above is the second. A row transcribed from the first would disagree with `git submodule status` on a tree nobody had populated yet.
  * **The deferred half of this item has a start-from, to be weighed at the hardening program's opening.** Verified assembly, linking and image construction are deferred like the backend's own proof (§0), and the Coq line covering two of the three exists: **CompCertELF** carries verified separate compilation from C through to ELF object files on Stack-Aware CompCert, and a **framework for developing verified assemblers for the ELF format** instantiates a parameterized template for the complete x86 and RISC-V assembly languages, generating verified encoders and decoders from *declarative instruction specifications* through CSLED at about 1.1% average slowdown. This repository already holds such a specification twice, in the model's `encdec` clauses and in [vos/dialect.py](../tools/vos/dialect.py)'s mnemonic table, which is what makes it worth weighing.
  * Four things bound how much is available. Both artifacts are CompCert extensions, so the first question at the milestone that would take either is whether the **INRIA Non-Commercial License Agreement** reaches them, read from each artifact's own licence file at that milestone against the containment arm M1.1a took. Neither is CHERI, so a capability-carrying encoding, a tag-bearing image and the sealed forms stay authored. Neither is this dialect, so the RISC-V instantiation is a template to re-home rather than a backend to adopt, the same act M1.2 prices for the compiler. And whether this or R-05-023a's decompilation into logic is the covering instrument for the source-correspondence theorem is a call at the hardening program's opening: this cell records a candidate and decides nothing.
* [x] **M1.5 · Run the CertiCoq-to-Wasm oracle** · 3 h actual · 0.3%
  * The loop closes end to end ([tools/wasm-oracle/](../tools/wasm-oracle/)): `demo.v`'s `Nat.eqb (fib 20) 6765` compiled by `CertiRocq Compile Wasm` and run on stock Node prints `true`, the §0 inner loop with no cross-toolchain, no image, and no machine model involved.
  * Release rather than pin: the oracle installs **CertiRocq 0.9.1 for Rocq 9.1** (`rocq-certirocq.0.9.1+9.1`, MIT), which carries the merged CPP 2025 CertiCoq-Wasm backend (`theories/CodegenWasm`, proven against WasmCert-Coq) that the 0.9 opam release predates, and which depends on `rocq-metarocq-erasure-plugin` and `rocq-metarocq-safechecker-plugin` at `>= 1.5.1`, released MetaRocq rather than the unreleased 9.1 branch. Neither a source commit nor a compatibility patch is carried here. Rocq is 9.1.1 by the release's own `rocq-core {>= "9.1" & < "9.2~"}` constraint, not staleness, and it is this oracle's alone: **M1.2 does not share it** and builds against the distribution's Coq 8.20.1, for the two reasons M1.2 measures.
  * **The retirement the release made available is taken.** The pinned commit `4f53ca97` and its two-site patch are gone, and the patch is not merely unnecessary but inapplicable: 0.9.1 already carries `inlining` inside `make_unsafe_passes`, no longer holds the `erasure_config` field the patch moved it from, and has moved both edited plugin sources. The prover does not move, so nothing this oracle stands on moves. Two caveats keep this a refresh: a release that adds a backend is not a release that has exercised it here, so the retirement is green **only against the same `demo.v` round trip**; and CertiRocq's `< 9.2~` constraint still fixes the prover, so a later Rocq bump is a separate act with the proof gate on the other end. It does not reach the claim that the same version serves M1.2, which rests on SECOMP's constraint rather than CertiRocq's.
  * **The round trip runs in an opam switch rather than in the container, because the container's base image has no arm64 build.** `rocq/rocq-prover` publishes `linux/amd64` alone and this box is arm64, so a container here is emulated. The release is installed into a switch of its own beside the proof gate's `rocq-9.1.1`. The evidence therefore covers the package and the dependency closure opam resolves for it; the [Dockerfile](../tools/wasm-oracle/Dockerfile) installs the same package by name and version, and its base image and repository configuration are the half this evidence does not reach.
  * Refresh evidence: `rocq-certirocq.0.9.1+9.1` installed from `rocq-released` on `ocaml-base-compiler.4.14.2`, resolving `rocq-core` 9.1.1, both MetaRocq plugins at 1.5.1+9.1, `coq-wasm` 2.2.0 and `coq-compcert` 3.17; `rocq c demo.v` emitting a 32,415-byte `demo.oracle_demo.wasm`; and `node --stack-size=10000000 run_demo.mjs` on Node 22.22.1 printing `true` at exit 0, `run_demo.mjs` needing no change because 0.9.1 exports the same names.
  * Environment findings, booked for later lanes. The container recipe exists for the prover rather than for privilege: CertiRocq pins Rocq 9.1.1 and [tools/run.py proofs](../tools/vos/cli/proofs.py) compiles the shipped proofs under the same version, in a switch of its own because `rocq-core` caps dune below what the Sail packages need, which is why [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) resolves the prover rather than assuming it. Neither privilege nor toolchain argues for a container, the distribution carrying gcc 15.2.0 and running as root, and no container runtime being installed in guest or host. The WSL VM idles out between commands and takes containers with it, which is the stock WSL2 `vmIdleTimeout` rather than a runtime problem; the switch that disables it lives only in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, global and permanent, so the fix is scoped into the repository instead: every loop in [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py) starts a detached, idempotent, self-expiring keepalive (8 h default, `VOS_KEEPALIVE_HOURS=0` opts out, `model.py keepalive --stop` ends one early). Cisco Umbrella re-signs `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`, so the Windows-exported proxy root CA must be installed into any Linux trust store fetching GitHub release assets.
  * WSL Containers (`wslc.exe`) declined, revisit at GA: it is Docker-shaped, unlicensed, and returns its session VM's memory to the host, but it needs WSL ≥ 2.9.3 from the pre-release channel (this box is 2.7.11), is public preview with GA targeted for fall 2026, and moves the wrong finding. On the proxy it regresses: [microsoft/WSL#40945](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/issues/40945) reports the backend session VM receives *no* proxy environment and Windows root CAs still do not propagate, so the registry pull happens where this lane cannot reach it with the Umbrella CA. Preview also lacks Compose and `build --platform`. The live reason to revisit is idle memory rather than container lifecycle.

The **reaching** exit, `ToCString` to C and then M1.2's contained purecap `ccomp`, is the only one aimed at a backend R-18-002 admits, and it runs the Gallina-to-C step through a pretty-printer carrying no theorem, neither `ToCString.v` holding a `Theorem`, a `Lemma`, a `Proof` or a `Qed`, bedrock2's at 324 lines and Rupicola's own at 217 and both `Definition`s and `Fixpoint`s alone. So this cell's *its target exists* is true of the exit with no proof on it and false of the exit that carries the proof, which is S12's reading confirmed from the artifact rather than cited. **Neither exit produced an R-18-002-admissible artifact here, and the second half of that is a measurement rather than a construction**: the verified exit lands on the target the entry forbids by name, and the reaching exit's backend emitted plain RV64 on this program, its capability printer stubbed at exactly the arms the program uses, so that exit's admissibility is contingent on M1.2 completing the backend rather than shown here.

Taken construct by construct over [SupervisionTree.v](../proofs/SupervisionTree.v)'s 1,905 lines rather than from the paper: **general recursion is at zero sites**, all thirteen `Fixpoint`s structural over a list or a `nat`, with no `Program Fixpoint`, no `Function`, no `Equations`, no well-founded order and no fuel parameter anywhere, which is the discipline that file's own header states it was written to and M6.1a's note already records as kept rather than claimed. The three inductives are real, 23 constructors across them, but every one is a closed nullary enumeration with no recursion and no parameter, which is the cheap case rather than the expensive one. **What blocks is that the artifact is a higher-order specification and the target language has no function values at all**: bedrock2's `expr` has seven forms and none is a closure, and its `cmd`'s `call` names its callee by a string literal, so there is no indirect call either. Against that, seven of `Machine`'s sixteen fields are function-valued and are projected 45 times, eleven of the file's type-valued definitions are function types with four of them second-order over `Signals`, and four of the thirteen fixpoints take a function argument, applied to a lambda at 22 sites and to a record field at four more.     * **A cell has four kinds and the gate has three outcomes**, which is what lets the report be read before its corpus exists. A `value` is a measurement, `n/a` is an axis that does not apply, `pending` names the symbol the cell waits on, and `fixture` is a number the wiring produced from a synthetic input; no predicate reads a fixture cell as a value, so the fixture exercises the §4 join end to end without ever clearing the gate. A predicate accordingly passes, rejects, or defers on a named symbol, and over today's record four decide, eight defer and none rejects.

    * Eleven findings.
      * **The item's landed half was gated on neither milestone §12 books it behind.** What an instrument needs from a backend is a transport rather than a design: §4 fixes the sidecar record's eleven fields, §2 the corpus, §6 every column and §8 every threshold, so the whole instrument is derivable from the contract alone. That is the M0.11 authoring decision paying out as booked, an instrument arriving with its own acceptance criteria having graded its own homework where one arriving against criteria authored before any backend existed cannot.
      * **§0's ordered act and the contract's §1 read as two different orders.** §0 lists the dictionary before outlining and §1 puts FD-3 first, on the ground that its transform changes the corpus every later row is measured against (R-15-036o, R-15-036p), which is what `G-6` mechanizes. They reconcile at different granularities: §0's clause is the **recipe's** order, where a build composes and merges before selecting a dictionary at S6, and §1's is the **decisions'**. The instrument runs both and the report prints both under their own headings; what would settle it is a word in §0 saying which its list is.
      * **The encoded image cannot be decoded per site before FD-2 is taken, so §4's third input arrives on two paths.** Recovering a site's dictionary index means decoding the bundle format, whose header width and slot count are what FD-2 decides, so an analyzer that decoded the image would need the answer FD-1 and FD-2 are jointly measuring. The composer knows the geometry it encoded at, so the image is delivered as its bytes and a per-site entry-and-escape table. The three inputs and the join are unchanged; what moves is which side produces that column.
      * **Two of the three declared bundle geometries are already decided at the break-even hit rate, and that is the one substantive result the instrument produces today.** R-15-036h's slot model and R-15-036j's packing term are arithmetic over the geometry and the hit rate, so each declared `(h, k)` is scorable against the derived 22.4-bit bar at the *p* = 0.804 break-even before anything is measured. The 64-bit bundle fails for every admissible packing term, its 16-bit header amortizing over three slots at 21.33 bits per slot, and would need *p* at least 0.95 with no packing loss at all; the 256-bit bundle clears it for every admissible term; the reference 128-bit bundle clears it only while the realized packing term is at most **0.029** against its own bound of 0.199, asking the encoder for about a seventh of its worst-case padding. All three are readings of the model rather than the acceptance test, which is on observed output; what they decide is where the sweep's attention goes.
      * **A gate with two outcomes would print the same twelve lines whether or not any predicate had a body.** Eight of the twelve defer over today's record, so nothing separates one deferring on a pending operand from one that would defer whatever it was handed. Each is therefore seeded a defect in [test_freeze.py](../tools/quarantine/tests/test_freeze.py) and required to reject it by name, and two needed their reading widened to reject at all: `G-7`'s rebuild is the CI gate's act, so what it decides here is a hash recorded with no pins beside it; and `G-4`'s delta-item half is K-70's, so what it decides is the record's own shape.
      * **§6's column paragraphs are not a membership a rule can read, which is a property of the document rather than a gap in K-77.** Each states its columns as backticked tokens, and the same idiom in one sentence carries an operand-class id, a corpus member and a formula, so a scan over them reads three vocabularies as one and would report a column named `OC-5`. The column sets are the instrument's schema, held by its own tests, and `G-4` decides over the record they produce.
      * **FD-2 is the one decision §6 states no default arm for.** FD-1 states that it has none and says why; FD-2 is in the same position, its structural tie-break selecting among configurations that clear the bar, and states nothing. The instrument carries `n/a` with that ground rather than inventing the reference instantiation as a default, which would be a decision taken by an analyzer.
      * **A membership held in both directions is not a relation held in both directions.** Two id sets held equal catch a member renamed on either side. A relation walked from one side catches only what that side names, so a decision measured over a corpus member neither roster declares is never reached at all. Both of K-77's relations are quantified over the union of the two rosters, and the same reading turns the declared-parameter pair from a set comparison into a per-decision binding.
      * **A floor under the document does not floor the rule.** The floors group catches a *document* whose row shape moved; it cannot catch a comparison deleted from the *rule*, K-77's feeds loop being removable with the checker green and its mutant still killed. So the two relations carry a floor over their own size, and one rule gets three selftest cases rather than one, a case seeded at one pair proving nothing about the other nine.
      * **A `vos` parse that reads a path off disk is outside the checker's corpus, and the checker's corpus is the git index.** K-77 reads the contract through `Context.text` where the tool reads it through `read(root)`, which is the one place a rule and the tool sharing a parse have to differ: a document deleted from the index and left in the working tree is one this repository does not have, and a rule reading it off disk reports green over it.
      * **§3 says §9 makes an omission a rejection and no predicate of §9 does.** The rule is that S6 runs inside every variant, which §3 calls its most consequential methodological rule and the easiest to skip. None of `G-1` to `G-12` is that predicate. The instrument records the rule per variant so a report says on its face whether the dictionary was re-selected, and the missing predicate is the contract's to add rather than this instrument's to invent.

### M2 · Fast emulator, struck

The CHERI-QEMU fork (§10) carried two jobs and neither is now its own. **The second executor is R2's**, TestRIG adjudicating one RVFI-DII trace format across the golden model and the RTL under Verilator (S11), which arrives on the same schedule and cross-checks the artifact that ships rather than a second model. **The first, the daily driver, is the Sail emulator itself**, at the rate S4 measures, which is taken before this strike is relied on rather than after.

**So M2.1 through M2.4 are struck and carry no estimate**, and what stood behind them was 64.5 h of class-X work, a fork of a tree five major releases behind its own upstream whose landed reconnaissance records 79 unimplemented symbols across four families of state the frozen format does not carry.

**Both rulings are discharged and the strike stands.** The first was a measurement and S4 took it: the emulator retires 71 to 107 kIPS untraced and 66 to 100 traced at the frozen profile, and *through* M6 nothing this plan runs is emulator-bound at that rate, the RVFI-DII rig being socket-bound between ten and forty times below it and the whole differential corpus being 8,830 records. What is left is the composed boot, which does not exist until M7.1, and S4 states the instruction budget that boot has to fit inside for this reading to hold and names M7.1 as what measures it against the budget. The second is taken at M4.4 as single-instance bring-up, the multikernel's defining property being first exercisable on the RTL track where a second implementation already stands, so no reinstatement rides it either. Reinstating costs the pin and nothing else: both upstreams stay gitlinks, and the containment rule below is unchanged by the strike. **What is not a trigger is the emulator feeling slow**, which is the reading this paragraph exists to refuse: what reopens M2 is a boot over S4's budget, and a strike re-argued without one is a strike re-argued from impatience.

The measured state of M2.1 stands as the record of what was done, because it is evidence rather than a plan: the library is narrowed, the 64+1-bit instantiation agrees with the Sail model over 21,546 model-emitted vectors with no disagreement, and the fork is cut as far as anything compiles before a decode surface exists.

* ~~**M2.1 · Fork CHERI-QEMU and narrow compressed capabilities**~~ · struck
  * **State: the library is narrowed and cross-checked, and the fork is cut as far as anything can compile before M2.2.** The two upstreams are gitlinks, `CTSRD-CHERI/cheri-compressed-cap` at `78a34ba5` and `CTSRD-CHERI/qemu` at `d0bb921c`, which is the tip of that project's own default branch `qemu-cheri` and carries `VERSION` 7.0.0. The 64+1-bit instantiation agrees with the Sail model over **21,546 vectors the model itself emitted, with no disagreement and no parameter mismatch**, beside the model's fifteen format properties ported and holding. The base is cloned at that commit on branch `verifiedos/m21` in the separate repository the containment rule puts it in, the format is in the vendored library's header chain and its target-selection macros, and **the library builds inside the fork's tree with its own suite at 590 of 590 passing**, so the five formats it already carried are unregressed. The curation is **four files, 820 insertions and 2 deletions**, which is the size a re-parameterization should be.
  * **The fork made one assumption the frozen profile breaks, and finding it is most of what cutting the fork bought.** `cheri_defs.h` derived the capability width from the target word size: 32-bit target to a 64-bit capability, 64-bit target to a 128-bit capability, `#error` otherwise. A capability is twice a register on every target that fork carries, and the frozen profile is the combination the chain has no arm for, **a 64-bit capability on a 64-bit target**. The width is now stated by the target with the old inference kept as the default, and the RV32 arm is refused outright rather than given a plausible format (R-15-005). That is where *the capability is the register* becomes a fact the emulator is built on, and anything downstream assuming `CHERI_CAP_SIZE == 2 * TARGET_LONG_SIZE` is now wrong where a compiler can find it.
  * **Where the build forces a choice the refusal is the choice.** The instantiation supplies no `flags` accessor, no execution-mode enum, no user permissions, no reserved-field accessor, no level bits, and none of `is_representable_cap_exact`, `get_alignment_mask` or `checked_setbounds`. A zero-width definition or a plausible zero would let a port read a field this format does not have and be wrong silently; left absent, each is a compile error at the line that wanted it, and that error list is M2.2's worklist rather than a defect here.
  * **What does not compile, stated exactly.** `qemu-system-riscv64cheri` does not link and cannot until M2.2, for two reasons: the 79 symbols below, and the fact that this machine carries no QEMU configure stack, no meson and no `glib-2.0` or `pixman-1` development packages, installing which on a shared VM to watch a build fail for reasons already enumerated is not a trade this item takes. The format-selection edit is therefore checked by a test quoting the macro chain verbatim and holding that it pastes to `cc64v_`, the half of that edit capable of producing a wrong-but-valid identifier.
  * **The narrowing is a new parameterization rather than a selection among the library's existing ones, which is what the estimate is against.** Upstream's 64-bit instantiation is Concentrate over a **32-bit** address in a 64-bit capability where this profile is a 64-bit capability over a 36-bit address, so *already carrying 64- and 128-bit instantiations* names a neighbour rather than a starting point. Three things move that are not widths and none is reachable by `#define`. The exponent is an explicit five-bit field carrying its own case flag, where upstream sets an internal-exponent bit and steals three bits from **each** mantissa, so the frozen format is *more* precise in the normalized case and upstream's split-exponent macros have no reading here. The permission field names one of 32 enumerated sets where upstream's is one bit per permission, so every operation over it is a lattice operation. And the address is narrower than its container, where upstream's fills a `uint32_t` exactly, which makes its cursor sign-extension a no-op there and would move every bounds decode in the top half of the space here. What **is** inherited unchanged is the genuinely Concentrate part: the three-bit region correction, the representable limit, the derivation of the top mantissa's high two bits, the wrap correction, the shape of setbounds, and the field-macro layer, included from the pin rather than copied.
  * **The oracle is the model and the vectors are its output rather than a transcription.** The rig compiles the model's own `prelude`, `core/xlen.sail`, `core/cap_format.sail` and `core/cap_common.sail` with a generator that calls those functions and prints what they return, then requires the C to reproduce every line, the vectors crossing as text so no adapter sits between the two implementations. That is upstream's own method. It reaches decode over every exponent field, `setCapBounds` across the byte-exact threshold and at every power of two, address movement and both offset forms, the representability check, sealing over all sixteen object types, the permission lattice over all 32 codepoints with every single-bit mask, and the bounds check.
  * **Finding, about the algebra rather than either implementation: `setCapBounds`'s containment property holds over a domain that nothing states.** The domain is a requested top at or below 2^36; above it the decoded top wraps below its own base, so the derived region does not contain the requested one. The model does exactly this and the cross-check agrees, so it is not a divergence, and it is unreachable with a tag, `CSetBounds` clearing the tag on precisely the test that fails there. What is owed is that **R-15-007a's representation-correctness proof state the condition** rather than carrying containment unconditionally, and that the format's `$[test]` harness reach that edge. Reported rather than closed: no register entry governs this item.
  * **The Rust device-model option this item was to price is measured and refused at this base.** [The alternatives entry](architectural-alternatives.md) books it contingent on the base having rebased far enough forward to carry QEMU's Rust build; the pinned tree is QEMU 7.0.0 with no `rust/` and system emulation still under `softmmu/`, five major releases short. Taking the option means carrying the CHERI capability layer across that rebase, which is larger than the fork it would serve, so it is refused at this pin and reopens at a later re-pin.
  * **Where M2.1 stops is where the library's consumers begin, and that boundary is measured rather than asserted.** `target/cheri-common/` binds the library through one macro pair and reaches it at **124 call sites naming 79 distinct symbols**, four families of which name state the frozen format does not carry: the capability-mode flag, the reserved field, user permissions, and the level bits. Six further permission bits and two object types have no reading in an enumerated lattice or a two-edge sentry split, and the rounding surface the fork calls is the one R-15-007k deletes. The instantiation implements none of them deliberately, an absent field given a zero-width definition still reading as a field a port will try to use. Deleting those readers is M2.2's decode-surface work, which is why the re-price stays on one cell.
  * **The terms are read at the pin rather than asserted from the project's reputation, and both of them are.** QEMU's `LICENSE` releases the emulator as a whole under **GPL version 2**, makes an unmarked file version 2 or later at the recipient's option, restricts four paths to version 2 only (`bsd-user/`, `linux-user/`, `hw/vfio/`, `hw/xen/xen_pt*`), and leaves the Tiny Code Generator mostly BSD or MIT. The fork is copyleft on every arm, which is all the containment rule needs, and the version-2-only term reaches it only through user-mode trees one bare-metal machine type does not want. **The library this item narrows is a separate and permissive work**: `CTSRD-CHERI/cheri-compressed-cap` is **BSD-2-Clause**, so an instantiation at the frozen widths is not a derivative work of QEMU and is not bound to the fork's repository. It sits beside the fork anyway, the library and the decode surface being read and changed together, and its copy of upstream is taken from the library's own repository rather than the fork's vendored subtree. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries both reads at the pins, plus one `test/` file under `Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception` in the fuzz harness the narrowing does not take.
  * The containment rule that follows is a rule rather than a gate: the fast emulator is a development instrument that no shipped image links against or embeds, so it lives in its own repository under those terms and nothing it touches propagates to the platform. What the rule forbids is the shortcut, vendoring any part of it into this tree, which would put a copyleft term on a repository that currently carries none. The differential rig reaches it as a separate executor over the trace schema, which is the interface that keeps the two apart.

* ~~**M2.3 · Add VLEN=4096 RVV, matrix, and FEC datapaths**~~ · struck; S7's reconnaissance is retained against it, the matrix surface it would have implemented not existing in the profile at all

### M3 · Boot chain

RoT core + firmware (§2), M-mode firmware (§3), crypto core (§4) → the golden emulator reaches the Machine-mode kernel.

* [x] **M3.1 · Add RoT configuration and peripherals to the curated Sail tree** · 13 h actual · 1.2%
  * The four peripherals land as memory-mapped register slaves and nothing else: [sys/rot.sail](../model/model/sys/rot.sail) carries the lifecycle enumeration and its acyclic relation (R-09-032), the one-way fuse bank (R-09-003, R-09-033), the two attributed noise sources into one conditioner behind separate health tests and a single latched fail-stop (R-15-241b, R-15-241c, R-15-241e), the four counters R-10-013's closed enumeration names, and the windowed watchdog whose pet is a challenge-response over a nonce the device issued (R-15-240); the four windows enter the address map in [sys/platform.sail](../model/model/sys/platform.sail) and answer the RoT alone; the composition-fixed parameters enter `platform_config` and the attested devicetree beside the sequencer's cadence M0.16 put there; and the shape a composition may declare is refused where it is wrong rather than carried. **No instruction, no access type, no CSR, and nothing on the decode surface** ([absence-contract.md](absence-contract.md) A-17): `Zkr`'s entropy-source CSR is excluded outright (R-15-037), the lifecycle state is read through a window because R-09-032 makes it a hardware input to the reset path rather than a software-writable register, and no extension-registry row and no opcode moves.
  * **The third configuration is a third *file* and not a third tree** (R-15-005), which is M0.8b's finding carried to the class R-15-005's own criterion names: *no second capability encoding forks the model, the RoT's scalar core included*. [config/verifiedos-rot.json](../model/config/verifiedos-rot.json) composes hart 15 and the same binary runs it, with the same roster, the same class table, and the same address map.
  * **This entry is itself the act of entering M3, and it is a plan call rather than a technical one.** Nothing technical gated it: the item needs the WSL Sail lane and nothing from M1 or M2, every mechanism it wants had a landed precedent, and M0.16's memory sequencer is already an RoT-driven register slave whose own file said *until the RoT core arrives (M3.1)*. What the ordering buys is that R-09-032's *no state outside the enumeration and no edge outside the relation exists in RTL or Sail*, the one place the register names Sail directly for the RoT, stops being a claim with no artifact behind it.
  * **The peripherals are the die's rather than the composition's**, the memory sequencer being the precedent read from the other side: its window is declared in a C-class composition and refuses every requester, the die having one management processor whichever of its cores an emulator composes (R-15-194, R-15-113). So the four windows are declared identically in all three shipped files and what decides whether a door opens is the composed hart's class, read off the roster rather than declared beside it (R-15-052b). Three things follow: no key says which class may open them, a second declaration of one fact being the defect this repository is built to catch; the divergence set K-65 holds carries none of the four; and **a corpus member is deliverable after all**, the C-class emulator reaching these windows from the outside, so [platform-rot.s](../corpus/platform-rot.s) is eleven checks of which ten are refusals.
  * **K-65 generalizes rather than being joined**, its claim unchanged and its subject widened: it holds every shipped configuration against the primary in the keys **that file's own row** declares, and grows a direction no pair against the primary can see, that no two of them compose one core. The divergence set had to become per file: the RoT configuration diverges in twelve keys where the V configuration diverges in two, eleven of the twelve forced by a validator clause, so a single tuple would either license those keys on the V pair, where each would be a second machine, or refuse them on the RoT pair, where each is the only value the model admits.
  * **K-78** in [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py) is the rule K-65 structurally cannot be: it holds a shipped configuration against the *model* rather than against another configuration, and it exists because K-65 is **satisfied** by the defect it catches. A vectorless composition that never moved its vector length agrees with the primary rather than diverging from it, so K-65 says nothing while the attested devicetree names four minimum-vector-length extensions the core does not implement. Both readings are parsed out of the extension registry rather than transcribed ([vos/coreclass.py](../tools/vos/coreclass.py)), so a rung re-based or a flag added joins the rule; the ladder is a member of the floors group's uncounted enumerations, a ladder that stopped matching reporting every geometry as below no rungs at all.
  * Net change: 3,080 lines added and 58 removed across 22 files, four of them new.
  * Exit evidence: build green; the model's own `$[test]` harness at **96 properties against 84**, twelve of them new; all three shipped configurations validating against the regenerated schema with their key sets agreeing with the generated max configuration at **312 keys against 290**; the differential corpus green at **26 of 26** with **245 checks against 234**, one member and 11 checks new, the manifest at version 9, and every other member's digest unmoved; `model.py devicetree` green, the generated tree compiling under `dtc` with no warning at **5,717 bytes against 4,890** and accepted by the emulator's own bound against the region it is written into, with the RoT composition's own tree compiling at 5,517 bytes and accepted by the same bound and naming no seed; each of the **fourteen** new validator refusals fired against a seeded configuration, the fifteenth reaching schema conformance instead, and all three unmutated compositions accepted; a corpus program running to a HTIF verdict under `verifiedos-rot.json`, so the third configuration is runnable end to end and not only loadable; the checker green on **all 77 rules against 76**, one of them new, with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green; the proof gate closing 19 constants; the co-read ledger unmoved, no register entry or prose span having been touched; and both type checkers clean.
  * Eight findings.
    * **`vlen_exp` cannot say *vectorless*, and what it can say was measured rather than argued.** The knob is Sail **type**-level and constrained `3 <= vlen_exp <= 16`, so zero is not available to mean *no vector register file*; and every `Zvl` rung is gated on `sizeof(vlen_exp)` **alone** with no vector-support conjunct, where the eight vector-crypto flags are each refused by name at load. A vectorless composition left at the C class's exponent therefore validates, runs, and emits `zvl32b_zvl64b_zvl128b_zvl256b` into the **attested** `riscv,isa` string of a core with no vector register file, fired against the landed emulator before a line of the repair was written. What the file declares instead is 3, the floor of the type's constraint. **The cost is stated rather than absorbed**: the generated test-matrix configurations cover 6 through 9 only and the SMT, Rocq, Lean and Lem lanes bake `config/rv64d_v256_e64.json` at emission, so 3 is realizable on the C++ lane alone. That costs this item nothing, those lanes composing hart 0 on the C class, and it is the standing limit on what a *proof* about the RoT composition could be stated against.
    * **The seeded PRNG evades the deterministic-replay obligation rather than discharging it, and saying which is part of the deliverable.** R-15-241 requires every draw accounted for in the replay nondeterminism record, R-16-015 puts every draw from the entropy root in that record's secret class, and R-16-017 requires a secret draw be recorded as a sealed commitment and **never as a value**. A seed in a configuration file replaces the nondeterminism with determinism rather than recording it. What the model carries is everything architectural about the root: two attributed sources, a verdict per source, one conditioner, one latch with no path out of it, and the two orderings R-09-006a and R-12-017 state. What it does not carry is any replay record, commitment or sealing, and [rot.sail](../model/model/sys/rot.sail) says so. The seed is an emulation fact and is kept out of the attested devicetree, checked at every measurement.
    * **One validator arm was dead the moment it was written, and firing the seeded refusals is what found that.** The noise-source *ceiling* is the type's, `plat_rot_noise_sources` being read at `range(0, rot_max_noise_sources)` which the emission writes into the schema as a maximum, so an over-wide declaration is refused at schema conformance before `config_is_valid` runs. The *floor* is the opposite case: a minimum of two is R-15-241e's and no type expresses it. That is M0.16's dead-arm finding one artifact over, and the reason the refusal list is fourteen rather than fifteen.
    * **The watchdog carries no ratio to the spine, and that is a requirement rather than an omission.** R-15-196 makes the RoT's independent slow clock one of exactly three genuinely asynchronous boundaries, *none modeled as fixed-latency*. A `slow_clock_divisor` beside the two bounds is the natural wrong move, being exactly that crossing and attested besides. So the bounds are stated in the watchdog's own ticks, its devicetree node carries no frequency, and the advance is a function **nothing in the model calls**: advancing it from this model's instruction tick would make the timeout a function of retirement, the coupling an independent clock exists to remove.

* [ ] **M3.4 · Integrate verified cryptographic artifacts**
  * Prefer pinned upstream verified implementations over fresh authoring.
  * **That preference is honoured where a Coq-native artifact exists and cannot be for the two post-quantum schemes, because none exists anywhere.** Every ML-KEM and ML-DSA verification effort in the field grounds its correctness in F\*, EasyCrypt, CBMC, HOL Light or Isabelle; Rocq appears only as the Jasmin compiler's meta-theory, and the one result joining a Rocq proof to ML-KEM states that join as informal. The single Rocq-native lattice artifact is a verified number-theoretic transform in an unmerged personal fork of Fiat-Crypto. So authoring the two schemes in Gallina from FIPS 203 and FIPS 204 is the only route consistent with the two-language rule rather than a shortfall against the preference.
  * **Three routes shorten the authoring and none removes it.** The verified NTT above specifies the transform in Rocq and *derives* complete and incomplete NTTs for several schemes, a parameterized derivation over the largest component the two schemes share; it is still a component rather than a scheme, unmerged, and published with no repository, so it is read and re-derived here rather than pinned. **hax**'s Rocq backend takes the Rust libcrux's ML-KEM is written in, but its own project grades that backend **experimental** where the F\* one is stable, which makes its output a draft to review. And the **Jasmin-to-SSProve** line composes the Jasmin compiler's correctness theorem with a translation into SSProve and has carried AES against a machine-readable specification, with `mlkem-libjade` end-to-end verified in Jasmin.
  * Four caveats hold that third route to what it is. It lands in **SSProve**, §5's reduction layer rather than an implementation lane, so what arrives is a Coq object about a Jasmin program rather than the Gallina reference this milestone owes. Jasmin's assembly target is AMD64. `mlkem-libjade`'s functional-correctness proof **assumes its SHA-3 is correct**, an assumption its maintainers expect to discharge during 2026. And the specification it is stated against is EasyCrypt's transcription of FIPS 203 rather than FIPS 203, the widening R-05-022 books.
  * None of the three changes the disposition: no Coq-native ML-KEM or ML-DSA implementation exists, so the destination §0 names is one this repository builds. The estimate is priced against authoring from the standards, so it is generous by however much one of them turns out to carry.
  * **The dispositions, per primitive.** Field arithmetic is **Fiat-Crypto**, incorporated: Coq-native, permissive, and the only candidate admissible to the trust base. SHA-256 and the DRBG take **VST's own BSD-2-Clause Coq layer**, which is the survey's find and which no document here had named: `sha/` carries the Gallina specification and its refinement proof, and `hmacdrbg/` carries the functional specification, the NIST known-answer vectors, and an FCF security proof. SHA-3, both AEADs, ML-KEM and ML-DSA are **authored in Gallina** against the standards. Everything else is an oracle and enters nothing.
  * **Two clauses of that carry a reason beyond availability.** The DRBG is HMAC-DRBG-SHA-256 because R-05-004a makes its computational security a named assumption the masking theorems rest on and VST's FCF proof is the Coq-native object that later discharges it, so a choice made for a functional reference pre-pays a deferred obligation; a Keccak-based construction would ride the vector unit better and forfeit that proof. And the Gallina Keccak is validated against **the same FIPS 202 and ACVP vectors as the Sail unit** R-15-056 through R-15-059 freeze, so the standard is transcribed once and the two transcriptions become a differential pair rather than two independent chances to be wrong.

[THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries them where the plan's own rule puts them. **Three of the four are read at the instrument in M3.4a below**, the act that pins Fiat-Crypto and the two oracles, and the reading corrects this cell's Fiat-Crypto sentence in none of its terms and its VST and vector sentences in three; the fourth, VST's, is read there too and reopens an acquisition-route decision M3.4c takes.

Vendoring the tree is the other arm and is declined on this document's own rule that pinning is free and vendoring is a commitment: it would buy self-containment for one component while M1.1a's containment already forfeits it for the toolchain that compiles every component. **The election is Apache-2.0**, matching this repository's own content and buying the express patent grant on the one primitive class where patents historically bite; the permissive arms stay available if a later act wants Apache's notice and patent-termination provisions out of the tree.

Re-taking that call is a decision at the milestone that owns it and not this item's, so it is reported and the cell above is unedited.
    * **The vectors carry three notice conditions and no licence file.** `usnistgov/ACVP-Server` at `975de31e` has no `LICENSE`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING` or `COPYRIGHT` anywhere at its root; the terms are in the README, and they are notice retention, a change notice on a modified work, and explicit acknowledgement of NIST as the source, with no usage restriction of any kind. No row is added to the fetched table, because K-80 wants that table's License-text column to locate a tracked file and this component has none to locate; the fetched shape is owed at the milestone whose build performs the fetch. What the tree carries today is the FIPS 202 half of the same oracle, read at `XKCP/XKCP`'s `tests/TestVectors/` and quoted as literals inside the statements that decide against it, under that tree's own arm and not these terms.
    * **The oracles are pinned in a pair of lineages rather than a pair of instances.** `hacl-star` is Apache-2.0 at the stock text and its proofs are F\* and Low\*; `libjade` is `CC0-1.0 OR Apache-2.0` in a forty-seven-byte `LICENSE` with both arm texts under `LICENSES/`, and its proofs are Jasmin and EasyCrypt. Neither is copied, vendored or extracted from. **The standing HACL\* takes here is the comparator's and not the interim anchor's, and the entry that books it has to be named** because the register puts that name on the other side of the sentence: R-05-061 makes libcrux/HACL\* an explicitly interim F\*/Z3 widening with a named Coq-native destination, and R-05-022's acceptance lists it first among the three interims carrying a destination and a consumer list rather than beside aiT and Binsec/Rel, which are the two that entry exempts. What R-05-061 books is the **import of a post-quantum primitive** whose correctness would then ride F\* and Z3, which is M3.4b's act; what is pinned here is a second implementation read for its answers, with nothing above it depending on it, so it is not an anchor by R-05-022's own test that an anchor is what something rides as its ground and it has no consumer set for R-05-022a to find growing. `libjade` carries no such entry at all. No entry is edited to say any of this and none is owed one: the distinction is R-05-061's own, between a widening and a comparison. **XKCP is read and declined** on the ground the bring-up SoC's row already carries: its `LICENSE` is a decomposition of a CC0 default across six named exceptions, the AVX2 and AVX512 Keccak assembly CRYPTOGAMS under BSD-or-GPL among them, so *XKCP is CC0* is false of part of what it ships. **What was quoted out of it is read at its own arm all the same**, `tests/TestVectors/` being named in none of the six exceptions and the NIST one of them covering the genKAT sources rather than the files they emit, so the ACVP terms one upstream over do not reach these literals and saying they did would be terms inferred from a lineage.
    * **The Gallina file is the functional layer and nothing else, and it says so.** [proofs/Keccak.v](../proofs/Keccak.v) carries theta, rho, pi, chi and iota by the standard's own names, in the standard's own order and unfused; both round counts under one round-index list; the sponge, pad10\*1, `sha3_224`, `sha3_256`, `sha3_384`, `sha3_512`, `shake128` and `shake256`. **No constant-time property is claimed and none is implied**: R-05-062 and R-05-067 put that obligation on a binary as a 2-safety hyperproperty, a Gallina reference is not one, and the file's being branchless on its inputs is a remark about its shape rather than evidence. No reduction and no distributional claim is made either, R-05-077a's reduction being stated over uniformly drawn keys that a functional reference knows nothing about. So what is shipped is one of R-05-059's three layers, and this is not a shipped primitive under that entry.
    * **The two transcriptions are a differential pair because one derives what the other writes down.** [keccak_p1600.sail](../model/model/extensions/keccak/keccak_p1600.sail) transcribes the twenty-five rho offsets and the twenty-four round constants as tables, on the ground that a reader checks a written row against the published one. This file derives both, the offsets from FIPS 202's own (t+1)(t+2)/2 recurrence over the (x, y) walk and the constants from Algorithm 5's shift register, and then computes that what it derived equals what the standard publishes. **That is the pair that catches both of M0.8d's own defects from the other side**: a mod-5 arm written one row early, exhibited here as a construction and refuted, and a table read in the wrong direction, refuted here by computing that the constant table is not its own reverse. It also states the two forms of pi against each other, the source-indexed one this file uses and the destination-indexed one the Sail file uses, so either is the other's inverse or one of them is wrong.
    * **R-15-056a's round-count clause is a theorem here and a vector besides.** Keccak-*p*[1600, n] is the **last** n rounds, so running the rounds that come *before* the last n and then the short form is the whole permutation, stated over an arbitrary state and an arbitrary round count and proved from the fold's own append law; that is what a known answer cannot show, holding at one input where this holds at every state and every count. The entry also wants both round counts covered by R-15-058's differential oracle, and the short form now carries a value too. **The answer it is checked against is neither transcription's.** The Keccak team publishes the 24-round all-zero state and not the 12-round one, so what stands in for it is the constant the Sail unit's own harness carries at `keccak_kat_zero_12`, which that file's header records as produced by an independent FIPS 202 implementation cross-checked against a standard library's SHA-3: a third route, which is exactly what a known-answer check buys, and not a copy of the Sail transcription, which is what declining it earlier was afraid of.
    * **How much of the word "permutation" is discharged, and whose obligation it is.** The word is in R-15-056's **IS** line and not in its acceptance, which reads whole "it clears all five admission tests; its correctness is a Sail invariant (fixed theta/rho/pi/chi/iota rounds, no tables, no secret-dependent control flow) riding RTL ⊑ Sail" and whose subject is the Sail unit, by the same test that keeps R-15-058 off this file. So showing it of the Gallina transcription is an obligation **this file states of itself**, and four of the five steps carry it: pi is a bijection on the twenty-five lanes and its inverse recovers a state of twenty-five arbitrary words; every rotation the rho table uses is undone by its complement on a lane of sixty-four arbitrary bits; iota is an involution over an arbitrary constant, an arbitrary lane zero and an arbitrary remainder; and chi is a bijection on a row of five bits, checked over all thirty-two rows and refuted of the same clause with its own term dropped. **Theta is not shown invertible**, being a linear map on the 320-bit column-parity space whose inverse is dense, and the composite claim is an obligation the file states of itself and does not close.
    * **Theta's index structure is stated and refuted even so, which is the half a round count and an offset table cannot reach.** The column parity is shown to fold the five lanes of its own column and no lane outside it, over twenty-five arbitrary words with the lanes named on the right so a transposed flattening fails the statement; and D[x] is shown to take the column below unrotated and the column above rotated by one, over five arbitrary parities. The near alternative that clause exists to exclude is the exchange of the two neighbours, which is a theta in every other respect, moves no lane, and agrees with the standard's wherever the two neighbouring parities coincide, so what separates them is the asymmetry alone; it is defined here, held to that single difference, and refuted at the whole permutation against the published state. Without it theta would be the one step map decided by nothing but two whole-permutation known answers.
    * **No decision the register owes is taken.** The word width and bit order, the lane flattening, the byte order of a squeezed output and whether a Gallina reference is inside any acceptance at all are readings of the file, stated as readings. The only literals are the standard's: the 5 x 5 x 64 geometry, twenty-four and twelve rounds, the four output lengths and two security strengths, c = 2n, the two domain separators, and Algorithm 5's eight-bit register and Algorithm 6's seven bit positions. Every rate is computed from the capacity rule rather than written down.

It reaches `proofs/` off the repository root with no corpus-window change, which is the precedent K-42 already sets by reading the apex statement the same way, and it is report-only on K-89's ground: either side may be the one that is right and the fact worth having is which file moved.

Oberon needed the quiescent point because it had no load barrier to buy instead.

Deferring was the third arm and is declining by another route: the payoff is purchasable only before M4.2 writes the incremental form, so a deferral past it costs the same thing without recording why.

### M6 · Userland spine

Init/supervision tree (§8) brings up the reference components; admission checker (§9) validates the package set; the package composer emits the finite typed handler/translator graph and pre-admitted media templates, and the contained object router exercises private namespaces, intents, live queries, deterministic translation caching, and protocol-bound credential handles over the existing IDL and rings.
The ring data plane is brought up in its contract order: the common ring schema and lifecycle authored in the IDL profile with the reference client/server bindings and Coq interface skeleton generated from it, then one copy-based service carrying the SPSC, notification, and capacity proofs, then one DMA service adding the extent, cancellation, teardown, and quiescence proofs, so the contract's constants and generated-proof interfaces are validated on two real services before every other server rides them. No service is grandfathered: one that cannot state its finite capacities, lifecycle semantics, cleanup bounds, and per-operation WCET is not admitted through the ring profile.

SECOMP is the standing demonstration that a fork of an Inria-licensed verified compiler is a workable base as long as the licence is preserved. Both other arms stay open.

No clause this repository has read distinguishes the two, `INRIA/velus` being unpinned and its own file unread here, so neither containment recovers what the other forfeits.

Four clauses are operative: §2 grants a "revocable, nonexclusive, nontransferable, royalty-free and worldwide license ... to use the Software solely for educational, research, or evaluation purposes" and permits Derivative Works "solely for academic, non-commercial research endeavors"; §3 provides that "Any other use is commercial use", making the restriction a property of each act of use rather than of the project; §4 requires a fork to be distributed "under the same terms and conditions as in this License"; and §7 terminates the grant "immediately without notice" on non-compliance. The licensor and the form of instrument are M1.1a's under a **separate grant**, so terms obtained for CompCert do not extend here.

What no entry states is its **membership**: no label set, no order, no join, and `CJ-NI`'s security policy model, row 2 of the inventory, is not authored, so IDL-041's predicates stay stated over an arbitrary lattice.
    * **Whether the IDL's own wire format is a member of the R-05-042 wire-format inventory is unstated.** R-12-024f puts translator content formats in it and R-05-046 makes each descriptor its own crown jewel; the mapping is row 3 under `CJ-IDL` rather than row 10 under `CJ-FORMAT`, and whether its descriptor is also a row-10 member, and so separately conferred, is undecided.
