    * **Nothing bounds the size of a declaration.** R-05-143 bounds every recursive former and R-12-012 bounds every list and string, both being bounds on values; the number of operations, types, fields, or worlds a declaration carries is not a runtime value and no entry bounds it.
    * **The Coq subset has no stated boundary.** R-12-010's criterion is that the admission checker verifies each Tier-1 proof is stated against the matching skeleton and R-18-037 requires conformance among four generated artifacts, and no entry says what a skeleton contains, so what a Tier-1 proof is stated against is undecided by the register.
    * **Two coverage cells read as though the profile existed.** `B-03` by `P-7` reads mode `proved` over R-12-010 and R-12-013a and `B-03` by `P-6` reads `admission-rejected` over R-12-012 and R-12-013, and the profile those four requirements fix did not exist when either mode was written. Authoring it makes neither proved: no proof, no checker and no declaration follows from a specification act.
    * **`CJ-IDL`'s row 3 is ready to flip and is not flipped here.** The status column reads `not authored`, §4 is the mapping that row names, and the flip is a crown-jewels edit whose reading is R-05-150's rather than this lane's. Until it is taken the tree states both halves of a contradiction, K-11 holding the row's id and K-25 only the status string's class, so nothing reads the disagreement.
    * **No entry gives an interface declaration an identity that moves when the declaration moves.** R-12-010's criterion has the admission checker verify each Tier-1 proof is stated against *the matching skeleton* and names nothing a match is decided on; a declaration name plus a profile version does not move when a declaration is edited in place, so a proof stated against the superseded skeleton would still match. The profile fixes a content address at IDL-036 as a declared parameter, which is a decision one document takes and no entry audits.
    * **The wire mapping's encoding shape has no register owner at three places.** The byte order of multi-byte scalars, the width ladder every length, count and discriminant is drawn from, and what a descriptor variant shorter than its declared slot leaves in it are each free: R-05-051b requires that one form be admissible and decides which at none of the three, and R-12-091 fixes the slot size without saying what fills it. Three of the twenty declared parameters exist because of this.
    * **An encoding table is not reachable from a mapping's obligations unless something states where each declared value rides.** Every obligation of §4.2 was stated over the descriptor, which R-12-092 restricts to scalars, buffer references, a tag, a deadline and a flag set, so four rows of the encoding table encoded nothing any obligation placed and an operation taking a record or a string had no stated place on the wire. IDL-023a is the joining rule; the lesson is that a mapping owes a placement rule beside its encoding rows, and that a table whose rows nothing reaches reads as complete.
    * **K-82's count-word vocabulary stopped at twenty where the plan's own figure convention spells word forms to ninety-nine.** A note declaring more than twenty findings matched no block, so the rule read the note as recording none and failed on the disagreement rather than on the count, which is fail-loud and still points at the wrong side. The table is now [vos/figures.py](../tools/vos/figures.py)'s own word form rather than a second list, so the reading stops where the convention stops; no note before this one had crossed the boundary.

The repair is at the next insertion rather than at this one, and §4.3 says so in place.

Everything a composition fixes is a field: the roster, the start order, the manifest, the retired set, the per-detector assertion and clear thresholds, the dwell, the intervention window, the backoff schedule and its ceiling, the boot bound, the detector-to-action map, the class ladders and the declared victim. A unit is a finite index below the roster count rather than an abstract carrier, which is the Rupicola subset kept as a discipline and not claimed as a result.
    * **The bring-up half reads the start order as a precedence over the manifest's own edges**, so starting a unit before its grantee is refused, and beside it the roster obligations that the order alone does not carry: every member started exactly once, and nothing named outside the signed roster. The loader is obliged to return the signed composition whatever it observes, refuted by one that drops the roster under pool pressure.
    * **The re-grant half states R-12-074 and R-10-037 apart, because read as one equality they contradict.** One says exactly the edges the manifest fixed; the other says authority is re-derived at the manifest *and the current revocation epoch*, so no restore resurrects what a revocation retired. The difference is machine-checked rather than argued: the construction that faithfully rebuilds the manifest mints nothing and resurrects a retired edge, so the two clauses are not one clause stated twice.
    * **Detection and reaction are stated as properties an alternative construction breaks rather than as absences.** A detector reads its own signal alone, refuted by one that scans every signal; an action does not vary with the observation, refuted by one that terminates past a threshold; a victim stays inside one criticality class, refuted by a selector that reaches the non-sacrificable unit under pressure; a bounded schedule bounds every attempt including past its last step, refuted by a doubling one that crosses at step four; admission respects the dwell and the window, refuted once per conjunct so neither is dead; and a reaction is a function of the declared state, refuted by a supervisor that answers differently at two states carrying one declared record.
    * **Twenty weakenings are generated over the start order rather than authored**, four transpositions, five deletions, five proper suffixes and six duplicate starts, every one refused as one conversion and again per family and again as a theorem quantified over the index. Each transposition is computed to break **exactly one** manifest edge and the specification to break none, which is the check that no conjunct is dead. Every refuting construction is also shown to satisfy the obligations it does not break, so what refuses it is the named defect and not its shape.
    * **Ten survivors on the first seeded run, every one real, and each closed by adding the statement whose absence it exposed.** Reading `precedes` at an absent unit was unreachable because the occurrence check refused a deletion first; four threshold comparisons had no signal sitting *on* the boundary; the stranger witness's index was unconstrained; and four steps of the doubling schedule never crossed the ceiling. Narrowing the population would have hidden all ten. The zero-width hysteresis band one of them exposed is now a stated theorem and a reported register gap.

It models no memory, no unit lifecycle, no object class.

    * The Lustre node and the Vélus → Clight → CHERI-CompCert lowering, taken on Inria's own terms per M6.0a.

What the arm carries into this cell is that nothing built here may be conveyed except on Inria's own terms, and that the Gallina reference model §8 keeps as its host-side oracle is now doing double duty as the standing route out if the grant ever has to be given up.
    * **The form the arm takes when this item opens is a fork on the licensor's own terms, which §4 expressly provides for**, the Agreement requiring that a distributed derivative go out "under the same terms and conditions as in this License". So the route is the one `upstream/SECOMP` already demonstrates against the same licensor: fork, preserve the instrument, convey nothing from this tree. It is chosen for now rather than forever, and the reason it can be is that every arm stays open behind it, the §8 Gallina model being an artifact rather than a plan. What the arm still does not buy is a commercial position: §3 makes noncommercial use a property of each act of use and §7 terminates the grant on non-compliance without notice, so a change in what the first release is for needs a new grant rather than a new reading. That is a product decision this cell records and does not take.
* [ ] **M6.2 · Refine admission checkers to CompCert-C**
  * **Split at entry on a ruling S1 has now taken: measured boot covers a statically composed image, and the device does not re-admit it.** Admission of a composed image is an offline act and the device verifies a signature, so the on-device CIC kernel is not on the boot path and only the composition-time half is pre-gate. R-13-001c is what decides it rather than convenience: that entry already makes the composer an untrusted producer joining no trust base, and already states the device's own act as naming the roster, fetching what it lacks, and admitting the result through the ordinary checks. The bring-up reading points the same way, the derivations being thin or stubbed until the Tier-0/1/2 proofs exist, so the arm not taken would put a proof-term checker on the boot path with nothing to check.
  * What the arm not taken would have cost is the split itself: M6.2b would join the critical chain and its 18.5 h of class-X work would fall before M8a rather than behind it. What this arm costs is a deferral, which is the cheaper way to be wrong, and it is why the split was drawn here before the ruling was taken.

The typed assembly language's seven judgment forms and TAL-074's six checker phases arrive through R-05-135a's pin, which is a gap reported rather than closed. Everything a composition fixes is a field: the per-tier required sets past Tier 2's, the four admitted versions and the composer's identity.
    * **Tier 2's requirement is R-13-012's six rows and not a row more.** That entry names ABI and type well-formedness, no runtime codegen, temporal safety, definite initialization, data-race freedom and control-flow integrity; R-05-036 and R-05-038 are what make *temporal safety* the memory-safety row's move-II half, and R-05-029's own sentence is what makes control-flow integrity both of its halves.     * **The discharge kind is the axis the register fixes exactly.** R-05-029 has a derivation carry an attribute, citation or deletion-check for each obligation and R-05-037, R-05-038 and R-05-039 assign one to each facet, so a record naming the right facet and making the wrong move discharges nothing. The checker that reads the facet and ignores the move is refuted, and the generated misrouting family refuses exactly the facets R-05-037 routes through a citation. The judgment form is the axis no entry fixes, and that freeness is machine-checked rather than assumed.

A checker that walks a tier's required set in the order its machine declares it fails closed, is complete, satisfies all eight refusal clauses, and still names a later phase where an earlier one is open. The three phases before them carry three clauses of their own, one per adjacent pair, each refuted by a checker that hoists the later phase above the earlier one and each of those three accepting exactly where the specification accepts; the pair TAL-074 itself leaves open, phase 0's commitment recomputation against its version comparison, is decided on TAL-008's *rejects before proceeding* and is refutable rather than asserted.

The two width rules, IDL-023's ladder pinned at each of its own boundaries and the flag set spending its declared width exactly (WF-10); the descriptor's and the completion's encoded sizes against their declared slots, and both slots' alignment (R-12-091, and §4.2's packed rule); the index span against its own declared width and the capacity dividing it; fill to capacity and the refusal one past it (R-12-095); completion capacity against the maximum simultaneously accepted; the batch bound, the drain inside it, and the declared batch and segment maxima each attained by a named operation (R-12-098, R-12-100); notifications coalesced to one (R-12-096); segments bounded and covering the declared payload exactly (R-12-100); the activation cost inside the slot budget and the cancellation interval inside the declared time to terminal completion (R-12-101, R-12-097); cancellability being the declaration and nothing else, a non-cancellable operation declaring no cancellation at all, and the commit point among the declared points (R-12-097); labels inside the declared lattice (R-12-011); the empty-validation case as a claim rather than a default (R-12-012a); the lifecycle advancing monotonically with one terminal state, and the malformed step skipping forward without acquiring authority (R-12-094); a stale generation and a duplicate live identifier each refused and a fresh unique request accepted (R-12-092, R-12-099); that the consumer's sleep condition excludes published work, which is that condition unfolded and so says what the discipline is rather than that an implementation keeps it (R-12-096); and the two answers cancellation's race fixes, a target past its commit point too late and a non-cancellable operation never live (R-12-097).
  * **The reference bindings are Gallina, which is the cell's own first limit rather than a shortfall against it.** §0 puts base components the specification assigns to safe Rust in Gallina for the reference, and no purecap backend exists, so what the compiler emits is the interface artifact in the reference language: the descriptor and completion records, the submit, accept, cancel and notification functions a client and a server are stated over, and the constants both read. Systems-language client and server bindings wait on M1.2.
  * **The second limit is the admission checker, and it is unmoved.** R-12-010's criterion and R-18-037's campaign each name it; M6.2's composition-time half is landed and its on-device half is not, so the artifact this item produces has an admission rule to satisfy and no checker to be admitted by. Neither limit is repaired by narrowing the schema, and the profile states both at IDL-068 rather than in this cell alone.
  * **The gate's green line means emitted, compiled, axiom-free and held, and it does not mean verified.** No ring exists, no service rides one, and no canonical SPSC, lost-wakeup or typestate proof is written; R-18-037 names those and this artifact claims none of them. What the campaign decides is that the declared composition satisfies the obligations the register states over it, which is admission arithmetic and not a proof about an implementation.
  * **The seeded population is what says whether those obligations constrain the declaration, and its first run said they did not.** Fifteen of twenty-four sampled mutants survived, every one an incremented composition constant the campaign's inequalities absorbed as slack. Each was closed by adding the statement its absence exposed rather than by narrowing the population, and the statements are one discipline: **a composition declares its margin instead of leaving it to be computed**, so a bound over a declared quantity becomes an equality against a declared slack and a weakening moves a figure rather than eating headroom. **Three obligations are still plain inequalities and still absorb an incremented constant**, and each is a relation between two constants the declaration already fixes with no third standing as the margin: completion capacity against the maximum simultaneously accepted, the batch against the capacity, and an operation's commit point among its declared points. Closing them is the same act at three more places, a declared headroom in the declaration rather than a statement in the artifact, and it is not taken here. The declared fill against the descriptor slot and the completion slot, the declared activation slack against the slot budget, the declared payload slack against the segments, and the declared cancellation slack against the time to terminal completion are that device at four places; the index span pinned to its own declared width and the capacity required to divide it are R-12-091's modulo interpretation made arithmetic; the width rule pinned at each of its own boundaries is IDL-023's one admissible form, and the flag set's declared width spent exactly by its flags and its declared spare bits is WF-10's; the lifecycle successor stated as *rank plus one* rather than *rank greater* is R-12-094's sequence read as a sequence; and a fifth reference operation was added because no declared operation carried a marked scalar without a buffer reference, so the disjunction deciding the receiver-validation obligation was untested by the declaration rather than unstated by the profile.
  * **The review gate found four claims no rule reads, and all four are repaired here.** Each was true of nothing and green everywhere, which is what a gate over derived facts cannot reach. The flag set's width is now declared where WF-10 says it lives and the emitter's second ladder is gone, so the one ladder the artifact carries is IDL-023's and the rung §8 refuses is not in it; a new obligation spends the declared width exactly, so a flag set the declared width no longer holds fails to compile rather than being absorbed. The malformed step's two ends and the unstarted target are read out of R-12-094's and R-12-097's own sentences and checked against the chain, so a state inserted between them moves the artifact, which is what IDL-056's Accept says of it; today's register emits the same bytes. §9's absence claim is repaired at the profile, where M6.0b's cell records it. And **K-89**'s third fail-closed place is one now rather than a crash, the emitter naming every key it reads where the reading is refused and the rule catching what no guard reaches, with [tools/tests/test_ring.py](../tools/tests/test_ring.py) the subject module the one-per-subject convention was owed and the thing that decides the repair rather than asserting it.
  * Net change over the files this item owns outright: 1,968 lines added and 1 removed across nine, of which 643 are the generated artifact, 861 the compiler, 157 the declaration, 104 the rule and 183 the rule's test module; the profile's §4.3 is 101 more, inside a document M6.0b owns.
  * Fourteen findings, eight of them owed acts: seven are register gaps reported rather than closed, and the eighth is a binding owed in the tools. Seven were raised by the review gate's own reading of this landing rather than by the authoring, and four of those are closed by the repair the cell records above.
    * **R-12-091 names an index width and no entry says what it counts.** The mapping needs a unit before it can hold a capacity against it, and §4.2's other widths are byte widths, so the declaration counts bytes; nothing in the register decides that.
    * **R-12-091's constant list is not the set a ring declaration must fix.** This declaration fixes fourteen ring constants where that entry names six. Four of the other eight are named by some other entry and by none as a ring constant: the maximum simultaneously accepted requests and the completion capacity (R-12-095), the maximum segment count (R-12-100, and R-12-101 for the per-variant bound over it), and the slot budget the joint proof is taken against (R-12-101). The remaining four are named by no entry at all: a segment's maximum size, which R-12-100 does not fix where it fixes a maximum segment count, the index span, the completion slot's size, and its declared fill.
    * **Nothing joins R-12-094's six states to R-12-097's cancellation answers.** That entry decides the answers by where a target stands against its declared points and never says which of the six lifecycle states are live to cancel, so the mapping of its four situations onto the six is the profile's rather than the register's.
    * **No entry says which of R-12-093's eight statuses are refusals.** R-12-092's criterion has a malformed descriptor produce *one of the defined refusal completions* that closed set names, and the set is stated as one vocabulary with no refusal subset marked, so the checker reading a refusal has nothing enumerated to read.
    * **R-12-101 names the terms of the joint bound and no inequality.** It requires the composition to prove that capacity, batch size, polling cadence, slot budget and device latency jointly prevent overwrite and meet the declared progress bound, which is five quantities and no relation, so the arithmetic the campaign decides is this document's and the register audits none of it.

The proof gate's figures were measured before it, on the bytes `run.py ring check` holds the tracked artifact to.
    * **The artifact shipped a width rung the profile's own declared-parameter table says this profile does not have.** §8 states the ladder as one, two or four bytes with no eight-byte rung and names what would add one, a declaration needing a bound at or above 2^32, which does not exist; a second ladder in the emitter ended in that rung anyway, under a comment attributing both to IDL-023, and a shipped theorem machine-checked it. IDL-023 fixes no flag-set width at all: WF-10 makes a flags value a bit field of the **declared** width, and the declaration declared none, so the emitter derived one.
    * **A figure computed as the difference of two ordinal positions reads no order at all.** The malformed step's skip was `states.index(states[4]) - states.index(states[2])`, which is two for any chain of distinct states, under a docstring saying it came from the register's own order. It made IDL-056's Accept, *a state added or reordered there moves the artifact*, false of the added half, with **K-89** green throughout because that rule holds the artifact against the emitter.
    * **Nothing holds the emitter's own statement of the wire encoding against §4.2.** The emitter writes IDL-023's ladder as a Gallina literal and §4.2's packed rule as three encoded sizes; **K-89** holds what it writes against the emitter and the declaration, which is agreement with the artifact's owners and not with the mapping, so a row edited in the profile moves neither. This is the drift the rung walked through, and the binding stays owed rather than repaired by a rule invented in the profile.
    * **A rule claiming three fail-closed places had two.** The checker caught the emitter's own refusal and nothing else, and [check.py](../tools/check.py) carries no `except` at all, so an owner shaped in a way no guard named ended the whole run. It was reachable: the key guard covered eight declaration keys and omitted the one `emit()` reads for the label lattice, and the arity guard read two states where the emitter indexed five.
    * **A seeded population figure is read by no rule, and this one was measured against a working copy rather than against the artifact that landed.** The cell said 191 where the tracked artifact yielded 215, a fifth of the population absent from the sentence describing it, with every gate green. It is re-measured at each repair now rather than carried.
    * **Where a value outside R-12-092's enumeration rides is undecided by the register.** That entry enumerates what a descriptor carries and says nothing about a value the enumeration does not admit or about how a delegated buffer's payload is encoded, so reading the enumeration as a placement rather than as a deletion is a decision; IDL-023a took it inside a §4.2 requirement before §8 carried it.
    * **Two entries disagree about whether a descriptor carries a session generation.** R-12-092 enumerates **exactly** six members and no generation is among them; R-12-099 binds every descriptor to a session generation and refuses every old-generation one, and R-12-092's own criterion has a stale generation produce a refusal completion, which a server cannot decide without reading the descriptor's own. The generated record carries the field and §4.2 has no row for it, so the encoded size does not spend it and the field is inert until one of the two entries moves.
    * **What the sample does not reach is what the register states no relation over, which is not the same as what it does not state.** Each of the four survivors is one of four things and none is a defect in the statements: a flow label, which nothing can constrain while the lattice has no owner anywhere; the count of an operation's marked scalars, which nothing bounds above while the receiver-validation declaration has no vocabulary; the count of a cancellable operation's declared cancellation points, which R-12-097 requires to exist and bounds in no direction; and a derived count the emitter writes, which the prover cannot reach and **K-89** does, a hand-edited count no longer being what `run.py ring emit` writes. One site outside the sample is the sharper case and belongs here: `ring_session_generation` occurs once in the artifact, at its own definition, so no statement reads it, and R-12-091 **does** name a session generation among the constants a ring instance carries. The gap there is in the relation rather than in the enumeration, and no statement over the declaration alone can close it.
  * Exit evidence, re-measured at the repair pass rather than carried from the landing: the three host gates green with all **100 mutants killed**, the repair path holding and the registry covered at **88 rules**; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.75 and ruff 0.16.5; the tools' own tests green at **330 cases over 37 modules**, of which [test_ring.py](../tools/tests/test_ring.py)'s **5** are this item's; the proof gate at **3,360 constants**, **33 of them this file's**, every one closed under the global context, with no `Require`, no `Compute` and no top-level axiom; `run.py ring check` reporting the tracked artifact byte-identical to what the emitter writes from the declaration and the **4** register entries it reads; and a seeded sample of **32 of the 207 mutants over nine operators, 28 killed, 4 surviving and none stillborn**, stillborn reported apart and being zero, every survivor real and each named above. **Both verdicts are the prover's alone and the sample is a one-oracle result**: [Vectors.v](../tools/quickchick/Vectors.v) requires the partition, executive, probe and endpoint sources and never this file, so the generated input side reaches none of it and every prover-accepted mutant survives the second oracle by construction, which is a measurement rather than a complaint. **The whole population is owed and is not run**: this sample alone was still running at **35 minutes** of wall clock against a guest carrying another lane's population, and the population is six and a half times it, so it is spread evenly across all nine operators rather than taken as a prefix of one. No `run.py evidence` sweep is owed: it measures the model build, the property harness, the profile sweep, the corpus, the devicetree and the reference, and this item touches none of them. No register, ISA-profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the co-read ledger is unmoved; the repair re-opens R-05-150's reading of §4.3 at IDL-027, IDL-051, IDL-055 and IDL-060, which is where this item's own Tier A landing already puts it.

A rule holding a generated file is not a substitute for reading what the file is generated to satisfy.
  * Landed: Tier A.
* [ ] **M6.5 · Port one copy-based and one DMA service through the ring contract**
  * The copy-based service carries the SPSC, notification, and capacity proofs; the DMA service adds extent, cancellation, teardown, and quiescence; together they validate descriptor sizes, completion capacity, and the generated-proof interfaces (R-18-037) before other servers ride the contract.
  * **Split at entry**: a booting system needs one service over the contract, and the DMA half's extent, cancellation, teardown and quiescence proofs are what a second one adds. Neither is grandfathered, which is the milestone's own rule and is unchanged by the split.

No proof is asked of the weights, which do not execute; the executing code is the server's GEMM and attention kernels, and they carry their obligations whichever weights they read.

### M7 · Full emulated system

The composed stack boots in the spec's §9 order on the composed **Sail emulator** (§10), which with the fast emulator struck at M2 is the one whole machine this plan builds; the Wasm track keeps running the same components host-side for iteration, and the boot is re-run by the item that owns it rather than by an unattended runner, S14 having declined on a measurement.
The composed system is also the first artifact able to measure **allocation churn**, so the measurement is booked here: the static-composition low-churn argument and the static-code-overlay deferral both wait on the same measured roster, and the elective applications widen the measurement later rather than gating it.
The same roster carries the ring-parameter measurement: queue depths, batch sizes, notification cadence, and slot budgets are read off the composed system against the §12 per-operation accounting, so the contract's constants are chosen from a measured roster rather than estimated per service.

* [ ] **M7.1 · Boot the composed stack on the golden emulator** · 18 h, range 12–24 · 1.7% · I
  * Re-priced once and then half back: the second executor is struck, so the boot is one machine rather than two adjudicated by hand, and the rerun is a clause of this item rather than an unattended runner's, S14 having declined on a measurement. The cell is deliberately not re-priced for it here, a rerun being a command rather than a build, and whether that reading holds is this item's own to take at entry.
* [ ] **M7.2 · Measure allocation churn across the composed roster** · 4.5 h, range 3–6 · 0.4% · I
  * The kernel-mediated teardown rate and the capability-bearing footprint swept per teardown, read off the composed roster and compared against the background slot class and quarantine pool the composition sized. R-08-008a is what reads the figure: the sweep's economics are booked there as an expectation about that rate, and this is the reading that settles it. Naming the reader adds nothing to the item, the measurement and the roster being the ones it already takes.
* [ ] **M7.3 · Measure ring parameters across the composed roster** · 4.5 h, range 3–6 · 0.4% · I
  * Queue depths, batch sizes, notification cadence, and slot budgets read off the composed system against the §12 per-operation accounting.

**M7 subtotal:** 27 h · 2% · open range 18–36 h.

### RTL track

The RTL track, in parallel from the M0 freeze:

* [ ] **R1 · Curate scalar CVA6-CHERI RTL and required platform devices**
  * CVA6-CHERI re-parameterized to the 64+1-bit dialect and curated per the profile and absence contract (§11: MMU deleted, static-only prediction, TSO store buffer), plus the RoT core, tag-carrying interconnect, boot ROM, UART, and block device; absence-contract state enumeration and synthesis-configuration provenance recorded at first elaboration.
  * Split at entry, and the split is by what each part is written against rather than by [the delta's](rtl-reparameterization-delta.md) three kinds, which sort the imported datapath and reach only two of the three children. R1a is the delta's third kind alone, *authoring against the model*, and it needs no imported file, no datapath and no gitlink, so it proceeds against a definition already in the tree. R1b is the delta's other two kinds together, following a parameter and replacing the constants a parameter does not reach, plus the blocks no configuration removes, and it is where the per-site count lives. R1c is the devices beside the core, which the delta does not read at all and which have no definition in this repository yet. Re-priced at the split, and the two open children come to more than the cell they replace, which is what the delta measured rather than a slip: `CLEN = 2 * XLEN` collapses across the 25 files of `core/` the delta books for it, and the caches that dominate the item have no disabling parameter at all.
  * **What stands is the reading, the measurement, and the format whole.** The upstreams are pinned, their licences are read at their own files, the re-parameterization delta is enumerated per site, the absence-contract evidence is recorded at a first elaboration that comes back clean, and [rtl/](../rtl/) carries the curated configuration, the capability-format package and the provenance record. What is not yet curated is the datapath itself: the caches, the store buffer, and the platform devices.
  * **The upstreams are pinned and each licence is read at the file at its own pin, and every one is permissive.** Six of the nineteen gitlinks under `upstream/` are this milestone's, and the reading below is over those six: the CHERI-CVA6 datapath (`lowRISC/cva6-cheri` at `36a1dc5c`), the tag controller (`Capabilities-Limited/axi_cheri_tagcontroller` at `173646d5`), the bring-up SoC (`lowRISC/mocha` at `ef1370c1`), and the three RoT references (`lowRISC/opentitan` at `bf4a2b24`, `lowRISC/ibex` at `8b8ee086`, `microsoft/cheriot-ibex` at `930feb29`). The two Solderpad readings and the five Apache readings carry no reciprocity, no field-of-use restriction and no source-disclosure condition, and the CERN Open Hardware family is on no route this plan takes, so this milestone incorporates without a decision of the kind M1.1a, M4.1a and M6.0a each had to take. [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) carries the rows, the Solderpad election quoted from the instrument, and the two readings that do not resolve at a single file.
  * **The tag object and the commit it names are different objects, and the pin is the commit.** `lowRISC/mocha` annotates its releases, so `git ls-remote --tags --refs` prints the tag object `435618bf` against `refs/tags/v0.1.1`, while `git ls-files -s upstream` prints the gitlink as `ef1370c1`, the commit that object points at, and a clone asked for the tag by name warns that the ref is not a commit before landing there. A row transcribed from the remote listing would name the tag object and disagree with the gitlink, which is the reading trap M1.4 records.
  * **The tag ladder is re-taken at the tags, dated 2026-08-31, and the pin is confirmed at the tag object rather than at the prose beside it.** The `v0.1.1` annotated tag reads *"Release that supports booting CHERI Linux. Minor release includes quality of life improvement on FPGA resets."*, tagged 2026-07-10, and it is the newest tag the repository carries; `v0.1.0` below it reads *"Release that supports booting CHERI Linux with tag v0.1.0. This is also known as the second minimal viable product (MVP-2)"*, tagged 2026-06-23, which is where §11's MVP-2 identification lands. The README's release-timeline table at the pin schedules `0.1.0` as MVP-2 and RC-1 for December 2026 under no version number at all, so the ladder the table states and the ladder the tags state are one ladder. The roadmap lag §11 records is the availability marker alone, which sits on MVP-1 and on nothing above it.
  * **The re-parameterization delta is measured and stands as [its own document](rtl-reparameterization-delta.md).** It enumerates the sites per file and line across the format package, the width identity outside it, the permission, object-type and mode sites in the functional units, and the tag plane and bus user bits, stating per site whether it follows a parameter, carries a literal a parameter does not reach, changes shape, is rewritten against the model, is deleted, or is behaviour the imported tree does not have. Two findings pace the item. `CLEN = 2 * XLEN` collapses onto `XLEN`, one declaration the delta books across 25 files of `core/`, taking a sixteen-way store rotate into an upper half that no longer exists with it, which the delta books whole because at `CLEN == XLEN` the table has no referent at all. And the bounds encoding is **not** a narrowing of the imported one, the frozen format having no internal-exponent flag and stealing no mantissa bits, so `set_cap_reg_bounds`, `encode_bounds`, `decode_bounds`, the representable-limit comparison and the malformed predicate are written against `cap_common.sail` rather than edited, and five behaviours the frozen definition requires have no imported counterpart at all.
  * **The absence-contract evidence is recorded at first elaboration, which is where §11 puts it.** The curated C-class configuration elaborates the CVA6-CHERI top under Verilator 5.032 at **zero errors**, against a baseline of the same tree at its own stock CHERI configuration. **It is not warning-free and never was**: the same invocation emits **272 warnings on the curated arm and 229 on the baseline**, and the figure this item first recorded said zero because `cmd_elaborate` prints the elaborator's output only on a non-zero exit while `-Wno-fatal` keeps warnings out of that exit, so the run measured the absence of output. Two of the 272 are the authored configuration package's own, an `unsigned'()` cast at each of two `bit` fields, which `run.py rtl lint` cannot reach because its authored set names the capability package alone. Eleven module types disappear and **none appears**: `bht`, `btb` and `ras` (A-04, A-05, A-06), `cva6_mmu`, `cva6_ptw`, `cva6_tlb` and `cva6_shared_tlb` (A-14), `pmp_entry`, `amo_buffer` (A-13), `compressed_decoder`, and `perf_counters`. Cell instances fall from 416 to 271 and declared variables from 5,862 to 5,071. **The synthesis-configuration provenance is not restated here**, R-15-103 asking that it bind a build rather than a reading: it is [the provenance record](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md), one row per claimed absence naming the parameters that remove it, held against both the absence contract and [the configuration package](../rtl/vos_c_class_config_pkg.sv) by rule K-76, and re-derived on demand by [`tools/run.py rtl`](../tools/vos/cli/rtl.py).
  * **What a parameter does not reach is the honest half of the same measurement, and it arrives by three different doors.** A-09 and A-10 have no parameter at all: `cva6_icache`, the five write-through data-cache modules, and 84 `prim_ram_1p`, 18 `sram_cache` and 18 `sram` instances all survive the curated configuration, so the caches are authoring work rather than a setting. A-11 has none for a different reason and is not work here: its tag cache sits in the imported tag controller rather than in the core, which is outside the netlist this record is taken over. And two rows a parameter *does* take leave a residue standing beside what it removed: `NrPMPEntries` at zero removes `pmp_entry` and leaves the `pmp` and `pmp_data_if` shells, and setting the hybrid extension off makes the capability-mode path unreachable and leaves its signals in the imported source. The three that are **work** are the caches, the PMP shells and the mode signals, and they are booked in [the record's own §4](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md#4-what-no-parameter-reaches-and-what-that-costs) rather than counted twice; A-11 is booked in the row itself, as a fact about where the structure is.
  * **Curated RTL has a home and it holds only what this repository wrote.** [rtl/](../rtl/) carries the authored half and the imported cores stay behind their gitlinks, so nothing binds this repository that a pin already covers: the configuration package, the capability-format package, and the provenance record are there, and no imported file is copied in. It takes the repository's default line endings rather than `model/`'s verbatim rule, there being no upstream here to stay byte-identical to. The lane that drives it is [`tools/run.py rtl`](../tools/vos/cli/rtl.py), and Verilator is pinned at 5.032 with its own [THIRD-PARTY.md](../THIRD-PARTY.md) row.
  * **The scope tension is resolved: the fabric is curated as a route-(b)/(c) functional reference here and authored under route (a) afterwards.** The tag-carrying interconnect is a net-new block R-15-092 requires authored in Kôika or Kami and proven, and taking that inside R1 would open route (a) before the co-simulation gate, which §11 explicitly says nothing is owed before. So the pinned `axi-cheri-tagcontroller` is elaborated and differentially tested as the functional reference it already is, and the authored, proven block is the closing act it always was; what changes is that the reading is written down rather than left equally available in both directions. The three-route ladder exists precisely so a block can sit at evidence tier before it sits at proof tier, and R-15-092's *authored* is a statement about the artifact of record rather than about every artifact that stands where it will stand.
  * **What that costs and where it is recorded.** No Coq or Kami authoring enters R1's children, which is what keeps them priced as curation, and the closing proof is deferred with route (a)'s other blocks rather than being given up. The disposition is recorded in [the record's own §4a](../rtl/synthesis-provenance.md#4a-the-tag-fabrics-route-recorded-so-it-is-not-derived-twice) rather than only here, so an auditor meeting the curated fabric finds the reading instead of deriving it.

The vectors cross as **text**, so no adapter sits between the two implementations, neither is compiled against the other's types, and a disagreement names a line a person reads on both sides. The two halves live in [tools/cheri-equiv/](../tools/cheri-equiv/) because a file-reading testbench is a checking harness and not something this repository would synthesize.
    * **Exit evidence.** The cross-check is green at **658,659 vectors with no disagreement**, line for line over 658,662 lines and over thirteen kinds: decode 540,672, `setCapBounds` 27,347, the address setter 16,000, each offset form 16,000, the representability check 16,000, the bounds check 16,000, the permission lattice 4,128, sealing 512, the null transform across the memory interface 2,000, an integer read as a capability 2,000, and the accessors over the decoded bounds 2,000. Two of those sweeps are **exhaustive over their own domain** rather than sampled: decode over the whole 19-bit bounds encoding, every exponent field against every base mantissa against every stored top mantissa and each at its own address, which is the totality R-15-007a states decode over; and `perms_narrow` over every one of the 4,096 masks the expanded bitmap can take, which is the whole of what `candperm` can hand it.     * Six findings.
      * **`setCapBounds`'s containment property holds over a domain nothing states, and this is the third implementation to measure it.** Over the 22,152 vectors with `base` at or below `top` at or below 2^36 the derived region contains the requested one without exception; over the 3,296 with a requested top above it, 48 do not, the decoded top having wrapped below its own base. Both implementations agree on every one, so it is the algebra's rather than either implementation's, and it is unreachable with a tag, `CSetBounds` clearing the tag on precisely the test that fails there. What is owed is that **R-15-007a's representation-correctness proof state the condition**. Reported and not closed: the same gap M2.1 booked from the emulator side, measured again from the RTL side.
      * **The same domain falsifies a sentence the model states about itself, which is that gap seen from the encode side.** `cap_common.sail`'s header says the effective exponent runs to `cap_max_E` = 30 "so *k* + 1 runs to 31 and the five bits are spent exactly". In the wrap domain `setCapBounds` returns an internal exponent of **31** on 1,437 of the 27,347 narrowing vectors, and `capToEncCap` writes 31 + 1 into five bits, truncating to zero and encoding the *denormal* case. Decode's own `min(cap_max_E, ...)` clamp catches it on the way back and the two implementations agree line for line, so nothing is wrong with either; the sentence describes the stated domain and reads as describing the format.
      * **A vector population off entropy alone cannot tell one clause of `setCapBounds` from its absence.** Inside the exponent-increment branch the base's lost-significance flag reads bit 0 of the mantissa as it stood *before* the recompute; reading the recomputed one instead reproduced all 647,179 vectors on the first population. The branch fires with neither mantissa having lost significance only where base and top are both aligned to a large power of two with the top below the base, and there that clause alone decides the exactness verdict. The sweep that reaches it is 840 lines, which turned a surviving mutant into one killed on 33 lines.

No count of the apertures is stated here, that being a figure nothing computes and the defect F-205 named. The **SoC top follows the map** rather than the other way round, which is why splitting them is what unblocks this: a top authored before the map fixes the map by implication, which is the defect this plan names one milestone over at M6.4.
    * **The RoT core leaves this item, and what decides it is that §2's uniformity argument has three legs and none of them is the pipeline.** They are that one Sail model and one CHERI-CompCert backend cover it, that the capability format is shared so the boot handoff crosses no format seam, and that 64-bit reach keeps measured boot clear of the 4 GiB windowing a 32-bit root would need. Each is an **architecture**-layer property and each is already carried: one model parameterized by class, one authored format package R1a cross-checks against it, and a composed RoT configuration this repository already runs at hart 15 (M3.1). Microarchitecture is per-class by construction, R-15-108 freezing issue width and pipeline depth per class, so two classes with different pipelines is this design's ordinary case rather than an exception. Realizing the RoT as a minimal configuration of the curated C-class datapath would assert a fourth leg §2 never argues for.
    * **Read the other way the proof argument runs against sharing rather than for it, which is the reverse of how the uniformity claim is usually spent.** R-15-097 states that no RTL ⊑ Sail artifact exists for a full application-class core and calls that the least-built layer of the stack, so sharing the datapath at the RoT inherits an *unbuilt* proof at the one block R-15-194 puts in the TCB and calls verified. A refinement's cost is the store buffer, the forwarding write buffer, the bus shim, the multi-cycle interlocks and the debug module rather than the instruction count, and an in-order cache-free core carries none of them, which makes a minimal core a different order of difficulty and not a smaller instance of the same one. It is also the one block where R-15-091's primary closing vehicle is reachable today: S7 recorded that what Kami and Kôika ship as example cores is RV32, in-order and cache-free, which is the shape of a minimal root of trust and precisely not the shape of the imported datapath, and §11's own existence proof rides that spine.

        * **The silence this item priced its whole decision against is narrower than five sites said it was, and the enclosing region's PMA is why.** `pmaCheck` decides an access before `checked_mem_read` and `checked_mem_write` reach the RAM fall-through (sys/mem.sail), so what falls through is only what the region already permits. The serial port and the block device are in an IO region that is readable and writable, so a load and a store inside either are the RAM path's; the boot ROM's region is readable and neither writable nor executable, so a load there reads zero while a store or an instruction fetch is an access fault. The over-broad sentence stood in this cell, in [platform_config.sail](../model/model/core/platform_config.sail) and in all three shipped compositions, and all five are repaired against the regions the composition actually declares.
        * **The boot ROM's extent is declared inside a region that refuses an instruction fetch**, which is the one access an SoC boot ROM exists for. `executable` reads false on that region and has since before this item, the attested devicetree sharing it. Reported and not closed: turning it on makes the attested tree's own bytes executable, so it is a composition decision to take with the top that fetches from the ROM rather than ahead of it, and it is R1c-ii's along with the device.
        * **An enumeration was presented as a grep result and was not that grep's result.** The finding above said *boot ROM* occurs at R-09-003 and R-09-036; searching the register returns three, the third being R-16-008c's acceptance. The conclusion is unmoved, R-16-008c's being the metal-mask ROM R-09-002 measures, so no entry obliges an SoC boot ROM; what was wrong is that a sentence stated what a search returns and stated a different set. The clause beside it holds as re-measured: zero hits for `serial port`, `console`, `block device` and `blkdev`, and each of the ten `serial` hits something other than a port.
        * **The repair for F-205 replaced a stale enumeration with a hand-maintained count, which is the defect F-205 names.** The parent bullet said the composition declares *eleven apertures*, and nothing computes eleven: K-94's own figure is over three files at once, and [counts.py](../tools/vos/checks/counts.py)'s claim table owns no aperture count. It is deleted rather than owned, the bullet now naming what this landing put there, which stays true when a twelfth window arrives. The same shape at M0.8a's landed cell, a ROM region size this item moves, is repaired in the same act.
        * **The whole-map check is a list somebody maintains, and two artifacts said it was built.** `declared_apertures` is written out one row per window; each row reads its own aperture's `supported` key and its own base, so a window switched off leaves the check by itself, but a window the composition gains joins only when a row is written. The Sail file's comment said exactly that and this cell and the findings register both dropped the qualifier. Both are repaired, and the half that was hoped rather than held now is: **K-94 gained a third hop** reading that list against what the shipped configurations declare, fired by deleting the serial port's row, which it reports three times, once per shipped file.
        * **The obligation the disjointness refusal fail-stops on is filed nowhere.** R-15-002b places every aperture inside the 36-bit space with none at a wide power-of-two offset and makes the placement a stated constraint on the attested devicetree; it does not say two apertures may not share a byte, and no other entry does. The refusal borrowed that entry's word *dense* to carry a claim the entry does not make. The message and the function's comment now state the ground they have, which is that two devices cannot answer at one address and an attested tree naming one address twice describes a die that does not exist. Reported and not closed: requiring it is a register act, and this is the lens this item applies to itself at the serial port's second arm.
        * **A composition comment narrated its own revision.** The ROM region's paragraph said what R1c-i changed there while the region's opening comment already states where each half is. It carries the durable half alone now, that the two are held apart at composition and again at the write.
        * **The evidence sweep's model revision is a stamp taken at cmake configure, not a reading of the tree it prints from**, which is what a note quoting it has to know. `git describe` runs once at configure and the emulator carries what it said (env.py's `git_dir`, which translates a lane's `.git` pointer through `wslpath` so the guest can resolve it at all), so a lane that configured over an edited tree keeps printing `-dirty` however clean the checkout has since become. Measured both ways rather than assumed: with that same `GIT_DIR` the guest resolves this worktree and names exactly the paths git names on the host, and `run.py model reference` over a checkout `git status --porcelain` reports empty still prints the marker. So the marker is honest about the tree that generated the reference and the misreading available is *when* it was taken, which is why this cell quotes no revision.
      * Landed: Tier B under **K-94**.
    * [ ] **R1c-ii · The platform devices and the SoC top** · 9 h, range 6–12 · 0.8% · X · beside the M8a chain
      * Boot ROM, UART and block device taken from `lowRISC/mocha` as route-(c) functional references, on the disposition every imported core here already carries, with the SoC top authored over the map R1c-i writes.
      * What the arm not taken forfeits: taking Mocha's top whole would import an address map from a design that fields an MMU this profile deletes, which is the same mismatch §11 already records for its CHERI-Linux boot and would put it in the interconnect rather than in the software stack.

  * The shared corpus passes under Verilator with the commit-trace diff against the golden model, `rvfi` the hook; a riscv-formal-style BMC smoke on the curated scalar core runs here as cheap bring-up evidence (R-15-094), distinct from the deferred FEV and refinement work.
  * **Re-priced because S11 supplies the rig rather than this item building one.** The corpus stops being authored, the shrinking is automatic, and one protocol runs across the golden model and the RTL, which is what §10's *one CI runner diffing one trace format across three executors* describes and has had no instrument behind it. **This is also where the struck M2's second-executor job lands**: an independent implementation to disagree with, arriving as the artifact that ships rather than as a second model.

This item authors the macro architecture those five imply, as the input to R5.
  * The commodity oxide-channel roadmap de-risks the deposition chemistry, the thermal budget, the stack-and-replace flow, and multi-deck stacking. It does not de-risk the gain cell, and it does not de-risk the macro at all.

  * R-15-247m's obligations as one package: measured usable density from a **repaired megabit-class macro** carrying complete tag, ECC, refresh, discharge, and routing overhead; the four per-class latency constants; the retention corner as a measured interval; bank-granularity tag discharge with a characterized worst-corner dwell and a dwell-invariant fail-stop indication; and the simultaneous-activation envelope against droop, thermal coupling, and endurance at the M0.17 bank granularity.
  * These are **qualification gates, not re-open conditions**: a macro that misses one is a part that does not qualify, not an architecture that reverts.

**RTL subtotal:** 159 h · 15% · 19 h complete · open range 89–191 h.

### M8–M10 and later

**The gate is two gates, because it was one rendezvous over two chains.** M8 as written is M7 *and* R3, so it is set by whichever of the two slips and a slip on either propagates to the milestone this document calls its most important. There is no technical reason for the weld, and both halves keep every exit criterion they had.

* [ ] **M8a · Software MVP: M7 complete** · 2 h, range 1–3 · 0.2% · I
  * The whole base system running in emulation: the composed stack boots in the spec's §9 order on the golden emulator, cross-checked, corpus green, with the roster's allocation-churn and ring parameters measured off it.
* [ ] **M8b · Co-simulation MVP: R3 complete** · 2 h, range 1–3 · 0.2% · I
  * The RTL artifact of record in hand, corpus-green under Verilator and booting the same image to the same console and event digests.
  * Everything after the two is hardware realization and hardening.
* [ ] **M9 · Synthesize and boot scalar purecap on FPGA** · 30 h, range 20–40 · 2.8% · X
  * Synthesize the R3 artifact for the board (§11); the purecap golden-model images (M1–M7) boot on it directly, differentially tested against M7; and the exit criterion is **M0.10's replacement pair rather than an ISA-test suite**, the differential corpus green on the board and the composed image reaching the same console and event digests as the golden emulator. M0.6f excluded `riscv-tests` outright, so *ISA tests green* names a corpus this machine refuses in full, and no item below generates a replacement: §0's Isla derivation of ISA test vectors from the frozen model is the route and it carries no cell.
* [ ] **M10 · Extend CHERI checks across V/M/FEC datapaths** · 45 h, range 30–60 · 4.1% · X
  * Extend the V/M/FEC datapaths to capability checks (the genuine new RTL, §18), the scalar core and purecap software are already in hand from M1/M9, so the FPGA then matches the golden model across all core classes.
  * The RTL is net-new and the design under it is not, which §11 states and this cell does not restate: what the CHERI-RVV line settles is the per-element question and not any of the hardware, so the estimate stands whole. Where it reaches this item is the amortized check, which M0.8b's per-element discard and R-15-085a's two vector-memory rows both price as though the question were closed, and which is the boundary a magnitude would come from if R-17-041's ever acquire one.
* [ ] **M9a · Discharge and refresh droop model** · 12.5 h, range 8–17 · 1.1% · X
  * Close the simultaneous-activation set against measured IR-drop, thermal-coupling, and power-signature limits at composition capacity and at the frozen bank granularity, then feed the admitted set back into the §11 mode-transition budget and R-15-247g.
  * It lands after both gates because it wants measured silicon behaviour that M0.17's exploration and R5's macro evidence only bound; the item is named in the tool's after-M8b list beside R4, R5, M9, M10, and the post-M10 obligations so neither gate figure quietly absorbs it.

Everything past M10, the CHERI-CompCert **secure-compilation criterion** (robust preservation; the *functional* backend already landed in M1), the **R-05-023a validation instrument** (the decompilation-into-logic producer of the §5 post-CompCert records, whose first non-fast-path artifact also opens the checker-binary question booked in [architectural-alternatives.md](architectural-alternatives.md)), the binary-level constant-time verifier, the masking obligations (the *d*-probing and composition theorems on the crypto artifacts with the Coco-class netlist discharge, R-05-004a, R-15-053a, §4), the R-16-008f detection theorem, the certifying-Rust *certificate* mode, the full VST refinement proofs, WCET, and RTL ⊑ Sail refinement, is the hardening program of §5/§6/§18, each piece replacing a golden-model component *in place* and checked against the reference this plan produces.
Three statements are owed at that program's opening rather than inside it. The **five fallback positions**: the RTL ⊑ Sail arrow is the least-built layer of the stack (R-17-039) and the hedge deletions spend it (R-17-037), so what each of the PMP-backstop, IOMMU, MTE, shadow-stack and initialization-plane rejections becomes under a late or partial close is stated per rejection and read per block (R-17-039a). What the opening owes there is the *instantiation* rather than the writing: which blocks landed on which rung, what the FEV evidence covers on each, and which imported cores still stand at evidence tier, partial coverage being what the three-route ladder produces by construction. The **reduction theorem's verification plan**, the combined-adversary route being already selected (R-17-058d), so the opening states a plan beside the masking obligations rather than a choice. And the **R-15-053a bring-up characterization**, rehearsed on the FPGA and executed at first silicon, the rehearsal validating the harness and never the axiom, an FPGA's leakage being the wrong silicon.

**The opening's statement and the work it states are priced separately, and the four items after it are the work.** R-05-004a's masked datapath and R-17-058d's reduction carry the hardening program's only first-of-kind formalization, and the three Coq objects §4 names do not cost alike: their nearest start-froms are a released machine-checked development in another prover, a body of proofs in another prover's logic, and nothing machine-checked anywhere. The composition theorem therefore takes two cells, its two halves being the pair a single figure would blend. What sits in a cell here is the Coq object alone; the tool ensemble beside it produces evidence and enters no trust base (§4), and the rest of the hardening program stays prose, so the grand total covers the golden model, the RTL track and these four rather than the program.

* [ ] **Post-M10 · Publish opening hardening obligations** · 6 h, range 4–8 · 0.6% · I
  * Instantiate R-17-039a's five fallback positions against the route coverage the program actually has, and state the reduction-theorem verification plan, the masking obligations, and the first-silicon characterization plan.
  * The first of those four is re-priced rather than dropped: the positions are written and the ladder makes them per-block, so what is left is reading each against the rungs admitted for each core, which is a shorter act than the writing was.
  * **The masking half of that plan has a machine-checked source to transcribe for one of its three theorems, and the source is in the wrong prover.** A 2026 line of work carries **PINI-class composition theorems for arithmetic masking over prime fields** in **Lean 4**, with the composed pipeline cases, the renewal argument for fresh masking between stages, the k-stage generalization, and the Barrett-reduction bounds beneath them, stated with no admitted goals and instantiated against a published post-quantum accelerator to diagnose a real flaw in its masking. That is precisely the PINI-class composition theorem §4 books as original formalization, and transcribing a machine-checked development is a different act from transcribing a paper.
  * Four caveats decide how much of that is transcribable, and the first two are structural. It is **Lean 4 and not Rocq**, so it is a source and never an import, the load-bearing artifact still being the Coq statement and §4's claim that no proof-assistant masking development exists *in Coq* untouched. It covers the **arithmetic** half rather than the Boolean half the AES and Keccak datapaths need, where the EasyCrypt gadget proofs remain the nearest source, so the two halves have machine-checked sources in two different provers and neither is this project's. It touches **R-17-058d's ineffective-fault reduction theorem not at all**. And how far it is relied on is a dated reading under R-18-001a: read 2026-08-23, the development is released as MIT-licensed repositories with archival deposits, sorry-free and carrying no non-standard axiom, pinned to Lean 4 v4.30.0-rc1 against one Mathlib revision and reproducible by a shipped script, while carrying no independent replication and stating its own scope as first-order, one probe per wire. So its statements and proof architecture are worth transcribing and its proofs are not something this repository stands on.
* [ ] **Post-M10 · Author the probing model in Coq** · 28 h, range 16–40 · 2.6% · X
  * R-15-053a's statement mechanized: a glitch- and transition-extended *d*-probing model over a gate-level circuit representation with its composition notion named, in the SSProve/FCF probabilistic ambient §5 already takes as this project's Coq-native destination for game-based reductions. That ambient is the item's one import and everything over it is authored: the circuit and leakage representation, the probe expansion the two extensions define, the adversary, and the sanity lemmas that make the statement usable rather than merely well-formed.
  * **The prior art supplies definitions and no encoding** (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23). The robust probing model and PINI are stated in the literature and operationalized in SILVER, PROLEAD, MATCHI and IronMask, every one of which is a checker over a circuit rather than a development in a logic; the only released machine-checked wire model is the Lean one the item above reads, and it is over ℤ_q at first order. No proof assistant carries the glitch and transition extensions, and none ties either to the netlist the R-15-053a discharge names.
  * The range is wider than this plan's factor of two because the cost is dominated by getting a statement right rather than by proving anything over it. This is the crown jewel whose wrongness no theorem beneath it catches, so the item is priced as specification authoring under R-05-150 review and not as proof engineering.
* [ ] **Post-M10 · Transcribe the arithmetic composition half** · 24 h, range 14–34 · 2.2% · X
  * PF-PINI over ℤ_q, the renewal argument that a fresh mask between pipeline stages erases the earlier stage's multiplicity, its k-stage generalization, and the algebraic-to-hardware Barrett bridge, re-proved in Coq against the Lean development's statements.
  * **The source is an artifact rather than a paper, which is what this cell rests on**: eighteen kernel-verified theorems over five files in the composition repository, MIT-licensed with an archival deposit beside it, so its terms permit whatever a transcription could carry, no provenance fork of the M1.1a kind arises, and nothing is vendored (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23).
  * **Two gaps decide the cell and both are the source's own.** It is first-order by its stated scope, one probe per wire, where R-05-004a's obligation is *d*-th order, so the order generalization is authored rather than transcribed. And Lean 4 with Mathlib is not Coq with mathcomp: statements and proof architecture cross, tactic proofs and the algebraic hierarchy beneath them do not, so what this item buys is a reachable target and a proof plan rather than a port. It is the cheapest of the four for exactly that reason, and a transcription costed as though it were an original formalization, or an original formalization costed as though it were a transcription, is the error this split exists to prevent.
* [ ] **Post-M10 · Author the Boolean composition half** · 40 h, range 22–58 · 3.7% · X
  * PINI at arbitrary order over GF(2) on the NI and SNI ladder beneath it, glitch-robust, for the HPC and DOM gadget families the datapath carries, instantiated on the AES S-box and the Keccak χ the R-05-004a secret path runs through.
  * **The nearest source is EasyCrypt's Boolean masking proofs, and it is a source in the weakest of the three senses** (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23). EasyCrypt is its own logic and its own checker rather than a Coq library, its masking line is NI and SNI over GF(2^n) and predates PINI, and the 2026 movement in the area is in checkers rather than in provers. One 2026 result is pinned for its technique and not its artifact, probing security routed through conditional independence in a separation logic for exactly that, and it is stated on paper with no mechanization.
  * **This is the largest of the four.** It is the half the AES and Keccak datapaths cannot do without, it is original in the prover and near-original in content at arbitrary order, and the transcription beside it buys it nothing: a prime field's uniformity arguments do not carry to GF(2), which is why the prime-field case was the gap and the Boolean one was not.
* [ ] **Post-M10 · Author the R-17-058d reduction theorem** · 36 h, range 18–54 · 3.3% · X
  * The selected route's combined claim, machine-checked on the artifact: every fault the single-fault-per-activation hypothesis admits on the masked datapath's activation is either effective, landing in the R-16-008f detection statement, or confined to one share's computation, landing inside the R-15-053a probing bound at unreduced order.
  * **Nothing machine-checked exists for it in any prover, and that is the reading rather than a gap in it** (R-18-001a, read 2026-08-23). Every verification vehicle in the class is a standalone checker: VERICA and FIVER, and the general random-fault and random-probing model beside them, whose verification is delivered as extensions of VERICA and IronMask. The constructions carrying their own combined model are CAPA, M&M and the CINI-class gadgets R-17-058d declines as the pin. The nearest mechanization is on the fault side alone and one level above where this design states it, the every-fault-detected-or-ineffective shape carried in Coq at CompCert's RTL level, which is a template for the statement and supplies none of the combined case.
  * **The range is the widest of the four and the widening is the pricing**, this plan's convention being to price a softer class by widening its own items rather than by a second figure over the total. What narrows it is that the theorem is small where the others are large, a case split over two axioms already stated rather than a new model. What does not narrow is that it stands over two objects this plan does not cost, the R-16-008f detection statement and the countermeasure's own construction, so what is priced here is the reduction and never its premises.

**M8–M10 subtotal:** 225.5 h · 21% · open range 134–317 h.

## Build-loop instruments

These items sit outside the milestone subtotals but inside the grand total, and each carries its own estimate cell like any other item. Every change must be benchmarked before adoption; canonical `-O2` RelWithDebInfo remains the exit criterion.

* [x] **Initial check/emit/FAST tooling** · 0.9 h actual · 0.1%
* [x] **I0 · Standardize the tools on one language** · 6 h actual · 0.6%
  * Every tool in [tools/](../tools/) is Python 3.14, the one interpreter both lanes carry: the host has no `bash` and the WSL guest no `pwsh`, so three languages could not be reduced any other way. The shell prelude's two load-bearing tricks survive the move and improve: the OCaml stack the Sail emission needs is `resource.setrlimit` in the parent rather than `ulimit -s`, and a stage's cost is `os.wait4` on the child that was asked about rather than `/usr/bin/time` reporting the running maximum over every child so far.
  * Shared parses move into `tools/vos/` and the checker's rules into one module per group, so no fact is parsed twice; the entry points collapse from sixteen to five, the four model loops and the trace rig becoming subcommands of [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py). [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md) states the lane rule, the conventions, and the three edits a new rule costs.
  * Exit evidence: the checker's output is unchanged line for line except where it now names the package rather than a file, and it runs in 0.95 s against 2.2 s; the mutation selftest kills all 50 mutants in 19 s against 129 s; `model.py build` is green at 150/150 with the same `ALL_DONE` marker; `sweep` returns the same 146/52/1 of 199; `trace-diff --corpus` returns the same 54 prefixes at 97 in 2.2 s against 7.1 s; and the proof gate returns the same 19 constants closed.
* ~~**I1 · Move sources to WSL ext4 and uncap local parallelism**~~ · struck, and the reason is that the item is not closeable where it stands. Two of its four clauses closed, one was refused on its own measurement, and the two left standing are settings on the human's box rather than in this tree, so no benchmark reaches them. **S9 was its disposition and is no longer**: that item is a provisioner rather than a move, so the lane stays on WSL by choice and these two clauses stay open with it, an accepted cost rather than an owed act. A dedicated host would close all four, and is a choice available per run rather than a scheduled migration. The measurement below stands as the record of what the layout costs.
  * The item's four clauses do not resolve alike and two are closed. `ctest -j$(nproc)` is done, the build setting `test_jobs=cpus`. Moving the checkout to `~/src/VerifiedOS` is refused on the measurement below. The two left standing are Remote-WSL and cache-hostile WSL memory reclamation, and they share one shape: each is a setting on the human's box rather than in this tree, the second reachable only through `[wsl2] autoMemoryReclaim` in `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig`, global to every distribution and permanent until a human deletes it, the same boundary [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) draws around the idle timer beside it. The item is open on a posture call rather than a measurement, and no benchmark closes it.
  * **The rejection under the refused half is overturned, and the move that clears the way for does not pay.** The `/mnt/c` rejection [vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) carried measured *reading* 1.19 MB of Sail sources across 125 files, where the stage that pays is `configure`, which walks the whole 824-file cmake project and pays per stat rather than per byte. On a quiet toolchain with the arms alternated, a re-configure is **17.0, 17.4 and 20.4 s at 10% to 12% of a core** from `/mnt/c` against **0.9, 1.0 and 1.1 s at 47% to 48%** from a byte-identical ext4 copy, and a fresh configure is **26.7, 29.5 and 34.9 s** against **11.3, 7.3 and 7.6 s**. CPU time is 1.7 to 2.4 s against 0.4 to 0.5 s, so the gap is wait rather than work. The suspect is real and worth about 17 s of every build.
  * **What the same measurement then removes is the move.** The tax is symmetric and the host lane pays it in reverse: `tools/check.py` costs **1.0, 1.1 and 1.6 s** over the NTFS checkout against **11.4, 13.5 and 18.5 s** over the same tree on the `wsl.localhost` share, and that is the loop run after every document edit. Host git refuses such a checkout until `safe.directory` is relaxed. So relocating the checkout alone would buy 17 s of a build and spend more than that on every checker run; what would pay is moving the *working posture* into the guest, which is this entry's Remote-WSL clause and a workflow call rather than a benchmark.
* [x] **I2 · Use one shared content-keyed SMT memo cache** · 0.9 h actual · 0.1%
  * **Declined, and on the pinned compiler's cache format rather than on a benchmark.** Sail's memo cache is one flat file of fixed records, a 16-byte digest of the SMT query and one byte of verdict, read whole into a map at startup by `load_digests` and rewritten whole from that map at exit by `save_digests` (libsail 0.20.2, `constraint.ml`). There is no lock, no atomic rename, and `open_out_bin` truncates in place. A build tree's cache measures 1,418,412 bytes, which is 83,436 records with no remainder, over verdicts 6,612 unknown, 31,725 sat and 45,099 unsat. Benchmarking is the wrong instrument here: what a shared path does is not slower, it is unsound, and a measurement that failed to trigger it would say nothing.
  * Three consequences, each alone enough. Concurrent runs do not **merge**: each writes back what it loaded plus what it learned, so the later writer's file lacks everything the earlier learned during the overlap, which is worst exactly when the cache is cold and the learning largest, the case sharing was proposed to fix. A reader arriving mid-rewrite sees a prefix, or one byte outside the verdict range and takes Sail's invalid path, which empties both maps and **replaces the shared file with only that run's entries**: measured deterministically, one such byte destroyed **77,260 of 83,436 records**, Sail exiting 0 having printed `SMT cache file 'sail_smt_cache' is invalid` into a build log nothing reads for it. And two writers interleave at whatever offset each buffer flushed; records are 17 bytes and OCaml flushes at 65,536, which is 1 modulo 17, so the sixteenth boundary falls on a verdict byte and leaves one run's digest carrying another run's answer to a different obligation. That last is a false answer to a typechecking obligation in a build whose output is a verification oracle, the trade I4 refuses on `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS`.
  * **What was done instead is copy-on-create, which buys the same cold start with one writer per file.** A lane standing up copies a warm cache from the primary worktree's tree rather than pointing at it, so the ~25 min a cold cache costs is paid once on this machine and not per lane, and a stale copy costs misses and nothing worse. That landed with I7, and the standing boundary is unchanged: content-keying is by obligation and not by prover, so no copy crosses a solver version or a distribution.
  * Finding, closed by I7 rather than by this item: the shared cache I2 proposed already existed for the typecheck loop, `/root/build/verifiedos-typecheck-smt-cache` being one file every checkout wrote, and it was live across four concurrent lanes. It is per lane now.
* [x] **I3 · Benchmark a current z3 binary on a cold emission** · 0.8 h actual · 0.1%
  * **The cold emission separates the solvers where the typecheck could not, and it separates them in the pin's favor.** Two alternated repeats per arm in a disposable lane, cold each time by deleting the memo cache and dirtying the emission edge: the pinned z3 **5.1.0** emits cold in 216.3 and 212.9 s against the distribution **4.13.3**'s 274.7 and 286.2, about 214.6 against 280.5 at the midpoints, **+31% for the distribution's solver**, every distro arm auditable by the tool's own WARNING line in its captured log. The open cell's reading, that the workload does not separate them and the pin stands on reproducibility rather than speed, was true of the typecheck it measured (cold 46.9/59.3 s pin against 44.5/48.2 distro, warm 5.5/5.8 against 5.7/8.0, spreads wider than gaps) and is false of the emission: the pin now stands on both grounds.
  * **The ~25 min cold figure the tools quoted was roughly 7× stale, and the staleness is the curation's.** A cold emission today is ~3.6 min pinned and ~4.7 distro against a warm 36 to 39 s in the same lane, so the once-measured figure described a model whose surface the batches since deleted. [tools/run.py model](../tools/vos/cli/model.py)'s seeding rationale now carries the measured pair; the seeding keeps paying about three minutes per lane standup, just no longer catastrophically.
  * Three observations ride the numbers. **The two solvers do measurably different work for byte-identical output**: 4.13.3 writes 23,306 memo records where 5.1.0 writes 17,558 and peaks ~117 MB higher, while the emitted C++'s md5 is unchanged across all four cold runs and the warm anchors, so the difference is solver-side effort and never artifact. **Per-arm determinism is exact**, both repeats of each arm writing byte-identical cache sizes. And **one emit regenerates about a fifth of the seeded cache**, 17,558 records against the seeded 84,849, so the cache a lane copies accumulates more than the emission's own obligations and a lane is returned to warm by a restore, never by a re-run.
  * Exit evidence: quiet-box gate empty; four cold runs and three warm anchors all exit 0, logs under /root/logs; the seeded cache saved before the first deletion and restored byte-exactly at 1,442,433 bytes, a 35.7 s warm emit confirming the lane; no cache crossed a solver boundary.

  * The ccache half is the half that is a choice rather than a measurement: the binary is installed and [tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) puts the two compiler launchers on every configure when it finds one, against a `CCACHE_DIR` shared by every build tree, with no `CCACHE_SLOPPINESS` set on purpose, a false hit in a build whose output is a verification oracle not being worth the hit rate.
