It does not, and the ccache half is why: a `touch` on one model source re-emits C++ whose md5 is the one it replaced, and the compile and link that follow cost **4.5 s over 23 edges**, the 10.5 MB unit among them a direct hit. Ninja's floor with nothing touched is 2.9 s of that, so emitting-only-on-change would save **1.6 s** against a 41.2 s emission, under 4% of the cycle and only where a model edit leaves the generated C++ identical, against a permanent divergence in a vendored upstream `CMakeLists.txt` that every reconciliation would carry. The emission's own 41.2 s is not addressable this way at all: what the output will be is not knowable without producing it.

[tools/vos/env.py](../tools/vos/env.py) now records what the cache does and does not reach.

The generated unit compiled alone at `-O2 -g` is 139.1 s wall and 115 s CPU with a 1.02 GB peak, and that wall figure is an upper bound rather than a clean one: a sibling lane was building and the compile held 83% of a core.

* [x] **I7 · Run canonical builds in the background and standardize worktree lanes** · 1.4 h actual · 0.1%
  * One WSL toolchain serves as many checkouts as there are git worktrees, and they shared one build tree between them. Each checkout now carries a **lane**, derived from the checkout rather than declared: a linked worktree's `.git` is a file naming its administrative directory under the primary checkout's `.git/worktrees/`, so the lane is git's own name for the worktree and is unique within the repository by construction. The primary worktree has no lane and keeps every path it had; a linked one builds under `/root/build/lane-<name>/`, one directory holding that lane's build tree, its iterate tree, and its SMT cache, so a lane is retired by deleting it. The M0.4 oracle's tree is the one every lane still shares, because it is stock upstream at a pinned commit and no curation here reaches it.
  * **Three things collided and only one of them was loud.** Measured while four lanes ran against this toolchain: cmake refuses outright to aim an existing cache at a second source tree, so `/root/logs/model-build.log` carries `CONFIGURE_EXIT=1` and `The source ".../VerifiedOS-m08c/model/CMakeLists.txt" does not match the source ".../VerifiedOS/model/CMakeLists.txt"`; that refused run had already opened the shared log with `w`, truncating the primary lane's from 10,667 bytes to 444, so a build that never started destroyed the record of the last green one. The third is silent and is the one lanes exist for: `sweep`, `corpus`, `trace-diff`, `devicetree` and `reference` all read the simulator back out of the build tree and none of them can tell whose model generated it, so a lane building after another lane reports on the other lane's machine.
  * A build **holds its lane** for as long as it runs, and a second one over a live one is refused by name rather than merged into it. The lock is `flock` and not a pidfile, so a killed build leaves nothing to break and there is no liveness test to get wrong; `build --background` hands the lock to the run it detaches by inheritance rather than dropping it, and `wait` blocks on that lock in the kernel before reading the log for the verdict. Waiting on the lock rather than on `ALL_DONE` is what ends a wait on a build that died without writing one.
  * **What a lane costs is a copy and not a cold build**, which is what makes one worth having and is why [I2](#build-loop-instruments) declines to share instead. A lane standing up copies the warm SMT memo cache and the downloaded riscv-tests from the primary worktree's tree, so the ~25 min a cold cache costs is paid once on this machine rather than once per lane: measured, a lane from nothing is green in 237.7 s. A build tree is 362 MB, so four lanes are about 1.5 GB against 947 GB free, and disk is not the constraint. What a lane genuinely does pay is the compiles, because no ccache entry crosses a worktree: that is I4's measured finding, 367 misses and no hits on a first build.
  * **Defect found by standardizing the lane, and fixed here: `git describe` cannot run in one at all.** A linked worktree made by the host's git writes a *Windows* path into its `.git` file, which inside the guest is not absolute, so git appends it to the worktree and exits 128 with `not a git repository`. cmake's `git describe` is one of those runs, so every lane configured with `Failed to run git describe: 128` and stamped its emulator `unknown commit`, silently restoring the state M0.10 exists to end; `model.py reference` caught it at exit 1. The configure now carries a `GIT_DIR` translated by `wslpath`, scoped to that one child so it cannot follow a later `git` into the oracle's tree; the lane's emulator reports `636aa07-dirty` and `reference` exits 0.
  * Finding, reported rather than closed: the sibling lanes had already worked the collision around by hand with a second build tree named for one worktree. That workaround is per-agent and undeclared, and reaches neither the log nor the typecheck cache, which every lane still shared as one file; the derivation reaches all three.
  * **Finding, closed here: two ways of standing a lane up reached no seeder, and the second was made by the fix above.** `emit` configures a tree that does not exist yet, and it is the loop the memo cache is worth most to, being exactly the emission [I3](#build-loop-instruments) prices cold at ~3.6 min against ~36 s warm; a lane opened with `emit` rather than `build` paid that. And the typecheck cache sits beside the build trees rather than inside one, so the seeder, which copies into a tree, never reached it: giving each lane a file of its own closed the sharing and left the copy owed, I2 declining to share a cache rather than to warm one. Both seed through one `_seed_cache_file` now, which keeps a target that already exists and copies nothing when the donor is the target, so the primary worktree is not a case any caller has to know about.
  * Exit evidence: `model.py lane` on the primary checkout prints the four paths it always used, unchanged. A lane standing up from nothing is green at 375 of 375 edges in 237.7 s, configure 29.6 s, build 172.9 s at 715% CPU, ctest 35.2 s, seeded from the primary tree rather than cold. A second `model.py build` against a live one exits 1 naming the holder's pid and writes nothing. `wait` returns the finished log's verdict at exit 0, and against a stand-in holder it returned 6.0 s after that holder exited, with no interval anywhere in it. In the lane, `corpus` is 21 of 21 with 193 checks over 5,231 records, `devicetree` compiles with no warning at 4,466 bytes inside its region, and `reference` names the model at `636aa07-dirty` over 62 properties. Every later figure in this section was taken in that lane, and the ones marked contended were taken with a sibling lane building.

K-81 holds the two together in both directions, a gitlink with no row and a row naming no gitlink alike, and it reads the commit out of the **git index** rather than out of a `git submodule status`, which is what makes it decide the same thing on the unpopulated checkout most checkouts here are.

A site is found by the shape a restatement takes, an abbreviated object id on a line that has already named the upstream, over the **whole index** rather than a list of places to look, so a site nobody registered is read on the day it is written. The one part of the index left unread is `model/` outside the ported files, vendored upstream at its own revisions, where a recorded commit is somebody else's provenance rather than a pin taken here.

A rewrite of the commit alone leaves each describing work done at a commit it no longer names, which is the half-a-sentence hazard that keeps K-70 report-only and is worse here, recording a licence reading nobody took under a flag run to repair arithmetic. Two further grounds would each suffice: the ported headers sit under the `-text` tree, and the oracle tree's commit is a directory's name rather than a sentence's figure. **A pin whose own row is a finding has its sites left alone by both hops**, so one gitlink advancing is one finding at the one place a person has to act.

Attribution by nearness across lines is worse in that document's own tables, which put one upstream's row four lines above another's cell; membership against the whole pin set is worse again, this plan recording many commits of other trees.
  * **Four commits are declared rather than filtered out.** Each is written beside an upstream's name and is not that upstream's pin: the commit the curated model was vendored from, the older `sail-riscv` the CHERI oracle's tree embeds, the tag object a submodule's tag points at, and the numeral of a Zenodo DOI. Naming each asks for a decision the next time one is written, where a shape rule tight enough to exclude them would quietly grow to admit the next one. **The vendored model's baseline stays unheld on purpose**: no gitlink owns it, a vendored tree having none, which is the shape K-71 already declines for the corpus manifest's edition.

It is a computed quantity and a claim row now, rewritten by `--fix` like every other. **The corpus's size as this plan's completion notes state it is deliberately not registered beside it**: those record what an edition measured at its gate, and holding a live manifest against a frozen measurement would be red the day the corpus advances as designed.

That cost nothing while no rule read one; a rule that does would have reported every pin as owned by nothing and failed the baseline, which reports as a red tree rather than as a bad mutant. The template now puts the repository's own gitlinks back into its index after the add, on every build rather than only a fresh one, because a carried index is the older of the two.

  * **Seventeen executables under [tools/](../tools/) become one.** [tools/run.py](../tools/run.py) reads the command table in [vos/cli/](../tools/vos/cli/), and each of the old entry points is now a module of that package: it keeps its docstring, its argparse and its `main(argv)`, and loses only its own preamble and `__main__` block. I0 collapsed sixteen entry points to five by making the model loops subcommands; this is that act finished, and the residue it removes is the one a caller pays: knowing which file answers which question.
  * **The lane stops being the caller's to remember.** The document gates run on the host and the model, RTL, generator and prover loops run inside WSL, and a guest command asked for on the host is now re-launched there by `run.py` rather than refused, so there is no `wsl -u root -e python3` to spell. The subcommands that read this checkout rather than drive a toolchain answer on either lane and are named in the table: `model config-keys`, `rtl provenance`, `oracle list`, `seed list`, `testrig protocol`.
  * **[tools/check.py](../tools/check.py) is the one path that does not move, and the ground is the register's rather than convenience.** The register, [the coverage matrix](coverage-matrix.md), [the crown jewels](crown-jewels.md), [the field bindings](field-bindings.md) and [the findings register](findings-register.md) all cite that path for what it decides, so moving it would put a path the tree does not carry into five documents, three of them normative, and dirty the co-read pairs of every entry that names it to say nothing new.
  * **`run.py evidence` is the composite the exit-evidence discipline was paying for by hand.** Every landed item quotes the same measurements, and taking them was six commands into the guest and then a transcription of six figures into a note, which is six chances to quote a figure from a run that is not the one being reported. It runs six members in order, the build and its bundled suite, the reference, the profile sweep, the differential corpus, the devicetree and the proof gate, and ends in the block a note quotes, the `$[test]` property harness being one of the block's figures rather than a member of its own, read back out of what each member printed rather than re-derived; a member that did not report a figure says so and nothing invents one. `--no-build` measures the tree as it stands.
  * **The subcommand dispatch is written once.** Four commands carried the same twenty lines, a parser built from the module docstring, one subparser per row of a `COMMANDS` table, that subcommand's own flags, and the handler the table names; that is the two-copies-of-one-parse defect these tools exist to catch, sitting in the tools. It is `cli.dispatch` now, with each module keeping only what differs, and `cli.Table` replaces four identical handler-type aliases.
  * **The quarantine keeps its own gate and is not a command here**, which is what K-83 makes it: a `run.py` row for it would be exactly the coupling that rule holds the landing loop out of.
  * Net change: 1,109 lines added and 906 removed across 54 files, fifteen of them renamed and three new.
  * Exit evidence: the host gate wave green over all four members with `--tests`; the checker green with every mutant killed and the registry covered; the tools' own suite green over 33 modules; both type checkers clean at ty 0.0.74 and ruff 0.16.4; `oracle list`, `seed list`, `rtl provenance` and the guest hop each answered through the entry point; and `run.py evidence --no-build` green in the guest over five members at **96 properties, 199 refusals of 199, the corpus at 26 of 26 over 245 checks and 8,830 records, the devicetree at 5,717 bytes and the proof gate closing 180 constants**. No register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage-matrix edit, so the review gate is not re-entered and the co-read ledger is unmoved.
  * Four findings.
    * **One module read `sys.argv` directly and would have raised the moment a dispatcher called it.** `rtl.main` took no argv at all, being the one entry point that parsed the process's own arguments rather than a list handed to it, so it typechecked green under a cast and failed only when called. It takes argv like every other command now, and keeps the marker it writes around the dispatch rather than pushing that into the shared helper.
    * **A selftest case anchored on a construct the refactor deleted, and the case said so rather than passing.** K-83's path mutant seeded the `sys.path` preamble in a tool that is now a package module with no preamble, so the mutation would not apply and the run reported it as unseeded. It is re-seeded at the one preamble the move leaves standing, which is the entry point's own; a case that had been written to match loosely would have seeded something else and killed a mutant about nothing.
    * **The documents cite tool paths 180 times and only one of those paths is cited normatively**, which is the measurement that decided the exception above rather than a taste for keeping `check.py` where it was. The register names `tools/check.py` and no other tool, in six places; every other citation is in the plan, the tools' own README, the differential corpus, the RTL pages or this repository's licence pages, where a path is a pointer rather than a claim a review gate audits.
    * **The tool surface is obligation rather than fat, which is worth recording before the next simplification is proposed.** A census over the tracked Python finds no public function defined in one module and named nowhere else, and no module outside `tests/` that nothing imports: 33,108 lines with no dead code in it. What makes the directory large is the number of cross-artifact facts somebody decided to hold, so the lever that shrinks it is S15's, generating what is transcribed, and not a search for unused code.
  * Landed: Tier A. No rule holds the one-entry-point fact, so the mechanical conditions Tier B is admitted on are not met, and saying so is cheaper than inventing a rule to hold a directory listing.

**Instrument subtotal:** 24.7 h · 2% · 24.7 h complete.

## Estimate and schedule basis

* Completed estimates are actual elapsed session intervals with overlapping build waits apportioned approximately.
* Open work classes:
  * A · pinning, configuration, and mechanical curation
  * B · semantic porting and tool re-homing with differential tests
  * C · fresh systems authoring with functional tests
  * D · RTL and FPGA work
* Confidence: every open item's range spans roughly a factor of two about its midpoint, and a class believed softer is priced by widening its own items' ranges rather than by a second figure stated over the total.
* Grand total: the sum of the item cells, 1,087.5 h midpoint over a 821.5–1,353.5 h range.
* **The authority class is the calibration, and it is measured rather than assumed.** Taking each completed item's earliest recorded estimate from `git log` against its recorded actual, across the thirty items carrying both, gives 107.8 h actual against 161.5 h estimated, a ratio of 0.67. The error is not uniform and in one class it runs the other way: eighteen items whose authority is in this repository ran at **0.66**, ten that read, pin, install or measure an external thing ran at **0.60**, and the two that authored against an external authority ran at **1.68**. Neither overrun is random. M0.8d authored FEC decoders and Keccak against FIPS 202, the ACVP vectors and TS 38.212, and its own finding is that the two defects the known-answer vectors found were both transcriptions; M0.12 authored a corpus, an assembler, a dialect table and an ELF composer from nothing, because no toolchain has one. The deepest under-runs are equally consistent, M0.6g at 0.27 adding six profile rows the register already enumerated and M1.1 at 0.25 reading a licence and pinning a commit.
* **The ratio is a prior on range width and not a re-price of midpoints**, which is what the conventions above already require of a class-X cell, and which is what the calibrated figure states separately rather than folding in. Its own weakness is stated: n = 2 in the pool that matters. It is every completed item that authored against an external authority, both overran, both for the same reason, and the consequence is asymmetric, so it is treated as a widening rather than a multiplier. **Turning the prior into a measurement is owned by no item.**
* **The strategy the classes imply is one sentence: move work across the authority line, and generate what is currently authored.** What has not moved is the class-X residue before the M8a gate, which is twelve open cells, six of them M1.2's children, and which the gate figures above put at more than half that gate's hours.
* At 10–20 attended hours per week, the M8a gate is set by the critical chain rather than by the bill, and the chain is lane 1's to release. Review capacity was the constraint beyond three lanes; the two-tier landing rule and the unattended runner are what move it, so lane count is bounded by the register acts that gate a lane rather than by attention at the end of one.
